Spaces of distributions with Sobolev wave front in a fixed conic set: compactness, pullback by smooth maps and the compensated compactness theorem Thanks: The work of S. Pilipović was partially supported by the Serbian Academy of Sciences and Arts, project, F10. Thanks: The work of B. Prangoski was partially supported by the bilateral project “Microlocal analysis and applications” funded by the Macedonian Academy of Sciences and Arts and the Serbian Academy of Sciences and Arts.
Stevan Pilipović, Bojan Prangoski

Abstract
. We consider the space 𝒟 L ′ r ​ ( M , E ) \mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E) of distributional sections of the smooth complex vector bundle E → M E\rightarrow M whose Sobolev wave front set of order r ∈ ℝ r\in\mathbb{R} lies in the closed conic subset L L of T ∗ ​ M \ 0 T^{*}M\backslash 0 . We introduce a locally convex topology on it to study the continuity of the pullback by smooth maps and generalise the result of Hörmander about the pullback on the space of distributions with 𝒞 ∞ \mathcal{C}^{\infty} wave front set in L L . We employ an idea of Gérard [ 18 ] to extend the Kolmogorov-Riesz compactness theorem to 𝒟 L ′ r ​ ( M , E ) \mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E) and we characterise its relatively compact subsets. We study the continuity properties of pseudo-differential operators when acting on 𝒟 L ′ r ​ ( M , E ) \mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E) , r ∈ ℝ r\in\mathbb{R} , and we generalise the Rellich’s lemma. As an application of our results, we extend the microlocal defect measures of Gérard and Tartar to sequences in 𝒟 L ′ 0 ​ ( M , E ) \mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E) and we show a microlocal variant of the compensated compactness theorem.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
46E35, 58J40

1.
Introduction

The Sobolev wave front set was first introduced and employed by Duistermaat and Hörmander in
[
14
]
in their analysis of propagation of singularities of solutions of PDEs. Together with the
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
wave front set previously introduced by Hörmander, it became an indispensable tool for classifying the singularities of solutions of PDEs; see
[
22
,
31
,
55
,
56
]
and the references therein for various generalisations and their applications. In
[
28
, Section 8.2]
(see also
[
26
, Subsection 2.5]
) Hörmander introduced a notion of convergence of sequences in the space
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)
consisting of all distributions on
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
whose
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
wave front set is contained in the closed conic subset
L
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
and then showed that the pullback of smooth functions by a smooth map
f:O\rightarrow U
can be extended to a sequentially continuous map
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{f^{*}L}(O)
whenever
f^{*}L
(the pullback of
L
) does not intersect the set of normals
\mathcal{N}_{f}
of
f
. If one writes the natural locally convex topology on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)
that induces this convergence of sequences (see
[
13
, Chapter 1]
), it turns out that the pullback is not continuous: see
[
5
]
for a counterexample. Recently, in
[
5
,
8
]
, the authors modified the topology so that the pullback by a smooth map becomes continuous (and not just sequentially continuous). Furthermore, they conducted an extensive analysis of the topological properties of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)
and its dual. We also point out two arXiv preprints
[
6
,
7
]
where the author investigates the topological properties of spaces of distributions whose Sobolev wave front sets of all orders are included in a fix conic set and the article
[
9
]
where the authors study the Besov wave front set. An important problem to consider is what happens with the pullback if one considers it as a map on the space
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
consisting of distributions that have Sobolev wave front set of order
r\in\mathbb{R}
in
L
. Notice that this is a refinement of the result on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)
because of the well-known algebraic identity
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)=\bigcap_{r\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
. One expects that there will be a loss of regularity since this happens in the simplest examples when considering restrictions of
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}
(the local Sobolev space of order
r\in\mathbb{R}
) to lower dimensional hyperplanes; see
[
29
, Appendix B]
. This is one of our primary goals in the article. In fact, we will introduce a locally convex topology on the spaces
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
,
r\in\mathbb{R}
, with
M
a smooth manifold and
L
a closed conic subset of the cotangent bundle without the zero section
T^{*}M\backslash 0
, and show the following:

(i)

The pullback
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(N)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(M)
, with
f:M\rightarrow N
smooth and satisfying
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
, is well-defined and continuous for appropriately chosen
r_{2}
and
r_{1}
. When
f
has constant rank, we show that the conditions on
r_{2}
and
r_{1}
are essentially optimal.

(ii)

The algebraic identity
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M)=\bigcap_{r\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
becomes topological when we interpret the right hand side as the projective limit. The algebraic identities
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\emptyset}(M)=H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{T^{*}M\backslash 0}(M)=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
also become topological.

(iii)

Given a properly supported pseudo-differential operator
A
of order
r_{0}
, the linear map
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M)
, which is well-defined by the results of Duistermaat and Hörmander
[
14
]
, becomes continuous. When
A
is elliptic (or better yet, non-characteristic in
(T^{*}M\backslash 0)\backslash L
), we show a priori estimates for
A
which reduce to the well-known a priori estimates for elliptic
A:H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M)\rightarrow H^{r-r_{0}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M)
.

Inspired by ideas from
[
21
, Chapter 7]
and
[
8
]
, we also identify the strong dual of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
: it consists of all compactly supported Sobolev distributions of order
-r
(technically speaking, they are distribution densities) that have
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
wave front set included in
\{(p,\xi)\in T^{*}M\backslash 0\,|\,(p,-\xi)\not\in L\}
. As a consequence of
(iii)
, we also show continuity properties of pseudo-differential operators when acting on these spaces.

In
[
18
]
, Gérard introduced a wave front set associated with a sequence in
L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}
which contains the directions in the cotangent bundle where the sequence does not behave like a relatively compact subset of
L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}
(see
[
11
]
for a related concept). The idea in
[
18
]
is to define the complement as the directions where the sequence satisfies the Kolmogorov-Riesz compactness criterium
[
23
]
for
L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}
. In the context of the topology we introduce on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
, we realised that this wave front set does something more. We define it for any bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
and, instead of the compactness criterium for
L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}
, we employ the
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}
compactness criterium. Then we show that:

(iv)

This wave front set with index
r
of a bounded set
B
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
is the closed conic subset
L^{\prime}
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
such that
B
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L^{\prime}}(M)
. In effect, it gives a convenient and computational characterisation of the relatively compact subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
.

This generalises the Kolmogorov-Riesz compactness theorem to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
and it allows us to show:

(v)

A generalisation of the Rellich lemma for the spaces
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
,
r\in\mathbb{R}
.

We point out that we show these results on general smooth complex vector bundles over manifolds.

Our results give a robust parallel between
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
and
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M)
when it comes down to the characterisation of the compact sets and the continuity properties of pseudo-differential operators so one can show microlocal variants of various results by employing similar convergence and continuity arguments as in the local Sobolev case. Important such examples are some of the weak convergence methods employed in the theory of nonlinear PDEs
[
16
]

1
1

1

For the reader familiar with the theory, we point out that one can always extract convergent subsequence of a relatively compact sequence in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
since we show that the bounded subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
are metrisable although
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
in general is not.
. We showcase the theory we developed by employing it to improve two closely related such results: the microlocal defect measures and the compensated compactness theorem.

The microlocal defect measures were independently developed by Gérard
[
18
,
19
]
and Tartar
[
53
]
who introduced them under the name H-measures. Nowadays they are indispensable tool in the study of linear and nonlinear PDEs; see for example
[
2
,
10
,
12
,
17
,
20
,
46
,
48
]
and the references therein. We also refer to the works of Lions
[
39
,
40
,
41
,
42
]
where he introduced the related concentration-compactness method for solving minimisation problems in the calculus of variations. Broadly speaking, for a bounded sequence
(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
in
L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)
which converges to
u\in L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
, the microlocal defect measure associated to
(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a positive Radon measure on
U\times\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
whose support contains the directions where
\{u_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
does not behave like a relatively compact set in
L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)
. Our results naturally lend themselves for generalising this to the case when one only knows that
u,u_{k}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M)
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and
\{u_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M)
. In this case, the microlocal defect measure is only defined in the directions outside of
L
and we show that
u_{k_{j}}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L^{\prime}}(M)
where
L^{\prime}
is the union of
L
and the directions in the support of the microlocal defect measure and
(u_{k_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a subsequence of
(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
.

The second place where we are going to apply our results is in showing a microlocal extension of the compensated compactness theorem. The theorem was first proved by Murat
[
44
,
45
]
and Tartar
[
52
]
(see also
[
24
,
25
]
) and letter improved by Gérard
[
19
]
. Without going into details, the theorem states the following. Let
u,u_{k}\in L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, be as above but with values in
\mathbb{C}^{N}
and let
A
and
Q
be properly supported polyhomogeneous matrix valued
\Psi
DOs of orders
r
and
0
and principal symbols
a
and
q
respectively. If
\{Au_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is relatively compact in
H^{-r}_{\operatorname{loc}}
and
q
satisfies a weak Legendre-Hadamard condition
q(x,\xi)z\cdot\overline{z}=0
for those
(x,\xi)
and
z\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
which satisfy
a(x,\xi)z=0
, then
Qu_{k}\cdot\overline{u}_{k}\rightarrow Qu\cdot\overline{u}
as distributions. As before, our generalisation is that one can take
u
and
u_{k}
to be in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}
. We point out that this is not a trivial generalisation since even the products
Qu_{k}\cdot\overline{u}_{k}
and
Qu\cdot\overline{u}
are meaningless a priori; moreover, one can not employ Hörmander’s definition of products of distributions to defined them because we only have information on the Sobolev wave front sets of
u
and
u_{k}
. However, as a byproduct of our results, we show that the sesquilinear map
(v_{1},v_{2})\mapsto Qv_{1}\cdot\overline{v}_{2}
on smooth functions uniquely extends to a hypocontinuous sesquilinear map on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}
if
Q
is of order
-\infty
at every point of
L
and then we show that
Qu_{k}\cdot\overline{u}_{k}\rightarrow Qu\cdot\overline{u}
as distributions. We prove the compensated compactness theorem as well as our generalisation of the microlocal defect measures on general smooth complex vector bundles. We point out that there are variants of the compensated compactness theorem in the
L^{p}
setting
[
3
,
32
,
47
]
, but we leave their microlocalisations for future research.

The paper is organised as follows. We collect in Section
2
the notations and necessary facts we are going to employ throughout the rest of the article. In Section
3
, we introduce the topology on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}
and show several key results in open sets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Sections
4
and
5
contain our main results on the spaces
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}
,
r\in\mathbb{R}
, on general smooth complex vector bundles as well as the continuity properties of pseudo-differential operators when acting on these spaces. Section
6
is devoted to the applications. Finally, in Appendix
A
we show the optimality of the loss in regularity in our theorem on the pullback by smooth maps.

2.
Preliminaries

We fix the constants in the Fourier transform as
\mathcal{F}f(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}e^{-ix\xi}f(x)dx
,
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Throughout the article,
B(x,r)
will stand for the open ball with centre at
x
and radius
r>0
. As standard,
\langle x\rangle:=(1+|x|^{2})^{1/2}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any open set
O
in a topological space,
K\subset\subset O
means that
K
is compact and
K\subseteq O
. If
B
is any subset of a topological space, we denote by
\mathbf{1}_{B}
the indicator function of
B
, i.e.
\mathbf{1}_{B}(x)=1
if
x\in B
and
\mathbf{1}_{B}(x)=0
when
x\not\in B
. For a
\mathcal{C}^{1}
map
f:O\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
, with
O
an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, we denote by
f^{\prime}(x)
the derivative of
f
at
x\in O
and, when
n=m
, we set
|f^{\prime}|(x):=|\det(f^{\prime}(x))|
,
x\in O
. As standard, we denote
\mathbb{R}_{+}:=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,|\,t>0\}
and, for
B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we set
\mathbb{R}_{+}B:=\{tx\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,t>0,\,x\in B\}
; clearly
\mathbb{R}_{+}B
is a cone in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Given a smooth manifold
M
,
T^{*}M\backslash 0
stands for the cotangent bundle
T^{*}M
without the image of the zero section. A subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
is said to be conic if it satisfies
(p,\xi)\in L
,
\xi\in T^{*}_{p}M
, implies
(p,t\xi)\in L
, for all
t>0
. Given a conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
, we denote by
L^{c}
the complement of
L
in
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. When
M
is an open subset
U
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
we will always canonically identify
T^{*}U
with
U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
and consequently,
T^{*}U\backslash 0=U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
and, for a conic set
L
,
L^{c}=U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})\backslash L
. Furthermore, for a conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
(or of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
), we denote

\check{L}:=\{(p,\xi)\in T^{*}M\backslash 0\,|\,(p,-\xi)\in L\}
.

Let
U
be an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
wave front set of
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
(as standard, we will always abbreviate it as the wave front set) is the closed conic subset
WF(u)
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
defined as follows:
(x,\xi)\in U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
does not belong to
WF(u)
if there are an open cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
containing
\xi
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
satisfying
\varphi(x)\neq 0
such that
\mathfrak{q}_{\nu;\varphi,V}(u):=\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\nu}\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)\|_{L^{\infty}(V)}<\infty
,
\nu>0
; see
[
28
, Section 8.1]
. Given a closed conic subset
L
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
, set
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U):=\{u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\,|\,WF(u)\subseteq L\}
. Throughout the article
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)
will always carry the following locally convex topology introduced in
[
8
]
: the topology defined by all continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
together with all seminorms
\mathfrak{q}_{\nu;\varphi,V}
where
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and
V
is a closed cone in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V)\cap L=\emptyset
; we refer to
[
8
,
5
]
for the topological properties of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)
.

We denote by
H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
the standard Sobolev space of order
r\in\mathbb{R}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, i.e.
H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n}):=\{u\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\}
.

Given two locally convex spaces
X
and
Y
(from now, always abbreviated as l.c.s.) we denote by
\mathcal{L}(X,Y)
the space of continuous linear operators from
X
into
Y
and we denote by
\mathcal{L}_{b}(X,Y)
this space equipped with the strong operator topology. When
X=Y
, we simply write
\mathcal{L}(X)
and
\mathcal{L}_{b}(X)
. Of course,
X^{\prime}
is the dual of
X
and
X^{\prime}_{b}
stands for
X^{\prime}
equipped with the strong dual topology.

2.1.
Distributions on manifolds

For later use, we collect standard definitions and classical results about distributions on smooth manifolds; throughout the rest of the section, we employ the Einstein summation convention.

Let
M
be a smooth
m
-dimensional manifold
2
2

2

Manifolds are always assumed to be second-countable.
. The
\sigma
-algebra of Lebesgue measurable sets on
M
can be unambiguously defined by declaring a set
X
to be Lebesgue measurable if
x(X\cap O)
is Lebesgue measurable for each chart
(O,x)
; the notion of a negligible set (nullset) in
M
is unambiguous since they are diffeomorphism invariant.

Let
(E,\pi_{E},M)
be a smooth complex vector bundle of rank
k
; all the vector bundles throughout the article will be complex and smooth so we will never emphasise this - the only exception to this are the tangent and cotangent vector bundles
TM
and
T^{*}M
which are real and smooth. We denote by
E_{p}
the fiber over
p\in M
, i.e.
E_{p}:=\pi^{-1}_{E}(\{p\})
, and, for an open set
O
in
M
,
E_{O}
stands for the restriction of
E
to
O
. For
l\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, we denote by
\Gamma^{l}(E)
the Fréchet spaces of
l
-times continuously differentiable sections, while
\Gamma^{l}_{c}(E)
stands for the space of compactly supported
l
-times continuously differentiable sections equipped with its standard strict
(LF)
-space topology; when
l<\infty
,
\Gamma^{l}_{c}(E)
is in fact a strict
(LB)
-space. For
K\subset\subset M
and
l\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, we denote
\Gamma^{l}_{K}(E):=\{\varphi\in\Gamma^{l}(E)\,|\,\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subseteq K\}
and recall that it is a closed subspace of both
\Gamma^{l}(E)
and of
\Gamma^{l}_{c}(E)
and they induce the same Fréchet topology on it; when
l<\infty
,
\Gamma^{l}_{K}(E)
is in fact a Banach space. When
l=\infty
, we will simply denote these spaces by
\Gamma(E)
,
\Gamma_{c}(E)
and
\Gamma_{K}(E)
respectively. Given another vector bundle
(F,\pi_{F},M)
of rank
k_{1}
,
(L(E,F),\pi_{L(E,F)},M)
stands for the smooth complex vector bundle of rank
kk_{1}
whose fibres are
L(E,F)_{p}:=\mathcal{L}(E_{p},F_{p})
,
p\in M
. We denote by
E^{\prime}
the dual bundle to
E
, i.e.
E^{\prime}:=L(E,\mathbb{C}_{M})
where
\mathbb{C}_{M}:=M\times\mathbb{C}
is the trivial line bundle. As standard,
DM
stands for the complex
1
-density bundle over
M
with fibres
DT_{p}M
,
p\in M
. With
E
as before, we denote by
E^{\vee}
the functional dual bundle over
M
: it is the smooth complex vector bundle with total space
E^{\vee}:=L(E,DM)
. Notice that
(E^{\vee})^{\vee}
is canonically isomorphic with
E
. The space of distributional sections
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
of
E
is the strong dual of
\Gamma_{c}(E^{\vee})
and the space of distributions
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
on
M
is the strong dual of
\Gamma_{c}(DM)
. Similarly, the space of distributional sections with compact support
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(M;E)
is the strong dual of
\Gamma(E^{\vee})
and the space of distributions with compact support
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(M)
is the strong dual of
\Gamma(DM)
. When
E
is the trivial
k
-bundle
\mathbb{C}_{M}^{k}:=M\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
over
M
,
(\mathbb{C}^{k}_{M})^{\vee}
can be identified with the Whitney sum bundle
\underbrace{DM\oplus\ldots\oplus DM}_{k}
:

(\mathbb{C}_{M}^{k})^{\vee}_{p}\ni T\mapsto(p,T(1,0,\ldots,0),\ldots,T(0,\ldots,0,1))\in\{p\}\times\underbrace{DT_{p}M\oplus\ldots\oplus DT_{p}M}_{k}.

Employing this identification, we have

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;\mathbb{C}_{M})=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)\,\,\mbox{and, in general,}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;\mathbb{C}^{k}_{M})=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)^{k}=\underbrace{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)\times\ldots\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)}_{k};

analogously,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(M;\mathbb{C}_{M}^{k})=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(M)^{k}
. The continuous inclusion
L^{p}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
,
1\leq p\leq\infty
, is given by
f\mapsto\langle f,\cdot\rangle
, with
\langle f,\varphi\rangle:=\int_{M}[f,\varphi]
,
\varphi\in\Gamma_{c}(E^{\vee})
, where
[f,\varphi]
stands for the measurable section of
DM
given by
[f,\varphi]_{p}:=\varphi_{p}(f_{p})
, a.a.
p\in M
.

As standard, we denote by
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
and
H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)
the spaces of local and compact Sobolev sections of
E
of order
r
. We recall that
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
is a reflexive Fréchet space,
H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)
is a reflexive strict
(LB)
-space, the strong dual of
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
is
H^{-r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E^{\vee})
and the strong dual of
H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)
is
H^{-r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{\vee})
. For
K\subset\subset M
, we denote
H^{r}_{K}(M;E):=\{u\in H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)\,|\,\operatorname{supp}u\subseteq K\}
and we recall that
H^{r}_{K}(M;E)
is a closed subspace both of
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
and of
H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)
, both of them induce the same topology on
H^{r}_{K}(M;E)
and with this topology
H^{r}_{K}(M;E)
is a Banach space. Furthermore,
H^{0}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)=L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
and
H^{0}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)=L^{2}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)
.

If
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
and
(O,x)
is a chart on
M
then we define
u_{x}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x(O))
by

(2.1)

\langle u_{x},\phi\rangle:=\langle u,(\phi\circ x)\lambda^{x}\rangle,\quad\phi\in\mathcal{D}(x(O)),

where
\lambda^{x}
is the unique section of
DO
which satisfies
\lambda^{x}(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{1}},\ldots,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{m}})=1
; we will always denote this section by
\lambda^{x}
. Let
(O,x)
be a chart in
M
and
\Phi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
a local trivialisation of
E
over
O
. Denote by
\Psi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{DM}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}
the local trivialisation of
DM
over
O
given by
\Psi_{x}(z\lambda^{x}_{p})=(p,z)
,
p\in O
,
z\in\mathbb{C}
. Let
\Phi_{x,p}:E_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{k}
and
\Psi_{x,p}:DT_{p}M\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
p\in O
, be the induced isomorphisms. We define the frame
(\sigma^{1},\ldots,\sigma^{k})
for
E^{\vee}
over
O
by
(\sigma^{j})_{p}:=\Psi_{x,p}^{-1}\circ\epsilon^{j}\circ\Phi_{x,p}
,
p\in O
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, where
\epsilon^{j}:\mathbb{C}^{k}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is the map
\epsilon^{j}(z^{1},\ldots,z^{k})=z^{j}
; we call
(\sigma^{1},\ldots,\sigma^{k})
the induced frame by
\Phi_{x}
. With its help, for each
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
, we can define
u_{\Phi_{x}}^{j}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x(O))
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, by

(2.2)

\langle u_{\Phi_{x}}^{j},\phi\rangle:=\langle u,(\phi\circ x)\sigma^{j}\rangle,\quad\phi\in\mathcal{D}(x(O)).

Notice that
\langle u,\varphi\rangle=\langle u_{\Phi_{x}}^{j},\varphi_{j}\circ x^{-1}\rangle
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
,
\varphi\in\Gamma_{c}(E^{\vee}_{O})
,
3
3

3

(E^{\vee})_{O}=(E_{O})^{\vee}
and we denote it simply by
E^{\vee}_{O}
.
where
\varphi=\varphi_{j}\sigma^{j}
. If
(U,y)
is another chart on
M
that has non-empty intersection with
O
and over which
E
locally trivialises via
\Phi_{y}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(U)\rightarrow U\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
, then

(2.3)

u_{\Phi_{y}}^{j}=(\tau^{j}_{l}\circ y^{-1})(x\circ y^{-1})^{*}u_{\Phi_{x}}^{l}\quad\mbox{in}\quad\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(y(O\cap U)),

where
\tau=(\tau^{j}_{l})_{j,l}:O\cap U\rightarrow\operatorname{GL}(k,\mathbb{C})
is the transition map: it is the unique smooth map that satisfies
\Phi_{y}\circ\Phi_{x}^{-1}(p,z)=(p,\tau(p)z)
,
p\in O\cap U
,
z\in\mathbb{C}^{k}
. If
(\rho^{1},\ldots,\rho^{k})
is the frame for
E^{\vee}
over
U
induced by
\Phi_{y}
, it holds that

(2.4)

\rho^{j}=(|(y\circ x^{-1})^{\prime}|\circ x)\tau^{j}_{l}\sigma^{l}\quad\mbox{on}\quad O\cap U.

If
N
is another manifold and
f:N\rightarrow M
a smooth map,
f^{*}E
stands for the pullback bundle. It is a smooth complex vector bundle of rank
k
over
N
with total space
f^{*}E:=\bigcup_{p\in N}\{p\}\times E_{f(p)}
and equipped with the topology induced from
N\times E
. The fibre over
p
is
\{p\}\times E_{f(p)}
, the projection is
\pi_{f^{*}E}(p,e)=p
,
e\in E_{f(p)}
, and the local trivialisations are defined as follows. For each local trivialisation
\Phi:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
of
E
define a local trivialisation
f^{*}\Phi:\pi^{-1}_{f^{*}E}(f^{-1}(O))\rightarrow f^{-1}(O)\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
,
f^{*}\Phi(p,e)=(p,\Phi_{f(p)}e)
. The pullback map
f^{*}:\Gamma^{l}(E)\rightarrow\Gamma^{l}(f^{*}E)
,
f^{*}\varphi(p):=(p,\varphi\circ f(p))
,
p\in N
, is well-defined and continuous for all
l\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.

2.2.
Pseudo-differential operators

As standard, for
r\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq\delta\leq\rho\leq 1
and
\delta<1
, we denote by
S^{r}_{\rho,\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
the Hörmander class of global symbols on
\mathbb{R}^{2n}

[
27
]
: it is the Fréchet space of all
a\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
which satisfy
\sup_{x,\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\xi\rangle^{-r+\rho|\alpha|-\delta|\beta|}|\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}\partial^{\beta}_{x}a(x,\xi)|<\infty
,
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
. The pseudo-differential operator with symbol
a\in S^{r}_{\rho,\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
is defined by

(2.5)

\operatorname{Op}(a)\varphi(x):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}e^{ix\xi}a(x,\xi)\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi)d\xi,\quad\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

It is a continuous operator on
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and it extends to a continuous operator on
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Furthermore, when
r=0
,
\operatorname{Op}(a)
is continuous on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the mapping
S^{0}_{\rho,\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{b}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
,
a\mapsto\operatorname{Op}(a)
, is continuous
[
38
, Theorem 2.5.1, p. 110, and Theorem 2.3.18, p. 100]
. With only a few exceptions, we will mostly employ the case when
\rho=1
and
\delta=0
and we will always denote this space by
S^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
for short.

We collect the notations and facts we need from the local theory of pseudo-differential operators; we refer to
[
14
,
26
,
29
]
for the complete account. If
U
is an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the local symbol space
S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
is the Fréchet space of all
a\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
which satisfy

(2.6)

\sup_{x\in K,\,\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\xi\rangle^{-r+|\alpha|}|\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}\partial^{\beta}_{x}a(x,\xi)|<\infty,\quad\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\,K\subset\subset U

(we will only use the variant when
\rho=1
and
\delta=0
). Furthermore,
S^{-\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}):=\bigcap_{r\in\mathbb{R}}S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
equipped with its natural Fréchet space topology. More generally, if
V
is an open cone in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we denote by
S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times V)
the space of all
a\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U\times V)
which satisfy (
2.6
) but on
K\times V^{\prime}
for every
K\subset\subset U
and every cone
V^{\prime}\subseteq V
such that
V^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\subset\subset V
; when
V=\mathbb{R}^{n}
, this space coincides with the above definition of
S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
. As before, set
S^{-\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times V):=\bigcap_{r\in\mathbb{R}}S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times V)
. For
r\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
and
K\subset\subset U
, we denote
S^{r}_{K}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}):=\{a\in S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,\operatorname{supp}a\subseteq K\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\}
; it is a closed subspace of
S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
(and of
S^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
). Furthermore, we denote
S^{r}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}):=\bigcup_{K\subset\subset U}S^{r}_{K}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
(
S^{r}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
can be equipped with a natural
(LF)
-space topology but we will not need this fact); clearly
S^{r}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\subseteq S^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
. For
a\in S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
, the operator
\operatorname{Op}(a)
is defined as in (
2.5
) but with
U
in place of
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{x}
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
. Then
\operatorname{Op}(a):\mathcal{D}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
is well-defined and continuous and it extends to a well-defined and continuous mapping
\operatorname{Op}(a):\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Every continuous operator
T:\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
is called a regularising operator; in view of the Schwartz kernel theorem, these are the operators whose kernels are smooth on
U\times U
. The space of regularising operators on
U
is denoted by
\Psi^{-\infty}(U)
. A pseudo-differential operator of order
r\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
is an operator
A:\mathcal{D}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
of the form
A=\operatorname{Op}(a)+T
with
a\in S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
T\in\Psi^{-\infty}(U)
;
a
is called the symbol of
A
and is unique modulo
S^{-\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
. The kernel of any
\Psi
DO is always smooth outside of the diagonal. The space of pseudo-differential operators on
U
of order
r
is denoted by
\Psi^{r}(U)
. For
r\in\mathbb{R}
, the operator
A\in\Psi^{r}(U)
is said to be polyhomogeneous of order
r
if some (or, equivalently any) symbol
a
of
A
has an asymptotic expansion
a\sim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{j}
, where
a_{j}\in S^{r-j}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
j\in\mathbb{N}
, are positively homogeneous of degree
r-j
when
|\xi|>1
, i.e.
a_{j}(x,t\xi)=t^{r-j}a_{j}(x,\xi)
,
x\in U
,
|\xi|>1
,
t>1
. The space of polyhomogeneous
\Psi
DOs of order
r\in\mathbb{R}
on
U
is denote by
\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(U)
. The space of polyhomogeneous symbols of order
r
, i.e. the symbols that have asymptotic expansion as above, is denoted by
S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc},\operatorname{phg}}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold of dimension
m
. A continuous operator
A:\mathcal{D}(M)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
is said to be pseudo-differential operator of order
r\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
if its kernel is smooth outside of the diagonal in
M\times M
and for every
p\in M
there is a coordinate chart
(O,x)
containing
p
such that the operator
A_{x}:\mathcal{D}(x(O))\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(x(O))
,
A_{x}(\phi):=A(\phi\circ x)\circ x^{-1}
, belongs to
\Psi^{r}(x(O))
; when this is the case, one can show this holds for all charts on
M
and this definition of
\Psi
DO coincides with the one above when
M
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. The space of all
\Psi
DOs of order
r\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
on
M
is denoted by
\Psi^{r}(M)
. Finally, the space of symbols of order
r\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
on
M
is denoted by
S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(T^{*}M)
and it consists of all
a\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(T^{*}M)
such that for every chart
(O,x)
it holds that
(\kappa^{-1})^{*}a\in S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x(O)\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
where
\kappa
is the chart induced total local trivialisation of
T^{*}M
over
O
:

(2.7)

\kappa:\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow x(O)\times\mathbb{R}^{m},\quad\kappa(p,\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p}):=(x(p),\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m}).

When
M
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, this definition coincides with the one above.

Let
E
and
F
be two vector bundles over
M
of rank
k
and
k^{\prime}
respectively. A continuous operator
A:\Gamma_{c}(E)\rightarrow\Gamma(F)
is said to be pseudo-differential operator of order
r\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
if its kernel is smooth outside of the diagonal in
M\times M
and if for every
p\in M
there is a coordinate chart
(O,x)
containing
p
over which both
E
and
F
locally trivialise via
\Phi:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and
\Phi^{\prime}:\pi_{F}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k^{\prime}}
and such that the operators
A_{\Phi,\Phi^{\prime},j}^{l}:\mathcal{D}(O)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(O)
,
A_{\Phi,\Phi^{\prime},j}^{l}(\varphi):=s^{\prime l}(A(\varphi e_{j}))
,
j=1,\ldots,k
,
l=1,\ldots,k^{\prime}
, belong to
\Psi^{r}(O)
where
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{k})
is the local frame for
E
over
O
induced by
\Phi
and
(s^{\prime 1},\ldots,s^{\prime k^{\prime}})
is the local frame for the dual bundle
F^{\prime}
induced by
\Phi^{\prime}
. When this is the case, one can show this holds for all charts on
M
over which both
E
and
F
locally trivialise and for any local trivialisations of
E
and
F
. This definition of a
\Psi
DO coincides with the one above on manifolds when
E=F=\mathbb{C}_{M}
. The space of all pseudo-differential operators of order
r\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
between the bundles
E
and
F
is denoted by
\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
. Every
A\in\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
extends to a continuous operator
A:\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;F)
. When
A
is properly supported (i.e., both projections from the support of the kernel of
A
in
M\times M
to
M
are proper maps),
A:\Gamma_{c}(E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(F)
is well-defined and continuous and it extends to a well-defined and continuous operator
\Gamma(E)\rightarrow\Gamma(F)
,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(M;F)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;F)
; furthermore, it also extends to a well-defined and continuous operator
H^{r^{\prime}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)\rightarrow H^{r^{\prime}-r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;F)
and
H^{r^{\prime}}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)\rightarrow H^{r^{\prime}-r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;F)
,
r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
.

For
r\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
, the space of symbols
S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(T^{*}M;E,F)
consists of all
a\in\Gamma(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F))
such that for every chart
(O,x)
over which
E
and
F
locally trivialise via
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
as above, the functions
a^{l}_{\Phi,\Phi^{\prime},j}:T^{*}O\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
j=1,\ldots,k
,
l=1,\ldots,k^{\prime}
, defined by
a_{|O}=a^{l}_{\Phi,\Phi^{\prime},j}\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}(e^{\prime j}\otimes s_{l})
belong to
S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(T^{*}O)
where
(e^{\prime 1},\ldots,e^{\prime k})
and
(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k^{\prime}})
are the local frames for
E^{\prime}
and
F
over
O
induced by
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
and
(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}(e^{\prime j}\otimes s_{l}))_{j,l}
is the pullback local frame for
\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)
over
T^{*}O
.

Let
A\in\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
. For every chart
(O,x)
on
M
over which
E
and
F
locally trivialise, there are
\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\in S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x(O)\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
and
\widetilde{T}^{l}_{j}\in\Psi^{-\infty}(x(O))
,
j=1,\ldots,k
,
l=1,\ldots,k^{\prime}
, such that

(A\varphi)_{|O}=\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j})(\varphi^{j}\circ x^{-1})\circ x\,s_{l}+\widetilde{T}^{l}_{j}(\varphi^{j}\circ x^{-1})\circ x\,s_{l},\quad\varphi\in\Gamma_{c}(E_{O}),\,\varphi=\varphi^{j}e_{j},

where
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{k})
and
(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k^{\prime}})
are the local frames over
O
for
E
and
F
induced by their local trivialisations respectively. We call
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}
symbols of the coordinate representation of
A
; for fixed chart and local trivialisations of
E
and
F
,
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}
are unique modulo
S^{-\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x(O)\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
. The principal symbol of order
r\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
is a surjective linear map
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r}:\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)\rightarrow S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(T^{*}M;E,F)/S^{r-1}_{\operatorname{loc}}(T^{*}M;E,F)
whose kernel is
\Psi^{r-1}(M;E,F)
. With
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}
and
(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}(e^{\prime j}\otimes s_{l}))_{j,l}
as above and
\kappa
given by (
2.7
),
a:=\kappa^{*}(\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j})\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}(e^{\prime j}\otimes s_{l})\in S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(T^{*}O;E_{O},F_{O})
and
a\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r}(A)|_{T^{*}O}
. If
A
is properly supported and
A^{\prime}\in\Psi^{r^{\prime}}(M;F,H)
is properly supported with
r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
and
H
a vector bundle over
M
, then
A^{\prime}A\in\Psi^{r+r^{\prime}}(M;E,H)
,
A^{\prime}A
is properly supported and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r^{\prime}+r}(A^{\prime}A)=\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r^{\prime}}(A^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r}(A)
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{R}
. We say that
A\in\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
is of order
r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
,
r^{\prime}\leq r
, at
(p,\xi)\in T^{*}M\backslash 0
if there are a chart
(O,x)
about
p
over which
E
and
F
trivialise and an open cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
containing
\left(\xi(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{1}}|_{p}),\ldots,\xi(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{m}}|_{p})\right)
such that
\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\in S^{r^{\prime}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x(O)\times V)
,
j=1,\ldots,k
,
l=1\ldots,k^{\prime}
, where
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}
are symbols of the coordinate representation of
A
. The definition is independent of local coordinates, local trivialisations of
E
and
F
and of the choice of
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}
. We say that
A
is of order
r^{\prime}
in a conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
if
A
is of order
r^{\prime}
at every point of
L
; if
L=T^{*}M\backslash 0
, then
A\in\Psi^{r^{\prime}}(M;E,F)
. We say that
A\in\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
is polyhomogeneous of order
r
if for every point there is a chart
(O,x)
over which both
E
and
F
trivialise such that
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}\subseteq S^{r}_{\operatorname{loc},\operatorname{phg}}(x(O)\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
where
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}
are symbols of the coordinate representation of
A
. When this is the case, this is valid on all charts on
M
over which both
E
and
F
locally trivialise and for any local trivialisations of
E
and
F
. The space of polyhomogeneous
\Psi
DOs of order
r
is denote by
\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
. If
A
and
A^{\prime}\in\Psi^{r^{\prime}}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;F,H)
are properly supported, then
A^{\prime}A\in\Psi^{r^{\prime}+r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,H)
.

Assume now that
E
and
F
have the same rank
k
and
r\in\mathbb{R}
. The operator
A\in\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
is said to be elliptic if there is
b\in S^{-r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(T^{*}M;F,E)
such that
ba-\operatorname{I}_{E}\in S^{-1}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E,E)
and
ab-\operatorname{I}_{F}\in S^{-1}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;F,F)
for some (or, equivalently all)
a\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r}(A)
; here
\operatorname{I}_{E}\in\Gamma(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E))
is given by
\operatorname{I}_{E}(p,\xi):=((p,\xi),\operatorname{Id})\in\{(p,\xi)\}\times\mathcal{L}(E_{p},E_{p})
and
\operatorname{I}_{F}
is defined analogously. If in addition
A
is properly supported, then there exists a properly supported
A^{\prime}\in\Psi^{-r}(M;F,E)
, called a parametrix for
A
, such that
A^{\prime}A-\operatorname{Id}\in\Psi^{-\infty}(M;E,E)
and
AA^{\prime}-\operatorname{Id}\in\Psi^{-\infty}(M;F,F)
and
A^{\prime}
is unique modulo
\Psi^{-\infty}(M;F,E)
. The operator
A\in\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
is said to be non-characteristic at
(p,\xi)\in T^{*}M\backslash 0
if there is
b\in S^{-r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(T^{*}M;F,E)
such that both
ba-\operatorname{I}_{E}
and
ab-\operatorname{I}_{F}
are of order
-1
in a conic neighbourhood of
(p,\xi)
, for some (or, equivalently all)
a\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r}(A)
. The set of characteristic points of
A
is denoted by
\operatorname{Char}A
; it is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. The operator
A
is elliptic if and only if
\operatorname{Char}A=\emptyset
. We will need the following result; its proof is analogous to the proof of
[
29
, Theorem 18.1.24’, p. 88]
and we omit it.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
A\in\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
be properly supported and
(p,\xi)\not\in\operatorname{Char}A
. Then there is a properly supported
A^{\prime}\in\Psi^{-r}(M;F,E)
such that both
A^{\prime}A-\operatorname{Id}
and
AA^{\prime}-\operatorname{Id}
are of order
-\infty
in a conic neighbourhood of
(p,\xi)
.

3.
Spaces of distributions with Sobolev wave front in a fixed conic set: topological properties, characterisation of compact sets and pullback by smooth maps

Let
U
be an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Following Hörmander
[
30
]
, for
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
, we define the Sobolev wave front set
WF^{r}(u)
of order
r\in\mathbb{R}
as follows. The point
(x,\xi)\in U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
does not belong
WF^{r}(u)
if there are an open cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
containing
\xi
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
satisfying
\varphi(x)\neq 0
such that
\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)\|_{L^{2}(V)}<\infty
;
WF^{r}(u)
is a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
(see
[
30
, Definition 8.2.5, p. 188, and Proposition 8.2.6, p. 189]
). The original definition is due to Duistermaat and Hörmander
[
14
, p. 201]
and is via pseudo-differential operators but this amounts to the same thing in view of
[
30
, Proposition 8.2.6, p. 189]
. Given a closed conic subset
L
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
, we define

(3.1)

\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U):=\{u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\,|\,WF^{r}(u)\subseteq L\}.

Notice that if
L=\emptyset
, then (
3.1
) is
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)
and when
L=U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
, (
3.1
) is the whole
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Our goal in this section is to introduce a useful locally convex topology on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
which will make the pullback by smooth maps continuous, the above identities topological and which will be compatible with the topology on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)
form
[
8
]
in the sense that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)=\bigcap_{r\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
topologically (the right hand side should be read as a projective limit; see Proposition
4.21
below).

3.1.
The Sobolev compactness wave front set and the topology of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)

Let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(U)
; notice that
\bigcup_{u\in B}\operatorname{supp}u
is relatively compact in
U
. For
r\in\mathbb{R}
, we define the set
\Sigma_{c}^{r}(B)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
as follows. The point
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}

does not
belongs to
\Sigma_{c}^{r}(B)
if there is an open cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
containing
\eta
such that

(3.2)

\sup_{u\in B}\int_{\xi\in V,\,|\xi|>R}|\mathcal{F}u(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\rightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad R\rightarrow\infty.

Clearly,
\Sigma^{r}_{c}(B)
is a closed cone in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
. Employing classical arguments as in the proof of
[
28
, Lemma 8.1.1, p. 253]
, it is straightforward to show the following result (see the proof of Lemma
3.4
below for a similar type of argument).

Lemma 3.1
.

With
r
and
B
as above, it holds that
\Sigma^{r}_{c}(\varphi B)\subseteq\Sigma^{r}_{c}(B)
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
.

Let now
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. For every
x\in U
, we define

(3.3)

\Sigma_{c,x}^{r}(B):=\bigcap_{\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U),\,\varphi(x)\neq 0}\Sigma^{r}_{c}(\varphi B).

Clearly,
\Sigma_{c,x}^{r}(B)
is a closed cone in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
. The compactness of the unit sphere together with Lemma
3.1
yield the following result (cf.
[
28
, p. 253]
).

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{R}
,
x\in U
and
B
a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. If
\Sigma_{c,x}^{r}(B)\subseteq V
for some open cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
, then there exists an open neighbourhood
U^{\prime}\subseteq U
of
x
such that
\Sigma^{r}_{c}(\varphi B)\subseteq V
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U^{\prime})
.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{R}
and let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. The
Sobolev compactness wave front set of order
r
of
B
is

WF^{r}_{c}(B):=\{(x,\xi)\in U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})\,|\,\xi\in\Sigma^{r}_{c,x}(B)\}.

When
r=0
, this was introduced by Gérard
[
18
, Definition 1.1]
as a bookkeeping device to keep track of the frequencies where a weakly convergent sequence in
L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)
fails to have a convergent subsequence. We will show that
WF^{r}_{c}(\cdot)
is an effective tool for characterising the relatively compact subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
once we introduce the locally convex topology on it. For the moment, we point out that
WF^{r}_{c}(B)
is a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
in view of Lemma
3.2
, and, if
B
has only one element
u
(i.e.,
B=\{u\}
), then
WF^{r}_{c}(B)
is exactly the Sobolev wave front set
WF^{r}(u)
of
u
of order
r
; in this case we will also simply write
\Sigma^{r}_{x}(u)
. Furthermore, Lemma
3.1
implies that
WF^{r}_{c}(a_{1}B_{1}+a_{2}B_{2})\subseteq WF^{r}_{c}(B_{1})\cup WF^{r}_{c}(B_{2})
for any
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
and any bounded subsets
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
; if in addition
B_{1}\subseteq B_{2}
then
WF^{r}_{c}(B_{1})\subseteq WF^{r}_{c}(B_{2})
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
and let
L
be a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
. Then
WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq L
if and only if for every
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and every closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V)\cap L=\emptyset
it holds that

(3.4)

\sup_{u\in B}\int_{V,\,|\xi|>R}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\rightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad R\rightarrow\infty.

Proof.

If
L=U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
, then when
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)\backslash\{0\}
it holds that
V\subseteq\{0\}
and the claim in the lemma is trivial. Assume that
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. If (
3.4
) is satisfied for every
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and
V
as in the lemma, then clearly
WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq L
. Assume now that
WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq L
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
V\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
, are such that
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V)\cap L=\emptyset
. Set
K:=\operatorname{supp}\varphi
. For each
(y,\eta)\in K\times V
there is
\varphi_{(y,\eta)}\in\mathcal{D}(U)
with
\varphi_{(y,\eta)}(y)\neq 0
such that
\eta\not\in\Sigma^{r}_{c}(\varphi_{(y,\eta)}B)
. In view of Lemma
3.1
, we can assume that
\varphi_{(y,\eta)}
is nonnegative and
\varphi_{(y,\eta)}(y)=1
. There is an open cone
V^{\prime}_{(y,\eta)}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
containing
\eta
such that

\sup_{u\in B}\int_{V^{\prime}_{(y,\eta)},\,|\xi|>R}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{(y,\eta)}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\rightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad R\rightarrow\infty.

Pick an open cone
V_{(y,\eta)}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
such that
\eta\in V_{(y,\eta)}
and
\overline{V_{(y,\eta)}}\backslash\{0\}\subseteq V^{\prime}_{(y,\eta)}
. Since
V\cap\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
is compact, we infer that for each
y\in K
there are a finite number of open cones
V_{y,l}:=V_{(y,\eta^{(l)})}
,
l=1,\ldots,k_{y}
, whose union covers
V
. For each
y\in K
, set
\varphi_{y}:=\varphi_{(y,\eta^{(1)})}\cdot\ldots\cdot\varphi_{(y,\eta^{(k_{y})})}
and notice that
\varphi_{y}\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and
\varphi_{y}(y)=1
. For every
y\in K
, denote by
O_{y}
the open set
\{x\in U\,|\,\varphi_{y}(x)>1/2\}
. As
K
is compact, there are a finite number of open sets
O_{j}:=O_{y^{(j)}}
,
j=1,\ldots,m
, whose union covers
K
. Set
\varphi_{j}:=\varphi_{y^{(j)}}
,
j=1,\ldots,m
, and
\psi=\varphi_{1}+\ldots+\varphi_{m}
. Clearly
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and
\psi>1/2
on a neighbourhood of
K
. Hence
\psi_{j}:=(\varphi/\psi)\varphi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and
\varphi=\psi_{1}+\ldots+\psi_{m}
. For each
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, set
k_{j}:=k_{y^{(j)}}
and additionally
V_{j,l}:=V_{y^{(j)},l}
,
V^{\prime}_{j,l}:=V^{\prime}_{(y^{(j)},\eta^{(l)})}
and
\varphi_{j,l}:=\varphi_{(y^{(j)},\eta^{(l)})}
,
l=1,\ldots,k_{j}
. We infer

\left(\int_{V,\,|\xi|>R}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{k_{j}}\left(\int_{V_{j,l},\,|\xi|>R}|\mathcal{F}(\psi_{j}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}.

We show that for every
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
l\in\{1,\ldots,k_{j}\}
,

(3.5)

\sup_{u\in B}\int_{V_{j,l},\,|\xi|\geq R}|\mathcal{F}(\psi_{j}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\rightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad R\rightarrow\infty,

which will complete the proof. Fix such
j
and
l
. Set
4
4

4

Here and throughout the rest of the article we employ the principle of vacuous (empty) products; i.e.,
\prod_{j=1}^{0}q_{j}=\prod_{j\in\emptyset}q_{j}=1
.

\chi_{j,l}:=(\varphi/\psi)\prod_{l^{\prime}\neq l}\varphi_{j,l^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and notice that
\psi_{j}=\chi_{j,l}\varphi_{j,l}
. There is
c\in(0,1)
such that

(3.6)

\{\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,\exists\xi\in V_{j,l}\,\,\mbox{such that}\,\,|\xi-\eta|\leq c|\xi|\}\subseteq V^{\prime}_{j,l}.

Since
\mathcal{F}(\psi_{j}u)=(2\pi)^{-n}\mathcal{F}(\chi_{j,l})*\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j,l}u)
, the Minkowski integral inequality gives

\displaystyle\left(\int_{V_{j,l},\,|\xi|\geq R}|\mathcal{F}(\psi_{j}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j,l}(\eta)|\left(\int_{V_{j,l},\,|\xi|\geq R}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j,l}u)(\xi-\eta)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{|r|}}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j,l}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{V_{j,l},\,|\xi|\geq R}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j,l}u)(\xi-\eta)|^{2}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq 2^{|r|}(2\pi)^{-n}(I_{1}+I_{2}),

with

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j,l}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V_{j,l}\\
|\xi|\geq\max\{|\eta|/c,R\}\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j,l}u)(\xi-\eta)|^{2}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j,l}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V_{j,l}\\
R\leq|\xi|<|\eta|/c\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j,l}u)(\xi-\eta)|^{2}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta.

We change variables in the inner integral in
I_{1}
to obtain

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j,l}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V_{j,l}-\{\eta\}\\
|\xi+\eta|\geq\max\{|\eta|/c,R\}\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j,l}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j,l}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{V^{\prime}_{j,l},\,|\xi|\geq(1-c)R}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j,l}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle=\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\chi_{j,l}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\left(\int_{V^{\prime}_{j,l},\,|\xi|\geq(1-c)R}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j,l}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2},

where in the inequality we employed the fact

(3.7)

\{\xi\in V_{j,l}-\{\eta\}\,|\,|\xi+\eta|\geq\max\{|\eta|/c,R\}\}\subseteq\{\xi\in V^{\prime}_{j,l}\,|\,|\xi|\geq(1-c)R\}.

To verify it, first notice that (
3.6
) gives
\{\xi\in V_{j,l}-\{\eta\}\,|\,|\xi+\eta|\geq|\eta|/c\}\subseteq V^{\prime}_{j,l}
. When
\xi
belong to the left-hand side of (
3.7
), we have
|\xi|\geq(c^{-1}-1)|\eta|
and consequently

|\xi|\geq R-|\eta|\geq R-c|\xi|/(1-c),\quad\mbox{whence}\quad|\xi|\geq(1-c)R,

which proves (
3.7
). To bound
I_{2}
, notice that since
B
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
there are
C^{\prime}_{1},s>0
such that
|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j,l}u)(\xi)|\leq C^{\prime}_{1}\langle\xi\rangle^{s}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
u\in B
. Hence

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{1}\int_{|\eta|>cR}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j,l}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V_{j,l}\\
R\leq|\xi|<|\eta|/c\end{subarray}}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle^{2(s+|r|)}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{1}(1+c^{-1})^{s+|r|}c^{-n}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\int_{|\eta|>cR}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j,l}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{2|r|+s+n}d\eta.

These bounds for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
imply the validity of (
3.5
) and the proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

Applying the lemma when
B
is a singleton, we deduce the following corollary.

Corollary 3.5
.

The distribution
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
belongs to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
if and only if for every
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and every closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V)\cap L=\emptyset
it holds that

\int_{V}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi<\infty.

With the help of the corollary, we define the following set of seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
:

\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(u):=\left(\int_{V}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2},

where
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and
V
are as in Corollary
3.5
. We equip
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
with the locally convex topology induced by all continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
together with all seminorms
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}
where
\varphi
and
V
are as in Corollary
3.5
. Clearly,
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
continuously.

Remark 3.6
.

If
L=U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
, then
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
topologically since in this case
V
can only be
\{0\}
or
\emptyset
when
\varphi
is not the zero function and hence
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}=0
.

When
L=\emptyset
,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\emptyset}(U)=H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)
topologically since one can take
V=\mathbb{R}^{n}
in this case.

All of the important topological properties of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
will follow from the following proposition. To set the stage, we need the following objects. Assume that
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. Pick a countable dense subset
\{(x^{(j)},\xi^{(j)})\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
of
L^{c}
and denote
\omega^{(j)}:=\xi^{(j)}/|\xi^{(j)}|\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Set
5
5

5

Here we employ
\operatorname{dist}(x,\emptyset)=\infty
, for any element
x
.

s_{j}:=\min\{1,\operatorname{dist}(x^{(j)},\partial U),\operatorname{dist}((x^{(j)},\omega^{(j)}),L)\}>0.

Clearly,
(\overline{B(x^{(j)},s_{j}/2)}\times\overline{B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/2)})\cap L=\emptyset
and
B(x^{(j)},s_{j})\subseteq U
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. For each
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we set:

\displaystyle O_{j,k}:=B(x^{(j)},s_{j}/(5k)),\quad O^{\prime}_{j,k}:=B(x^{(j)},s_{j}/(4k)),\quad O^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}:=B(x^{(j)},s_{j}/(3k));

\displaystyle V_{j,k}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/(5k)),\quad V^{\prime}_{j,k}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/(4k)),\quad V^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/(3k)).

Notice that

\overline{O_{j,k}}\times\overline{V_{j,k}}\subseteq O^{\prime}_{j,k}\times(V^{\prime}_{j,k}\cup\{0\}),\quad\overline{O^{\prime}_{j,k}}\times\overline{V^{\prime}_{j,k}}\subseteq O^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}\times(V^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}\cup\{0\}),\quad(\overline{O^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}}\times\overline{V^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}})\cap L=\emptyset.

For each
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, pick
\phi_{j,k}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
which satisfy the following conditions:

(a)

0\leq\phi_{j,k}\leq 1
and
0\leq\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\leq 1
;

(b)

\phi_{j,k}=1
on
\overline{O_{j,k}}
and
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{j,k}\subseteq O^{\prime}_{j,k}
;

(c)

\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}=1
on
\overline{V}_{j,k}\backslash B(0,2)
and
\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\subseteq V^{\prime}_{j,k}\backslash\overline{B(0,1)}
;

(d)

\|\partial^{\alpha}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}<\infty
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
satisfying
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. Let
O_{j,k}
,
O^{\prime}_{j,k}
,
O^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}
,
V_{j,k}
,
V^{\prime}_{j,k}
,
V^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}
,
\phi_{j,k}
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}
be as above. Then the mapping

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\times(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}}),\quad\mathcal{I}(u)=(u,\mathbf{f}_{u}),\,\,\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{u}(j,k):=\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u),\,j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},

is a well-defined topological imbedding with closed image.

Proof.

The proof that
\mathcal{I}
is a well-defined continuous injection is straightforward and we omit it (cf. Corollary
3.5
). We now prove that
\mathcal{I}
is open mapping onto its image. It suffices to show that for each
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)\backslash\{0\}
and every closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
V\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
, satisfying
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V)\cap L=\emptyset
, there is a finite
J\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, a continuous seminorm
\mathfrak{p}
on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
and
C>0
such that

(3.8)

\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(u)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(u)+C\sum_{(j,k)\in J}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},\quad u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U).

Fix such
\varphi
and
V
. There is
0<\varepsilon<1
such that

\big((\operatorname{supp}\varphi+\overline{B(0,\varepsilon)})\times((V\cap\mathbb{S}^{n-1})+\overline{B(0,\varepsilon)})\big)\cap L=\emptyset\quad\mbox{and}\quad\operatorname{supp}\varphi+\overline{B(0,\varepsilon)}\subseteq U.

Define the closed cones
V^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime\prime}
by

V^{\prime\prime}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}\big((V\cap\mathbb{S}^{n-1})+\overline{B(0,\varepsilon)}\big)\cup\{0\}\quad\mbox{and}\quad V^{\prime}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}\big((V\cap\mathbb{S}^{n-1})+\overline{B(0,\varepsilon/15)}\big)\cup\{0\}

and the compact sets
K^{\prime\prime}
,
K^{\prime}
and
K
by

K^{\prime\prime}:=\operatorname{supp}\varphi+\overline{B(0,\varepsilon)},\quad K^{\prime}:=\operatorname{supp}\varphi+\overline{B(0,\varepsilon/15)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad K:=\operatorname{supp}\varphi.

Notice that

K\times V\subseteq K^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}\subseteq K^{\prime\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime}\subseteq U\times V^{\prime\prime}\quad\mbox{and}\quad(K^{\prime\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime})\cap L=\emptyset.

Set
\widetilde{J}:=\{(j,k)\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}\,|\,k\geq 15/\varepsilon,\,(O_{j,k}\times B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/(5k)))\cap(K\times V)\neq\emptyset\}
. We claim that

(3.9)

\widetilde{J}\neq\emptyset\quad\mbox{and}\quad K\times(V\backslash\{0\})\subseteq\bigcup_{(j,k)\in\widetilde{J}}O_{j,k}\times V_{j,k}\subseteq\bigcup_{(j,k)\in\widetilde{J}}\overline{O^{\prime}_{j,k}}\times\overline{V^{\prime}_{j,k}}\subseteq K^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}.

We show
\widetilde{J}\neq\emptyset
and the first inclusion simultaneously. Let
(x,\xi)\in K\times(V\backslash\{0\})
and set
\omega:=\xi/|\xi|\in V\cap\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
and
s:=\min\{1,\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial U),\operatorname{dist}((x,\omega),L)\}>0
. Pick any integer
k\geq 15/\varepsilon
(the condition
k\geq 15/\varepsilon
is important for the last inclusion in (
3.9
) and the subsequent part of the proof) and set
m:=10k+4
. Since
(\overline{B(x,s/m)}\times\overline{B(\omega,s/m)})\cap L=\emptyset
, there is
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
|x-x^{(j)}|<s/m
and
|\omega-\xi^{(j)}|<s/m
. The second inequality yields

|1-|\xi^{(j)}||=||\omega|-|\xi^{(j)}||\leq|\omega-\xi^{(j)}|<s/m

which implies
|\xi^{(j)}|>3/4
. It also gives
||\xi^{(j)}|^{-1}-1|<s/(m|\xi^{(j)}|)<4s/(3m)
. Since

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}((x,\omega),L)

\displaystyle\leq|(x,\omega)-(x^{(j)},\omega^{(j)})|+\operatorname{dist}((x^{(j)},\omega^{(j)}),L)\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial U)

\displaystyle\leq|x-x^{(j)}|+\operatorname{dist}(x^{(j)},\partial U),

we infer

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle\leq|x-x^{(j)}|+|\omega-\omega^{(j)}|+s_{j}\leq\frac{s}{m}+\left|\omega-\frac{\omega}{|\xi^{(j)}|}\right|+\left|\omega^{(j)}-\frac{\omega}{|\xi^{(j)}|}\right|+s_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{s}{m}+\left|1-\frac{1}{|\xi^{(j)}|}\right|+\frac{|\xi^{(j)}-\omega|}{|\xi^{(j)}|}+s_{j}<\frac{4s}{m}+s_{j}

and hence
s<ms_{j}/(m-4)
. Consequently,

\left|\frac{\omega}{|\xi^{(j)}|}-\frac{\xi^{(j)}}{|\xi^{(j)}|}\right|<\frac{s}{m|\xi^{(j)}|}<\frac{2s}{m}<\frac{2s_{j}}{m-4}=\frac{s_{j}}{5k}.

We deduce
\omega/|\xi^{(j)}|\in B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/5k)\cap V
. We also have
|x-x^{(j)}|<s/m<s_{j}/(5k)
and hence
x\in O_{j,k}\cap K
. Thus,
\widetilde{J}\neq\emptyset
. The above considerations also immediately yield
(x,\xi)\in O_{j,k}\times V_{j,k}
which shows the first inclusion in (
3.9
).

To prove the last inclusion in (
3.9
), let
(x,\xi)\in\overline{O^{\prime}_{j,k}}\times\overline{V^{\prime}_{j,k}}
for some
(j,k)\in\widetilde{J}
. Then
|x-x^{(j)}|\leq s_{j}/(4k)
and there are
t\geq 0
and
\xi^{\prime}
such that
\xi=t\xi^{\prime}
and
|\xi^{\prime}-\omega^{(j)}|\leq s_{j}/(4k)
. As
(j,k)\in\widetilde{J}
, there are
\widetilde{x}\in K
and
\widetilde{\xi}\in V\backslash\{0\}
such that
|\widetilde{x}-x^{(j)}|<s_{j}/(5k)
and
|\widetilde{\xi}-\omega^{(j)}|<s_{j}/(5k)
. Similarly as before, the last inequality immediately gives
|\widetilde{\xi}|>1/2
. These inequalities also imply
|x-\widetilde{x}|\leq s_{j}/(4k)+s_{j}/(5k)<s_{j}/(2k)\leq\varepsilon/30
and hence
x\in K^{\prime}
. Similarly,
|\xi^{\prime}-\widetilde{\xi}|<\varepsilon/30
and hence
|\xi^{\prime}/|\widetilde{\xi}|-\widetilde{\xi}/|\widetilde{\xi}||<\varepsilon/(30|\widetilde{\xi}|)<\varepsilon/15
. We infer
\xi^{\prime}\in V^{\prime}
. Consequently
\xi\in V^{\prime}
and the proof of the last inclusion in (
3.9
) is complete.

Now, a standard compactness argument yields the existence of a finite
J_{0}\subseteq\widetilde{J}
such that

K\times(V\backslash\{0\})\subseteq\bigcup_{(j,k)\in J_{0}}O_{j,k}\times V_{j,k}\subseteq\bigcup_{(j,k)\in\widetilde{J}}\overline{O^{\prime}_{j,k}}\times\overline{V^{\prime}_{j,k}}\subseteq K^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}.

Set
\phi:=\sum_{(j,k)\in J_{0}}\phi_{j,k}\in\mathcal{D}(\bigcup_{(j,k)\in J_{0}}O^{\prime}_{j,k})
and notice that
\phi\geq 1
on
\bigcup_{(j,k)\in J_{0}}\overline{O_{j,k}}
. Hence
\varphi/\phi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
. We also denote
\psi_{j,k}:=(\varphi/\phi)\phi_{j,k}\in\mathcal{D}(O^{\prime}_{j,k})
,
(j,k)\in J_{0}
, and notice that
\sum_{(j,k)\in J_{0}}\psi_{j,k}=\varphi
. As
V\backslash\{0\}\subseteq\bigcup_{(j,k)\in J_{0}}V_{j,k}
, we infer

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(u)

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)\|_{L^{2}(B(0,2))}+\sum_{(j,k)\in J_{0}}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B(0,2))}

(3.10)

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)\|_{L^{2}(B(0,2))}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(j,k)\in J_{0}\\
(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B(0,2))}.

Let
(j,k),(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})\in J_{0}
be arbitrary but fixed. The last inclusion in (
3.9
) implies that there is
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
(x^{(m)},\xi^{(m)})\in O_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\times B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/(5k))
; hence

(3.11)

|x^{(j^{\prime})}-x^{(m)}|<s_{j^{\prime}}/(5k^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon/75,\quad|\omega^{(j)}-\xi^{(m)}|<s_{j}/(5k)\leq\varepsilon/75.

The second bound yields
|1-|\xi^{(m)}||\leq|\omega^{(j)}-\xi^{(m)}|\leq\varepsilon/75
and hence

(3.12)

|\omega^{(j)}-\omega^{(m)}|\leq|\omega^{(j)}-\xi^{(m)}|+\left|\xi^{(m)}-\frac{\xi^{(m)}}{|\xi^{(m)}|}\right|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{75}+||\xi^{(m)}|-1|\leq\frac{2\varepsilon}{75}.

We claim that

(3.13)

\operatorname{dist}((x^{(m)},\xi^{(m)}),L)\geq\delta,\quad\mbox{with}\quad\delta:=67\varepsilon/150\in(0,1).

Assume that (
3.13
) does not hold. There is
(y,\eta)\in L
such that
|(x^{(m)},\xi^{(m)})-(y,\eta)|<\delta
and hence

\displaystyle\left|(x^{(j^{\prime})},\omega^{(j)})-(y,\eta)\right|\leq\left|(x^{(j^{\prime})},\omega^{(j)})-(x^{(m)},\xi^{(m)})\right|+\left|(x^{(m)},\xi^{(m)})-(y,\eta)\right|<2\varepsilon/75+\delta.

Consequently,
|x^{(j^{\prime})}-y|\leq 2\varepsilon/75+\delta
and
|\omega^{(j)}-\eta|\leq 2\varepsilon/75+\delta
. Since
x^{(j^{\prime})}\in K^{\prime}
, the first inequality implies that
y\in K^{\prime\prime}
. Pick
\widetilde{\xi}\in B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/(5k))\cap V
(such
\widetilde{\xi}
exists by the way we defined
\widetilde{J}
). Similarly as before, one easily shows that
|\widetilde{\xi}|>1/2
. As
|\widetilde{\xi}-\eta|\leq|\widetilde{\xi}-\omega^{(j)}|+|\omega^{(j)}-\eta|\leq\varepsilon/25+\delta
, we infer

\left|\frac{\widetilde{\xi}}{|\widetilde{\xi}|}-\frac{\eta}{|\widetilde{\xi}|}\right|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{25|\widetilde{\xi}|}+\frac{\delta}{|\widetilde{\xi}|}<\frac{2\varepsilon}{25}+\frac{67\varepsilon}{75}<\varepsilon.

Thus,
\eta\in V^{\prime\prime}
. We deduce
(y,\eta)\in(K^{\prime\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime})\cap L
which is a contradiction. Hence, (
3.13
) holds true. Analogously, assuming
\operatorname{dist}(x^{(m)},\partial U)<\delta
leads to
\operatorname{dist}(x^{(j^{\prime})},\partial U)<\varepsilon/2
which is in contradiction with
K^{\prime\prime}\subseteq U
; whence
\operatorname{dist}(x^{(m)},\partial U)\geq\delta
. Consequently
s_{m}\geq\delta
. We show that

(3.14)

O^{\prime}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\subseteq O_{m,1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad V^{\prime}_{j,k}\subseteq V_{m,2}.

The first inclusion follows from the following (cf. (
3.11
))

x\in O^{\prime}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\,\,\,\Rightarrow\,\,\,|x-x^{(m)}|\leq|x-x^{(j^{\prime})}|+|x^{(j^{\prime})}-x^{(m)}|<s_{j^{\prime}}/(4k)+\varepsilon/75<\varepsilon/30<s_{m}/5.

To verify the second inclusion, it suffices to show that
B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/(4k))\subseteq B(\omega^{(m)},s_{m}/10)
. Let
\xi\in B(\omega^{(j)},s_{j}/(4k))
. In view of (
3.12
), we infer

|\xi-\omega^{(m)}|\leq|\xi-\omega^{(j)}|+|\omega^{(j)}-\omega^{(m)}|<s_{j}/(4k)+2\varepsilon/75\leq\varepsilon/60+2\varepsilon/75<s_{m}/10,

which yields that
\xi\in B(\omega^{(m)},s_{m}/10)
. The inclusions (
3.14
) imply
\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}=\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\widetilde{\phi}_{m,2}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B(0,2)
and
\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\phi_{m,1}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. We infer

\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B(0,2))}\leq\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{m,2}\mathcal{F}(\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\phi_{m,1}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B(0,2))},\quad u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U).

Since
\overline{V^{\prime}_{m,2}}\backslash\{0\}\subseteq V_{m,1}
, there is
0<c<1
such that

(3.15)

\{\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,\exists\xi\in V^{\prime}_{m,2}\,\,\mbox{such that}\,\,|\xi-\eta|\leq c|\xi|\}\subseteq V_{m,1}.

Let
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
be arbitrary but fixed. As
\mathcal{F}(\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\phi_{m,1}u)=(2\pi)^{-n}\mathcal{F}\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}*\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)
, the Minkowski integral inequality and a change of variables give

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{m,2}\mathcal{F}(\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\phi_{m,1}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B(0,2))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(\eta)|\left(\int_{V^{\prime}_{m,2}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)(\xi-\eta)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{|r|}}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\xi\in V^{\prime}_{m,2}-\{\eta\}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq 2^{|r|}(2\pi)^{-n}(I_{1}+I_{2}),

where

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V^{\prime}_{m,2}-\{\eta\}\\
|\xi+\eta|\geq|\eta|/c\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V^{\prime}_{m,2}-\{\eta\}\\
|\xi+\eta|<|\eta|/c\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta.

To estimate
I_{1}
notice that (
3.15
) yields
\{\xi\in V^{\prime}_{m,2}-\{\eta\}\,|\,|\xi+\eta|\geq|\eta|/c\}\subseteq V_{m,1}
and hence

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\left(\int_{V_{m,1}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\psi_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\left(\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)\|_{L^{2}(B(0,2))}+\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{m,1}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\right).

Since the mapping
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
u\mapsto\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)
, is continuous, there is a continuous seminorm
\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}
on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
such that

I_{1}\leq C_{1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}(u)+C_{2}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{m,1}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{m,1}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

To estimate
I_{2}
, we make the following

Claim.
Let
f
be a nonnegative measurable function on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
which satisfies
\langle\cdot\rangle^{\nu}f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, for all
\nu>0
. For each
r\in\mathbb{R}
,
t>0
and
\chi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
, the mapping

(3.16)

\mathfrak{p}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow[0,\infty),\quad\mathfrak{p}(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}f(\eta)\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
|\xi+\eta|<t|\eta|\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}(\chi u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta,

is a well-defined continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
.

We defer its proof for later and continue with the proof of the proposition. The claim implies that
I_{2}
is bounded from above by a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
of the same type as (
3.16
). In view of (
3.10
) we conclude the validity of (
3.8
); notice that the term
\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)\|_{L^{2}(B(0,2))}
in (
3.10
) is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
(since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
u\mapsto\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)
, is continuous). This completes the proof that
\mathcal{I}
is a topological imbedding.

We now show that the range of
\mathcal{I}
is closed. Let
(u_{\mu})_{\mu\in\Lambda}
be a net in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
such that
\mathcal{I}(u_{\mu})=(u_{\mu},\textbf{f}_{u_{\mu}})
converges to
(u,\mathbf{g})\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\times(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}})
in the topology of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\times(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}})
. Hence
u_{\mu}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
and, for each
(j,k)\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u_{\mu})\rightarrow g_{j,k}
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. The former implies that
\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u_{\mu})\rightarrow\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)
in
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and consequently
\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)=g_{j,k}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. It is straightforward to show that this implies
WF^{r}(u)\subseteq L
(cf. the proof of the first inclusion in (
3.9
)) and thus
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
. This completes the proof that the range of
\mathcal{I}
is closed.

Proof of Claim.
Set
\widetilde{K}:=\operatorname{supp}\chi
. We show that
\mathfrak{p}
is well-defined and bounded on bounded subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Hence
B
is equicontinuous (as
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
is barrelled) and consequently also equicontinuous as a subset of
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{K}},\mathbb{C})
. Whence, there are
C>0
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
|\langle u,\varphi\rangle|\leq C\sup_{|\alpha|\leq k}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{K}}
,
u\in B
. This implies

(3.17)

|\mathcal{F}(\chi u)(\xi)|=|\langle u,\chi e^{-i\,\cdot\,\xi}\rangle|\leq C2^{k}\langle\xi\rangle^{k}\sup_{|\alpha|\leq k}\|\partial^{\alpha}\chi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},\quad\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\,u\in B.

For each
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,|\xi+\eta|<t|\eta|\}\subseteq B(0,(1+t)|\eta|)
and thus

\mathfrak{p}(u)\leq C_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}f(\eta)\left(\int_{\xi\in B(0,(1+t)|\eta|)}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(|r|+k)}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta\leq C_{2}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|+k+n}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

Hence,
\mathfrak{p}
is a well-defined seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
and it is bounded on bounded subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
is bornological,
\mathfrak{p}
is continuous and the proof is complete.
∎

Remark 3.8
.

Employing the same arguments as in the proof of the first inclusion in (
3.9
), one shows that
L^{c}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}O_{j,k}\times V_{j,k}
for each fixed
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.

Remark 3.9
.

Arguing as in Lemma
3.4
and employing similar reasoning as in the proof of the above claim, one shows that the bilinear map
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
,
(\chi,u)\mapsto\chi u
, is well-defined and hypocontinuous.

Corollary 3.10
.

Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
. Then
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
is complete, semi-reflexive and a strictly webbed space in the sense of De Wilde.

Proof.

The fact that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
is complete is an immediate consequence of the proposition and it is semi-reflexive in view of
[
35
, Theorem 5 and Theorem 6, p. 299]
. Furthermore,
[
36
, Theorem 1, p. 61, and Theorem 6, p. 62]
implies that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
is a strictly webbed space in the sense of De Wilde (
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
is strictly webbed in view of
[
36
, Theorem 13, p. 64]
).
∎

The fact that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
is a strictly webbed space allows one to employ De Wilde’s closed graph and open mapping theorems (see
[
36
, Chapter 35]
) when considering maps to and from
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
. As a consequence of Proposition
3.7
, we now derive the following important characterisation of the relatively compact subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
which we announced at the beginning of the subsection.

Corollary 3.11
.

Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
and let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Then
B
is a relatively compact subset
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
if and only if
WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq L
.

Remark 3.12
.

The corollary can be viewed as a generalisation of the Kolmogorov-Riesz compactness theorem
[
23
]
. Indeed, taking
L=\emptyset
, the corollary together with Lemma
3.4
immediately give the following well known characterisation of relatively compact subsets of
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)
. A bounded subset
B
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
is a relatively compact subset of
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)
if and only if

\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{u\in B}\int_{|\xi|>R}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)(\xi)|^{2}d\xi=0,\quad\mbox{for all}\,\,\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U).

Proof of Corollary
3.11
.

The claim is trivial when
L=U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
(since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
is Montel). Assume that
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. If
B
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
, Proposition
3.7
implies that for each
(j,k)\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
\{\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)\,|\,u\in B\}
is relatively compact in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the Kolmogorov-Riesz compactness theorem
[
23
, Theorem 5]
yields

\sup_{u\in B}\int_{\xi\in V_{j,k},\,|\xi|>R}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\rightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad R\rightarrow\infty,\quad\mbox{for each}\,\,j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

It is straightforward to verify that the latter implies
WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq L
(cf. Remark
3.8
).

Assume now
WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq L
; hence
B\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
. In view of Lemma
3.4
, we infer

\sup_{u\in B}\int_{|\xi|>R}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi)^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\rightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad R\rightarrow\infty,\quad\mbox{for each}\,\,j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

We claim that this implies that

(3.18)

\{\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)\,|\,u\in B\}\,\,\mbox{is relatively compact in}\,\,L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\,\,\mbox{for each}\,\,j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

Once we show (
3.18
) the claim in the corollary follows from Tychonoff’s theorem and Proposition
3.7
(
B
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
is Montel). Since
B
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
, we immediately deduce that

\sup_{u\in B}\int_{|\xi|\leq R}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi)^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi<\infty,\quad\mbox{for all}\,\,R>0,\,\,j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},

and hence the sets in (
3.18
) are bounded in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. In view of the Kolmogorov-Riesz compactness theorem
[
23
, Theorem 5]
, to verify (
3.18
) it suffices to show that for each
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
it holds that

(3.19)

\sup_{u\in B}\|\langle\cdot+\eta\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\cdot+\eta)\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\cdot+\eta)-\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\rightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad\eta\rightarrow 0.

We Taylor expand
\langle\xi+\eta\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+\eta)\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+\eta)
at
\xi
up to order
0
to infer

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\langle\xi+\eta\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+\eta)\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+\eta)-\langle\xi\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi)\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi)|

\displaystyle\leq|\eta|\sum_{l=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}|r(\xi_{l}+t\eta_{l})\langle\xi+t\eta\rangle^{r-2}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+t\eta)\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+t\eta)

\displaystyle{}\qquad\qquad\qquad+\langle\xi+t\eta\rangle^{r}\partial_{l}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+t\eta)\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+t\eta)

\displaystyle{}\qquad\qquad\qquad+\langle\xi+t\eta\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+t\eta)\partial_{l}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+t\eta)|dt.

Set
\phi_{j,k;l}(x):=x_{l}\phi_{j,k}(x)
,
l=1,\ldots,n
. We infer

\displaystyle|
