\displaystyle\langle\xi+\eta\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+\eta)\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+\eta)-\langle\xi\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi)\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi)|

\displaystyle\leq n|r||\eta|\int_{0}^{1}\langle\xi+t\eta\rangle^{r-1}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+t\eta)|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+t\eta)|dt

\displaystyle{}\quad+|\eta|\sum_{l=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}\langle\xi+t\eta\rangle^{r}|\partial_{l}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+t\eta)||\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+t\eta)|dt

\displaystyle{}\quad+|\eta|\sum_{l=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}\langle\xi+t\eta\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+t\eta)|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k;l}u)(\xi+t\eta)|dt.

Employing the Minkowski integral inequality, a change of variables and the property
(d)
of
\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}
we obtain

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\langle\cdot+\eta\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\cdot+\eta)\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\cdot+\eta)-\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq n|r||\eta|\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\xi+t\eta\rangle^{2r-2}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+t\eta)^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+t\eta)|^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}dt

\displaystyle{}\quad+|\eta|\sum_{l=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\xi+t\eta\rangle^{2r}|\partial_{l}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+t\eta)|^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)(\xi+t\eta)|^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}dt

\displaystyle{}\quad+|\eta|\sum_{l=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\xi+t\eta\rangle^{2r}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}(\xi+t\eta)^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k;l}u)(\xi+t\eta)|^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}dt

\displaystyle\leq C|\eta|\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)\|_{L^{2}(\overline{V^{\prime}_{j,k}})}+|\eta|\sum_{l=1}^{n}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k;l}u)\|_{L^{2}(\overline{V^{\prime}_{j,k}})}.

Lemma
3.4
together with the fact that
B
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
implies that

\sup_{u\in B}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u)\|_{L^{2}(\overline{V^{\prime}_{j,k}})}<\infty\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{u\in B}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k;l}u)\|_{L^{2}(\overline{V^{\prime}_{j,k}})}<\infty,\,l=1,\ldots,n.

Consequently, the above estimates verify (
3.19
) and the proof is complete.
∎

We end this subsection with the following result on the existence of a sequence of smoothing operators on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
that approximate the identity operator on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
.

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
\psi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(U)
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, be such that
0\leq\psi_{j}\leq 1
,
\psi_{j}=1
on the compact
\{x\in U\,|\,|x|\leq j,\,\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial U)\geq 3/j\}
and
\operatorname{supp}\psi_{j}\subseteq\{x\in U\,|\,\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial U)>2/j\}
. Let
\chi
be a nonnegative function in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfying
\operatorname{supp}\chi\subseteq\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,|x|\leq 1\}
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\chi(x)dx=1
and set
\chi_{j}(x):=j^{n}\chi(jx)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. For each
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, the operators

(3.20)

P_{j}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(U),\quad P_{j}u=\chi_{j}*(\psi_{j}u),

are well-defined and continuous. Furthermore, for each
r\in\mathbb{R}
and each closed conic subset
L
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
,
\{P_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))
and
P_{j}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))
(the index
p
stands for the topology of precompact convergence). In particular,
\mathcal{D}(U)
is sequentially dense in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
.

Proof.

Clearly, (
3.20
) are well-defined and continuous. It is a well-known fact that
P_{j}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U))
(the index
\sigma
stands for the topology of simple convergence). The Banach–Steinhaus theorem
[
50
, Theorem 4.5, p. 85]
together with the fact that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
is Montel implies that the convergence holds in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U))
and consequently
\{P_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U))
. This proves the claim when
L=U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
.

Assume that
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. Because of the above, to prove the boundedness of
\{P_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))
it remains to show that for every bounded set
B
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
and every seminorm
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}
, it holds that
\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\sup_{u\in B}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(P_{j}u)<\infty
. Let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
V\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
, are such that
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V)\cap L=\emptyset
. Employing a standard compactness argument, one can find
\widetilde{\varphi}\in\mathcal{D}(U)
satisfying
\widetilde{\varphi}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
and a closed cone
\widetilde{V}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
V\backslash\{0\}\subseteq\operatorname{int}\widetilde{V}
such that
(\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{\varphi}\times\widetilde{V})\cap L=\emptyset
. There is
j_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
u_{j}:=\chi_{j}*(\widetilde{\varphi}u)\in\mathcal{D}(U)
, for all
u\in B
,
j\geq j_{0}
. There is
j^{\prime}\geq j_{0}
such that
\psi_{j}=\widetilde{\varphi}=1
on
\operatorname{supp}\varphi+\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,|x|\leq 2/j\}
, for all
j\geq j^{\prime}
. Consequently,

\varphi P_{j}u-\varphi u_{j}=\varphi(\chi_{j}*((\psi_{j}-\widetilde{\varphi})u))=0,\quad j\geq j^{\prime},\,\,u\in B.

Hence, it suffices to show that
\sup_{j\geq j^{\prime}}\sup_{u\in B}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(u_{j})<\infty
. Since
|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi)|\leq 1
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we infer (for
j\geq j^{\prime}
)

(3.21)

|\mathcal{F}(\varphi u_{j})(\xi)|=(2\pi)^{-n}|(\mathcal{F}\varphi)*(\mathcal{F}(\chi_{j})\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u))(\xi)|\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)||\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi-\eta)|d\eta.

There is
c\in(0,1)
such that

(3.22)

\{\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,\exists\xi\in V\backslash\{0\}\,\,\mbox{such that}\,\,|\xi-\eta|\leq c|\xi|\}\subseteq\operatorname{int}\widetilde{V}.

In the same way as in the proof of Proposition
3.7
(and Lemma
3.4
) we employ the Minkowski integral inequality together with (
3.21
) to infer
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(u_{j})\leq 2^{|r|}(2\pi)^{-n}(I_{1}+I_{2})
with

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V\\
|\xi|\geq|\eta|/c\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi-\eta)|^{2}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V\\
|\xi|<|\eta|/c\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi-\eta)|^{2}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta.

Again, as in the proof of Proposition
3.7
, (
3.22
) implies

(3.23)

\{\xi\in V-\{\eta\}\,|\,|\xi+\eta|\geq|\eta|/c\}\subseteq\widetilde{V}

which in turn yields that
I_{1}\leq\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\widetilde{\varphi},\widetilde{V}}(u)
. Since
B
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
(as it is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
), there are
C,l>0
such that
|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi)|\leq C\langle\xi\rangle^{l}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
u\in B
. This immediately implies that
I_{2}
is uniformly bounded for all
u\in B
. We conclude
\sup_{j\geq j^{\prime}}\sup_{u\in B}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(u_{j})<\infty
which completes the proof of the boundedness of
\{P_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))
.

It remains to prove that
P_{j}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))
. Since
P_{j}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U))
, it suffices to show that for every precompact subset
A
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
, every
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)\backslash\{0\}
and every closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
V\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
, satisfying
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V)\cap L=\emptyset
, it holds that
\sup_{u\in A}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(P_{j}u-u)\rightarrow 0
, as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Fix such
A
,
\varphi
and
V
. Let
\widetilde{\varphi}
,
\widetilde{V}
,
c
and
j^{\prime}
be as in the first part of the proof. Again, we denote
u_{j}:=\chi_{j}*(\widetilde{\varphi}u)\in\mathcal{D}(U)
,
j\geq j^{\prime}
, and point out that
\varphi P_{j}u=\varphi u_{j}
,
j\geq j^{\prime}
,
u\in A
. Similarly as above, we have
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(P_{j}u-u)\leq 2^{|r|}(2\pi)^{-n}(I_{1}+I_{2})
with

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V\\
|\xi|\geq|\eta|/c\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi-\eta)-1|^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi-\eta)|^{2}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in V\\
|\xi|<|\eta|/c\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi-\eta)-1|^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi-\eta)|^{2}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta.

There are
C_{1},l>0
such that
|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi)|\leq C_{1}\langle\xi\rangle^{l}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
u\in A
. Let
\varepsilon>0
be arbitrary but fixed. In view of Corollary
3.11
,
WF^{r}_{c}(A)\subseteq L
and hence Lemma
3.4
yields that there is
R>1
such that

(3.24)

\sup_{u\in A}\left(\int_{\widetilde{V},\,|\xi|\geq R}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}\leq\varepsilon/(2^{|r|+3}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}).

We choose
R
large enough so that we additionally have

\int_{|\eta|\geq Rc/(c+1)}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{2|r|+l+n}d\eta\leq\varepsilon\cdot\left(2^{|r|+3}C_{1}(1+1/c)^{|r|+l+n}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\right)^{-1}.

Since
\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}=\mathcal{F}\chi(\cdot/j)
and
\mathcal{F}\chi(0)=1
, dominated convergence implies that there is
j^{\prime}_{1}\geq j^{\prime}
such that

\|(\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}-1)\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|+l}\|_{L^{2}(B(0,R))}\leq\varepsilon/(C_{1}2^{|r|+2}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}),\quad j\geq j^{\prime}_{1}.

Let
j\geq j^{\prime}_{1}
and
u\in A
be arbitrary. To estimate
I_{1}
, we change variables in the inner integral and employ (
3.23
) to obtain (recall,
\|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=1
)

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{\widetilde{V}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi)-1|^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\left(\int_{\widetilde{V},\,|\xi|\geq R}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle{}\quad+C_{1}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\left(\int_{B(0,R)}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi)-1|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(|r|+l)}d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/2^{|r|+1}.

We estimate
I_{2}
as follows:

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{|\xi+\eta|<|\eta|/c}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi)-1|^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{|\xi|<(1+1/c)|\eta|}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi)-1|^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{|\xi|<\min\{(1+1/c)|\eta|,R\}}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi)-1|^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle{}\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\eta}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{R\leq|\xi|<(1+1/c)|\eta|}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi)-1|^{2}|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}u)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\left(\int_{B(0,R)}|\mathcal{F}\chi_{j}(\xi)-1|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(|r|+l)}d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle{}\quad+2C_{1}\int_{|\eta|\geq Rc/(c+1)}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{|r|}\left(\int_{R\leq|\xi|<(1+1/c)|\eta|}\frac{\langle\xi\rangle^{2(|r|+l+n)}}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2n}}d\xi\right)^{1/2}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2^{|r|+2}}+2C_{1}(1+1/c)^{|r|+l+n}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\int_{|\eta|\geq Rc/(c+1)}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta)|\langle\eta\rangle^{2|r|+l+n}d\eta\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2^{|r|+1}}.

Combining these estimates for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
we deduce
\sup_{u\in A}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,V}(P_{j}u-u)\leq\varepsilon
,
j\geq j^{\prime}_{1}
, which completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

3.2.
The dual of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)

Notice that (cf. Proposition
3.13
)

(3.25)

H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\quad\mbox{continuously and densely}

and hence the dual of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
is a space of distributions on
U
. In fact (
3.25
) gives

(3.26)

\mathcal{D}(U)\subseteq(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}\subseteq H^{-r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(U)\quad\mbox{continuously}.

Our goal is to identify the space
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
. For this purpose, given an open conic subset
W
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
, we define the space

\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U):=\{u\in H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(U)\,|\,WF(u)\subseteq W\}.

To introduce a locally convex topology on it, we consider the following auxiliary space. Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
and
K
a compact subset of
U
satisfying
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(L)\subseteq K
, where
\operatorname{pr}_{1}
stands for the projection on the first variable. We define

\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U):=\{u\in H^{r}_{K}(U)\,|\,WF(u)\subseteq L\}.

The distribution
u\in H^{r}_{K}(U)
belongs to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)
if and only if for every
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and every closed cone
\emptyset\neq V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V)\cap L=\emptyset
it holds that (cf.
[
28
, Section 8.1]
)

\mathfrak{q}_{\nu;\varphi,V}(u):=\sup_{\xi\in V}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi u)(\xi)|<\infty,\quad\mbox{for all}\,\,\nu>0.

We equip
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)
with the locally convex topology induced by the norm on
H^{r}_{K}(U)
together with all seminorms
\mathfrak{q}_{\nu;\varphi,V}
where
\varphi
,
V
and
\nu
are as above. Clearly,
\mathcal{D}_{K}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)\subseteq H^{r}_{K}(U)
continuously.

Proposition 3.14
.

Let
K\subset\subset U
and let
L
be a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
satisfying
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(L)\subseteq K
. Let
O_{j,k}
,
O^{\prime}_{j,k}
,
O^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}
,
V_{j,k}
,
V^{\prime}_{j,k}
,
V^{\prime\prime}_{j,k}
,
\phi_{j,k}
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}
be as in Proposition
3.7
. Then the mapping

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)\rightarrow H^{r}_{K}(U)\times(\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}}),\quad u\mapsto(u,\mathbf{f}_{u}),\,\,\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{u}(j,k):=\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u),\,j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},

is a well-defined topological imbedding with closed image. Consequently,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)
is a reflexive Fréchet space.

Proof.

Clearly, the map is injective. The continuity follows from the estimate

\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\phi}_{j,k}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,k}u))\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha}\mathfrak{q}_{\nu;\phi_{j,k,\beta},V^{\prime}_{j,k}}(u),\quad\mbox{where}\quad\phi_{j,k,\beta}(x)=x^{\beta}\phi_{j,k}(x).

The fact that the map is a topological imbedding with closed image can be shown in the same way as in the proof of Proposition
3.7
(to bound the appropriate variant of
I_{2}
, one employs the inequality
|\mathcal{F}(\psi u)(\xi)|\leq C\langle\xi\rangle^{|r|}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|r|}\mathcal{F}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|u\|_{H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
,
u\in H^{r}_{K}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
). From this, we immediately deduce that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)
is a reflexive Fréchet space since the codomain of the map is a countable topological product of reflexive Fréchet spaces.
∎

Notice that

(3.27)

\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)\,\,\mbox{continuously if}\,\,L\subseteq\widetilde{L}\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,K\subseteq\widetilde{K}.

Given an open conic subset
W
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
, denote by
\mathfrak{W}
the set of all pairs
(L,K)
, where
K
is a compact subset of
U
and
L
a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
satisfying
L\subseteq W
and
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(L)\subseteq K
. Then
(\mathfrak{W},\leq)
becomes a directed set with order
(L,K)\leq(\widetilde{L},\widetilde{K})
if
L\subseteq\widetilde{L}
and
K\subseteq\widetilde{K}
. Notice that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)=\bigcup_{(L,K)\in\mathfrak{W}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)
. We define the locally convex topology on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)
by

\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
(L,K)\in\mathfrak{W}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U),

where the linking mappings in the inductive limit are the continuous inclusions (
3.27
); notice that the inductive limit topology on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)
is indeed Hausdorff since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)\subseteq H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(U)
continuously. For any sequence
(L_{j},K_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\subseteq\mathfrak{W}
which satisfies

(3.28)

L_{j}\subseteq\operatorname{int}L_{j+1},\,\,K_{j}\subseteq\operatorname{int}K_{j+1},\,\,\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}L_{j}=W,\,\,\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}K_{j}=U,

it holds that

\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
j\rightarrow\infty\end{subarray}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L_{j};K_{j}}(U)\quad\mbox{topologically}

(one can always find such sequence, cf.
[
8
, p. 1354]
; when
W=\emptyset
, we can take
L_{j}=\emptyset
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
). Consequently,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)
is an
(LF)
-space and thus it is barrelled and bornological. Furthermore, we have the following continuous inclusions:

(3.29)

\mathcal{D}(U)\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)\subseteq H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(U)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{E}^{\prime r_{1}}_{W_{1}}(U)\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime r_{2}}_{W_{2}}(U),\,r_{1}\geq r_{2},\,W_{1}\subseteq W_{2},

Remark 3.15
.

Since the continuous inclusion
\mathcal{D}_{K}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{\emptyset;K}(U)
is a bijection, the open mapping theorem for Fréchet spaces yields that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{\emptyset;K}(U)=\mathcal{D}_{K}
topologically. Consequently,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{\emptyset}(U)=\mathcal{D}(U)
topologically.

Similarly, since the continuous inclusion
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{K\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\});K}(U)\subseteq H^{r}_{K}(U)
is a bijection, the open mapping theorem for Fréchet spaces shows that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{K\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\});K}(U)=H^{r}_{K}(U)
topologically. Consequently,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})}(U)=H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(U)
topologically.

Remark 3.16
.

The bilinear map
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)\times\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)
,
(\chi,u)\mapsto\chi u
, is well-defined and continuous. Indeed, the fact that it is well-defined is trivial and a standard closed graph argument implies that it is separately continuous since both spaces are Fréchet. Now,
[
36
, Theorem 1, p. 158]
verifies its continuity. Consequently,
[
36
, Theorem 5, p. 159]
shows that
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)\times\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)
,
(\chi,u)\mapsto\chi u
, is hypocontinuous.

The space
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)
satisfies similar approximation result to Proposition
3.13
for
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
.

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
\chi_{j}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, be as in Proposition
3.13
. For every
K,\widetilde{K}\subseteq U
satisfying
K\subset\subset\operatorname{int}\widetilde{K}
and
\widetilde{K}\subset\subset U
there is
j_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
\chi_{j}*u\in\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{K}}
for all
u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)
,
j\geq j_{0}
and
L
a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
satisfying
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(L)\subseteq K
. Furthermore
\chi_{j}*u\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;\widetilde{K}}(U)
for all
u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)
.

If
P_{j}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, are the operators from Proposition
3.13
, then
P_{j}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U))
for any open conic subset
W
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
. In particular,
\mathcal{D}(U)
is sequentially dense in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)
.

Proof.

The proof of the first part is similar to the proof of Proposition
3.13
and we omit it. This implies
P_{j}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U))
and, since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)
is barrelled, the Banach-Steinhaus theorem
[
50
, Theorem 4.5, p. 85]
yields that the convergence holds in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U))
.
∎

We need the following preparatory lemma for the result on the duality (see the preliminaries for the meaning of
\check{L})
.

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
\emptyset\neq\widetilde{K}\subset\subset U
and let
L
and
\widetilde{L}
be closed conic subsets of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
satisfying
\widetilde{L}\subseteq\check{L}^{c}
and
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\widetilde{L})\subseteq\widetilde{K}
.

(i)

There are open subsets
U_{1},\ldots,U_{k}
of
U
, closed cones
V_{1},\ldots,V_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\phi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(U_{j})
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, which satisfy the following conditions:

(3.30)

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}\subseteq\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}U_{j},\quad(U_{j}\times(-V_{j}))\cap L=\emptyset\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,(U_{j}\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V_{j}))\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset,\,\,j=1,\ldots,k,

(3.31)

\displaystyle\phi_{1}^{2}+\ldots+\phi_{k}^{2}=1\,\,\mbox{on a neighbourhood of}\,\,\widetilde{K}.

(ii)

If
U_{j}
,
V_{j}
and
\phi_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, are as in
(i)
, then
\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi_{j}v)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, for all
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
and
v\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)
, and, for each
v\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)
, the linear functional

(3.32)

\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathbb{C},\quad u\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\xi)\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi_{j}v)(\xi)d\xi,

is continuous. Furthermore, when
v
varies in a bounded subset of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)
, (
3.32
) becomes an equicontinuous subset of
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}
.

Proof.

To show
(i)
, assume first
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. For
x_{0}\in\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\widetilde{L})
, the compactness of the unit sphere implies that there are an open set
U^{\prime}\subseteq U
that contains
x_{0}
and an open cone
V^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
(U^{\prime}\times(-\overline{V^{\prime}}))\cap L=\emptyset
and
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,(x_{0},\xi)\in\widetilde{L}\}\subseteq V^{\prime}
. We claim that there is an open neighbourhood
U_{0}\subseteq U^{\prime}
of
x_{0}
such that
(U_{0}\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V^{\prime}))\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
. To verify that this is true, assume the contrary. Then there are
x^{(j)}\in U^{\prime}
and
\eta^{(j)}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V^{\prime})
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, such that
x^{(j)}\rightarrow x_{0}
and
(x^{(j)},\eta^{(j)})\in\widetilde{L}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. In view of the compactness of the unit sphere, there is a subsequence
(\eta^{(j_{k})})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
which converges to some
\eta_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V^{\prime})
. Hence
(x_{0},\eta_{0})\in\widetilde{L}
and thus
\eta_{0}\in V^{\prime}
which is a contradiction. We showed that there are an open neighbourhood
U_{0}\subseteq U
of
x_{0}
and a closed cone
V_{0}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

(3.33)

(U_{0}\times(-V_{0}))\cap L=\emptyset\quad\mbox{and}\quad(U_{0}\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V_{0}))\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset.

When
x_{0}\in\widetilde{K}\backslash\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\widetilde{L})
, we can take
V_{0}:=\{0\}
and
U_{0}:=U\backslash\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\widetilde{L})
for (
3.33
) to be satisfied. Now, a compactness argument verifies the existence of
U_{j}
and
V_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, as in
(i)
which satisfy (
3.30
). The existence of
\phi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(U_{j})
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, which satisfy (
3.31
) is straightforward. In the case when
L^{c}=\emptyset
, (
3.30
) is satisfied by taking
k=1
,
U_{1}\subseteq U
an open neighbourhood of
\widetilde{K}
and
V_{1}=\{0\}
; in this case, for (
3.31
), we take
\phi_{1}\in\mathcal{D}(U_{1})
which equals
1
on a neighbourhood of
\widetilde{K}
.

To prove
(ii)
, let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)
and let
U_{j}
,
V_{j}
and
\phi_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, be as in
(i)
. For
v\in B
and
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
, we estimate as follows (cf. (
3.30
)):

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\xi)||\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi_{j}v)(\xi)|d\xi

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-V_{j}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\xi)||\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}v)(-\xi)|d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash(-V_{j})}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\xi)||\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}v)(-\xi)|d\xi

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{p}_{r;\phi_{j},-V_{j}}(u)\sup_{v\in B}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}v)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\sup_{v\in B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash(-V_{j})}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\xi)||\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}v)(-\xi)|d\xi.

We claim that

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow[0,\infty),\quad f\mapsto\sup_{v\in B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash(-V_{j})}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}f)(\xi)||\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}v)(-\xi)|d\xi,

is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. This can be proven by showing that it is bounded on bounded subsets on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
by employing similar technique as in the prove of the Claim in Proposition
3.7
(
\sup_{v\in B}\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V_{j}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}v)(\xi)|\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}<\infty
, for all
\nu>0
, in view of (
3.30
) and the boundedness of
B
); whence, it is continuous since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
is bornological. Now, all claims in
(ii)
immediately follow from the above estimate.
∎

Remark 3.19
.

Of course, we can always take
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{k}
to be nonnegative.

Now, we are ready to show that the strong dual of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
is
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)
. In the first part of the proof, we employ some of the ideas used in
[
21
, Proposition 7.6, p. 80]
and
[
8
, Lemma 3 and Proposition 7]
where the dual of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(U)
was identified. However, the ideas from these results can not be applied in the second part of the proof: here we employ pseudo-differential operator techniques and
L^{2}
-estimates.

Theorem 3.20
.

Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
and
r\in\mathbb{R}
.

(i)

The bilinear map
\mathcal{D}(U)\times\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
(\varphi,\psi)\mapsto\langle\varphi,\psi\rangle=\int_{U}\varphi(x)\psi(x)dx
, uniquely extends to a separately continuous bilinear mapping
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, given by

(3.34)

\langle v,u\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\xi)\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi_{j}v)(\xi)d\xi,\quad u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U),\,v\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U),

with
\phi_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, as in Lemma
3.18

(i)
.

(ii)

It holds that
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)
topologically and the duality
\langle\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U),\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rangle
is given by (
3.34
).

Proof.

We first address
(i)
. In view of Lemma
3.18

(ii)
and the density of
\mathcal{D}(U)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
, the right-hand side in (
3.34
) does not depend on any of the choices made in Lemma
3.18

(i)
, i.e. on
\widetilde{L}
,
\widetilde{K}
,
U_{j}
,
V_{j}
and
\phi_{j}
; whence
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is well-defined. Notice that when
v\in\mathcal{D}(U)
, the right-hand side in (
3.34
) is exactly the duality
\langle\mathcal{D}(U),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rangle
and thus coincides with
\int_{U}v(x)u(x)dx
when
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
. Lemma
3.18

(ii)
verifies the continuity of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
u\mapsto\langle v,u\rangle
, for each fixed
v
. When
u
is fixed, Lemma
3.18

(ii)
shows that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
v\mapsto\langle v,u\rangle
, maps bounded sets into bounded sets and hence it is continuous since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)
is Fréchet space (hence bornological). This immediately implies the continuity of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
v\mapsto\langle v,u\rangle
, when
u
is fixed and the proof of
(i)
is complete.

We turn our attention to
(ii)
. In view of (
3.29
) and Proposition
3.17
, we infer

(3.35)

H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)\subseteq(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b}\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\quad\mbox{continuously}.

We are going to show

(3.36)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}\,\,\mbox{algebraically and}

(3.37)

\displaystyle\mbox{the identity mappings}\,\,(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\rightarrow(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}\,\,\mbox{are continuous.}

This would immediately give the continuous inclusions
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\rightarrow(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}\rightarrow((\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b})^{\prime}_{b}
, which, in view of the fact that the evaluation map into the strong bidual
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\rightarrow((\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b})^{\prime}_{b}
is a topological imbedding since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)
is barrelled, would yield that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\rightarrow(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
is a topological imbedding
7
7

7

Here we employed the fact that if the continuous injections between topological spaces
f:X_{1}\rightarrow X_{2}
and
g:X_{2}\rightarrow X_{3}
are such that
g\circ f:X_{1}\rightarrow X_{3}
is a topological imbedding, then so is
f
.
and hence
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
topologically. Thus we need to show (
3.36
) and (
3.37
).

STEP 1:
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}
and the continuity of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\rightarrow(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
.
The validity of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\subseteq(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
immediately follows from
(i)
. Its continuity is a consequence of Lemma
3.18

(ii)
; indeed, the latter shows that the inclusion
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)\subseteq(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
maps bounded sets into bounded sets and hence it is continuous (as
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)
is Fréchet) which, in turn, shows the continuity of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)\subseteq(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
. To verify that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}
as sets, let
v\in(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}
. In view of (
3.26
), it suffices to show
WF(v)\subseteq\check{L}^{c}
. There is
C>0
, a bounded subset
B_{0}
of
\mathcal{D}(U)
and
\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{k}\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and closed cones
V_{1},\ldots,V_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying
(\operatorname{supp}\psi_{j}\times V_{j})\cap L=\emptyset
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, such that

|\langle v,u\rangle|\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi_{j},V_{j}}(u)+C\sup_{\varphi\in B_{0}}|\langle u,\varphi\rangle|,\quad u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U).

We are going to show that
WF(v)\subseteq\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\operatorname{supp}\psi_{j}\times(-V_{j}\backslash\{0\})
; as the latter is a subset of
\check{L}^{c}
, this will complete the proof of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}
. Let
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and the closed cone
V
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
be such that
(\operatorname{supp}\psi\times V)\cap(\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\operatorname{supp}\psi_{j}\times(-V_{j}\backslash\{0\}))=\emptyset
. For
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, define
\phi_{\xi}:=e^{-i\xi\,\cdot}\psi\in\mathcal{D}(U)
. Since
\mathcal{F}(\psi v)(\xi)=\langle v,\phi_{\xi}\rangle
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, for
\nu>0
, we infer

\sup_{\xi\in V}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}|\mathcal{F}(\psi v)(\xi)|\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sup_{\xi\in V}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi_{j},V_{j}}(\phi_{\xi})+C\sup_{\xi\in V}\sup_{\varphi\in B_{0}}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}|\langle\phi_{\xi},\varphi\rangle|.

Assume that
V\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
. As
\langle\phi_{\xi},\varphi\rangle=\mathcal{F}(\psi\varphi)(\xi)
, we infer
\sup_{\xi\in V}\sup_{\varphi\in B_{0}}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}|\langle\phi_{\xi},\varphi\rangle|<\infty
. We only need to bound
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi_{j},V_{j}}(\phi_{\xi})
when
V_{j}\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
. Notice that it is zero if
\operatorname{supp}\psi\cap\operatorname{supp}\psi_{j}=\emptyset
. When
\operatorname{supp}\psi\cap\operatorname{supp}\psi_{j}\neq\emptyset
, we have
V\cap(-V_{j}\backslash\{0\})=\emptyset
and thus there is
c>0
such that
|\xi/|\xi|-\eta/|\eta||>c
,
\xi\in V\backslash\{0\}
,
\eta\in-V_{j}\backslash\{0\}
. For such
\xi
and
\eta
, we have

|\xi-\eta|\geq\left|\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}-\frac{\eta}{|\eta|}\right||\xi|-\left|\frac{\eta}{|\eta|}-\frac{\eta}{|\xi|}\right||\xi|>c|\xi|-||\xi|-|\eta||\geq c|\xi|-|\xi-\eta|,

and thus
|\xi-\eta|>c|\xi|/2
; analogously,
|\xi-\eta|>c|\eta|/2
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in V}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi_{j},V_{j}}=\sup_{\xi\in V}\left(\int_{-V_{j}}\langle\xi\rangle^{2\nu}\langle\eta\rangle^{2r}|\mathcal{F}(\psi\psi_{j})(\xi-\eta)|^{2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}<\infty

(as
\mathcal{F}(\psi\psi_{j})\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
). We deduce
\sup_{\xi\in V}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}|\mathcal{F}(\psi v)(\xi)|<\infty
. Since this trivially holds in the case when
V\backslash\{0\}=\emptyset
, the proof of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U)=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U))^{\prime}
is complete.

STEP 2:
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
and the continuity of
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
.
Since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
is semi-reflexive (cf. Corollary
3.10
), STEP 1 verifies the algebraic inclusion
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\subseteq(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}
. It remains to show
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b}\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
continuously. This is trivial when
L^{c}=\emptyset
. Assume that
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. We estimate the seminorm
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi,V}
of the distributions in
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}
, where
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(U)\backslash\{0\}
and
V
is a closed cone in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
(\operatorname{supp}\psi\times V)\cap L=\emptyset
and
V\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
. Pick an open cone
V^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
V\backslash\{0\}\subseteq V^{\prime}
and
(\operatorname{supp}\psi\times\overline{V^{\prime}})\cap L=\emptyset
. We are going to show that for all
u\in(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}
, the seminorm
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi,V^{\prime}}(u)
is bounded from above by a continuous seminorm on
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
of
v
. Since
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi,V}\leq\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi,V^{\prime}}
, this would imply
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b}\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
continuously; in view of (
3.35
) for every continuous seminorm
\mathfrak{p}
on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
,
\mathfrak{p}(u)
is bounded by a continuous seminorm on
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
of
u
.

Denoting
\widetilde{K}:=\operatorname{supp}\psi
and
\widetilde{L}:=\operatorname{supp}\psi\times(-\overline{V^{\prime}}\backslash\{0\})
, we infer
\widetilde{L}\subseteq\check{L}^{c}
and
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\widetilde{L})=\widetilde{K}
. Since
V^{\prime}
is open, for
u\in(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}
, we have

(3.38)

\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi,V^{\prime}}(u)=\sup\left\{\left|\int_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}(\psi u)(\xi)\chi(\xi)d\xi\right|\,\Bigg|\,\chi\in\mathcal{D}(V^{\prime}),\,\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi\|_{L^{2}(V^{\prime})}\leq 1\right\}.

For
\chi\in\mathcal{D}(V^{\prime})
satisfying
\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi\|_{L^{2}(V^{\prime})}\leq 1
, we infer

(3.39)

\left|\int_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}(\psi u)(\xi)\chi(\xi)d\xi\right|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(\psi u)(\xi)\chi(\xi)d\xi\right|=|\langle u,\psi\mathcal{F}\chi\rangle|.

Notice that
\psi\mathcal{F}\chi\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)
. We claim that
\widetilde{B}:=\{\psi\mathcal{F}\chi\,|\,\chi\in\mathcal{D}(V^{\prime}),\,\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi\|_{L^{2}(V^{\prime})}\leq 1\}
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(U)
. We have

\displaystyle\|\psi\mathcal{F}\chi\|_{H^{-r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}(\psi\mathcal{F}\chi)(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{-2r}d\xi=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\mathcal{F}\left(\langle D\rangle^{-r}(\psi\langle D\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi))\right)(\xi)\right|^{2}d\xi

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{n}\left\|\langle D\rangle^{-r}(\psi\langle D\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi))\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2}.

Since
\langle D\rangle^{-r}\psi\langle D\rangle^{r}
is a pseudo-differential operator with symbol in
S^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
, it is continuous on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Hence,

\|\psi\mathcal{F}\chi\|_{H^{-r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C^{\prime}\|\mathcal{F}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C^{\prime},\quad\chi\in\mathcal{D}(V^{\prime}),\,\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi\|_{L^{2}(V^{\prime})}\leq 1.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(U)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
V_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
V_{0}\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
, be such that
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V_{0})\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
. If
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\cap\operatorname{supp}\psi=\emptyset
, then
\mathfrak{q}_{\nu;\varphi;V_{0}}(\widetilde{B})=0
, for all
\nu>0
. Assume that
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\cap\operatorname{supp}\psi\neq\emptyset
. Then
V_{0}\cap(-\overline{V^{\prime}}\backslash\{0\})=\emptyset
. Similarly as before, this implies that there is
c^{\prime}>0
such that
|\xi-\eta|>c^{\prime}|\xi|
and
|\xi-\eta|>c^{\prime}|\eta|
,
\xi\in V_{0}\backslash\{0\}
,
\eta\in-\overline{V^{\prime}}\backslash\{0\}
. For
\nu>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in V_{0}}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi\psi\mathcal{F}\chi)(\xi)|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\xi\in V_{0}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}\mathcal{F}(\varphi\psi)(\xi-\eta)\chi(-\eta)d\eta\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\int_{-V^{\prime}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-r-n}|\chi(-\eta)|d\eta\leq C_{2},

for all
\chi\in\mathcal{D}(V^{\prime})
satisfying
\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi\|_{L^{2}(V^{\prime})}\leq 1
. We conclude that
\widetilde{B}
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{K}}(U)
. In view of (
3.38
) and (
3.39
), we deduce that
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\psi,V^{\prime}}(u)=\sup_{\phi\in\widetilde{B}}|\langle u,\phi\rangle|
. This completes the proof of STEP 2 since the right-hand side is a continuous seminorm on
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U))^{\prime}_{b}
.

The fact that the duality
\langle\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(U),\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rangle
is given by (
3.34
) follows from
(i)
. The proof of the theorem is complete.
∎

3.3.
Pullback by smooth maps

Following Hörmander
[
28
, Section 8.2]
,
[
26
, Subsection 2.5]
, given a smooth map
f:M\rightarrow N
between the manifolds
M
and
N
, we denote by
\mathcal{N}_{f}
the following conic subset of
T^{*}N
:

(3.40)

\mathcal{N}_{f}:=\{(f(p),\eta)\in T^{*}N\,|\,\eta\in T^{*}_{f(p)}N,\,df^{*}_{p}\eta=0\in T^{*}_{p}M\}.

If
L
is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}N\backslash 0
which satisfies
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
, then

(3.41)

f^{*}L:=\{(p,df^{*}_{p}\eta)\in T^{*}M\,|\,(f(p),\eta)\in L\}

is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Given a smooth map
g:\widetilde{M}\rightarrow M
and diffeomorphisms
\iota_{1}:M\rightarrow\widetilde{M}
and
\iota_{2}:N\rightarrow\widetilde{N}
, it is straightforward to check that

(3.42)

(f\circ g)^{*}L=g^{*}f^{*}L,\quad\mathcal{N}_{\iota_{2}\circ f}=(\iota^{-1}_{2})^{*}\mathcal{N}_{f}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{N}_{f\circ\iota^{-1}_{1}}=\mathcal{N}_{f}.

When
M
and
N
are two open subsets
O
and
U
of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
\mathbb{R}^{n}
respectively, employing the canonical identifications, (
3.40
) and (
3.41
) boil down to

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{f}

\displaystyle=\{(f(x),\eta)\in U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,x\in O,\,{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x)\eta=0\},

\displaystyle f^{*}L

\displaystyle=\{(x,{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x)\eta)\in O\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\,|\,(f(x),\eta)\in L\}.

We are now ready to state and prove the result on the pullback by smooth maps.

Theorem 3.21
.

Let
O
and
U
be open subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
\mathbb{R}^{n}
respectively, let
f:O\rightarrow U
be a smooth map and let
L
be a closed conic subset of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
satisfying
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
. The pullback
f^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(O)
,
f^{*}(u)=u\circ f
, uniquely extends to a well defined and continuous mapping
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(O)
when
r_{2}-r_{1}>n/2
and
r_{2}>n/2
.

If
f
has constant rank
k\geq 1
, then this is valid when
r_{2}-r_{1}\geq(n-k)/2
and
r_{2}>(n-k)/2
. When
f
is a submersion,
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(O)
is well-defined and continuous even when
r_{2}\geq r_{1}
. Consequently, if
f
is a diffeomorphism, then
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{f^{*}L}(O)
is a topological isomorphism for each
r\in\mathbb{R}
.

Remark 3.22
.

We consider the trivial case when
f
has constant rank
0
in Remark
3.23
below. Before we prove the theorem, we point out the following:

(i)
When
L=\emptyset
the theorem states that the pullback is well-defined and continuous map
f^{*}:H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)\rightarrow H^{r_{1}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(O)
with
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
as in the theorem. If
m<n
,
O=U\cap\mathbb{R}^{m}
viewed as an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
f:O\rightarrow U
the canonical imbedding, then
f^{*}
is just restriction and the theorem claims that
f^{*}:H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U)\rightarrow H^{r_{1}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(O)
is well-defined and continuous when
r_{2}\geq r_{1}+(n-m)/2
and
r_{2}>(n-m)/2
. Thus, we can view this case as a local version of the Sobolev imbedding theorem for restrictions to lower dimensional hyperplanes
[
1
, Theorem 4.12, p. 85]
; this case is also shown in
[
29
, Appendix B, p. 476]
.

(ii)
In the constant rank case, the theorem can not be much improved. In Appendix
A
we give examples of maps with constant rank where the theorem fails if
r_{2}<(n-k)/2
or
r_{2}-r_{1}<(n-k)/2
. The only open problem is the case when
r_{2}=(n-k)/2
and
r_{2}-r_{1}\geq(n-k)/2
which is equivalent to
r_{2}=(n-k)/2
and
r_{1}\leq 0
.

Proof of Theorem
3.21
.

Throughout the proof, for
y\in U
, we denote
L_{y}:=\{\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}\,|\,(y,\eta)\in L\}
. Notice that
L_{y}
is a closed cone in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
(which may be empty!). We employ analogous notations for closed conic subsets of
O\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
.

We first make the following observations for a general smooth map
f:O\rightarrow U
satisfying
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
. Let
x_{0}\in O
be arbitrary but fixed and set
y_{0}:=f(x_{0})\in U
. Let
G
be an open cone in
\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\}
such that
(f^{*}L)_{x_{0}}={}^{t}f^{\prime}(x_{0})L_{y_{0}}\subseteq G
. The compactness of
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\cap L_{y_{0}}
implies that there are closed cones
V^{\prime}
and
V
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
such that
L_{y_{0}}\subseteq\operatorname{int}V^{\prime}\subseteq V^{\prime}\subseteq\operatorname{int}V
and
{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x_{0})V\subseteq G
; consequently,
{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x_{0})\eta\neq 0
,
\eta\in V
(when
f^{\prime}(x_{0})=0
the condition
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
implies
L_{y_{0}}=\emptyset
and the above is satisfied with
V=V^{\prime}=\emptyset
). There is an open neighbourhood
U_{0}\subseteq U
of
y_{0}
such that
\bigcup_{y\in U_{0}}L_{y}\subseteq\operatorname{int}V^{\prime}
. To see that this is true, assume the contrary. Then there is a sequence
(y^{(j)})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
which tends to
y_{0}
such that for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
there is
\eta^{(j)}\in L_{y^{(j)}}\backslash\operatorname{int}V^{\prime}
and
|\eta^{(j)}|=1
. The compactness of
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
implies that there is a subsequence
(\eta^{(j_{k})})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
which converges to some
\eta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\backslash\operatorname{int}V^{\prime}
. Since
(y^{(j_{k})},\eta^{(j_{k})})\in L
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we infer
\eta\in L_{y_{0}}\backslash\operatorname{int}V^{\prime}
which is a contradiction since the latter set is empty. Consequently, the open neighbourhood
U_{0}
exists. The continuity of
(x,\eta)\mapsto{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x)\eta
together with the compactness of
V\cap\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
yields that there is a relatively compact open neighbourhood
O_{0}
of
x_{0}
such that
\overline{O_{0}}\subseteq O
and
{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x)\eta\in G
,
x\in\overline{O_{0}}
,
\eta\in V
. We take
O_{0}
small enough so that
f(\overline{O_{0}})\subseteq U_{0}
. The fact
{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x)\eta\in G
,
x\in\overline{O_{0}}
,
\eta\in V
, together with the continuity of the function
(x,\xi,\eta)\mapsto{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x)\eta-\xi
implies that there is
\varepsilon>0
such that
|{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x)\eta-\xi|\geq\varepsilon
on the compact set
\overline{O_{0}}\times\{(\xi,\eta)\in(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash G)\times(V\cup\{0\})\,|\,|\xi|+|\eta|=1\}
. Consequently

(3.43)

|{}^{t}f(x)\eta-\xi|\geq\varepsilon(|\xi|+|\eta|),\quad x\in\overline{O_{0}},\,\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash G,\,\eta\in V\cup\{0\}.

Given
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
, we define

\displaystyle I_{\varphi}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{C},\quad I_{\varphi}(\eta):=\int_{O}e^{if(x)\eta}\varphi(x)dx,

\displaystyle\widetilde{I}_{\varphi}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{C},\quad\widetilde{I}_{\varphi}(\xi,\eta):=\int_{O}e^{i(f(x)\eta-x\xi)}\varphi(x)dx.

Clearly
I_{\varphi}\in\mathcal{D}_{L^{\infty}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
\widetilde{I}_{\varphi}\in\mathcal{D}_{L^{\infty}}(\mathbb{R}^{m+n})
and
\widetilde{I}_{\varphi}(0,\eta)=I_{\varphi}(\eta)
,
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For
u\in\mathcal{D}(U)
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
it holds that

\displaystyle\langle f^{*}u,\varphi\rangle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{O}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}e^{if(x)\eta}\mathcal{F}u(\eta)\varphi(x)d\eta dx=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{F}u(\eta)I_{\varphi}(\eta)d\eta.

CASE 1:
f
is smooth and satisfies
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
.
We show that
f^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(O)
uniquely extends to a well-defined continuous mapping
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(O)
, when
r_{2}-r_{1}>n/2
and
r_{2}>n/2
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
\emptyset\neq G_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
be such that
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times G_{1})\cap f^{*}L=\emptyset
(when
f^{*}L=\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\}
, we take
G_{1}=\{0\}
). Set
G:=\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash G_{1}
. Then
G
is an open cone in
\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\}
and
{}^{t}f^{\prime}(x)L_{f(x)}\subseteq G
,
x\in\operatorname{supp}\varphi
. We apply the above construction for this
G
and each
x\in\operatorname{supp}\varphi
to obtain the open neighbourhoods
O_{x}
and
U_{x}
of
x
and
f(x)
respectively having the above properties. As
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
is compact there are finitely many such
O_{j}

j=1,\ldots,l
, whose union covers
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
. We denote by
U_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,l
, the corresponding subsets of
U
and by
V^{\prime}_{j}
and
V_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,l
, the corresponding closed cones in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
from the above construction. Let
\psi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{j})
,
0\leq\psi_{j}\leq 1
,
j=1,\ldots,l
, be such that
\sum_{j=1}^{l}\psi_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
. Pick
\phi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(U_{j})
such that
\phi_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
f(\overline{O_{j}})
,
j=1,\ldots,l
. For
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
, we infer

(3.44)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\varphi f^{*}u)(\xi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{l}\langle f^{*}(\phi_{j}u),e^{-i\,\cdot\,\xi}\psi_{j}\varphi\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)\widetilde{I}_{\psi_{j}\varphi}(\xi,\eta)d\eta=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{l}(I_{1;j}(\xi)+I_{2;j}(\xi)),

with

I_{1;j}(\xi):=\int_{V_{j}}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)\widetilde{I}_{\psi_{j}\varphi}(\xi,\eta)d\eta,\quad I_{2;j}(\xi):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V_{j}}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)\widetilde{I}_{\psi_{j}\varphi}(\xi,\eta)d\eta.

Hence,

(3.45)

\mathfrak{p}_{r_{1};\varphi,G_{1}}(f^{*}u)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{l}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{1}}I_{1;j}\|_{L^{2}(G_{1})}+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{1}}I_{2;j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}.

In view of (
3.43
), the stationary phase method
[
28
, Theorem 7.7.1, p. 216]
verifies that for every
N>0
there is
C_{N}>0
such that
|\widetilde{I}_{\psi_{j}\varphi}(\xi,\eta)|\leq C_{N}(1+|\xi|+|\eta|)^{-N}
,
\xi\in G_{1}
,
\eta\in V_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,l
. Hence, employing the same technique as in the proof of the Claim in the proof of Proposition
3.7
, one shows that

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow[0,\infty),\,u\mapsto\left(\int_{G_{1}}\left(\int_{V_{j}}\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)||\widetilde{I}_{\psi_{j}\varphi}(\xi,\eta)|d\eta\right)^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2},\,\,j=1,\ldots,l,

are continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Hence, the first sum in (
3.45
) is bounded by a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
of
u
. To estimate the second sum in (
3.45
), first notice that

|I_{2;j}(\xi)|\leq\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{2}}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j})}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V_{j}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}}|\widetilde{I}_{\psi_{j}\varphi}(\xi,\eta)|^{2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}

and hence,

(3.46)

\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{1}}I_{2;j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi_{j},\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}}(u)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r_{1}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}}|\widetilde{I}_{\psi_{j}\varphi}(\xi,\eta)|^{2}d\xi d\eta\right)^{1/2}.

Notice that
\widetilde{I}_{\psi_{j}\varphi}(\xi,\eta)=\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)(\xi)
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. If
r_{1}\leq 0
then the last integral in (
3.46
) is bounded by

\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}}\|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}d\eta\right)^{1/2}=(2\pi)^{m/2}\|\psi_{j}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r_{2}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

which is finite since
r_{2}>n/2
. Assume now that
r_{1}>0
. As standard, denote
\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor=\max\{k\in\mathbb{Z}\,|\,k\leq r_{1}\}
and pick
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
so that
r_{1}\leq\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k/l
and
r_{2}-\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor-k/l>n/2
. We estimate as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\xi\rangle^{2lr_{1}}

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)(\xi)|^{2l}

\displaystyle\leq(1+|\xi|^{2})^{l\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k}|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)(\xi)|^{2l}

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)(\xi)|^{2(l-1)}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq l\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k}\frac{(l\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k)!}{(l\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k-|\alpha|)!\alpha!}|\mathcal{F}(\partial^{\alpha}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi))(\xi)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{1}|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)(\xi)|^{2(l-1)}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq l\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k}\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\alpha|}\langle\eta\rangle^{2|\beta|}|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\varphi_{\alpha,\beta,j})(\xi)|^{2},

for some
\varphi_{\alpha,\beta,j}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{j})
. Hence

\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)(\xi)|\\
\leq C^{\prime 1/(2l)}_{1}\langle\eta\rangle^{\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k/l}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq l\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k}\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\alpha|}|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\varphi_{\alpha,\beta,j})(\xi)|^{1/l}|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)(\xi)|^{(l-1)/l}.

We employ the Hölder inequality with
p=l
and
q=l/(l-1)
to infer

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{1}}\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime 1/(2l)}_{1}\langle\eta\rangle^{\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k/l}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq l\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k}\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\alpha|}\|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\varphi_{\alpha,\beta,j})\|^{1/l}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\|\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)\|^{(l-1)/l}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{m/2}C^{\prime 1/(2l)}_{1}\langle\eta\rangle^{\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k/l}\|\psi_{j}\varphi\|^{(l-1)/l}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq l\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k}\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\alpha|}\|\varphi_{\alpha,\beta,j}\|^{1/l}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq C^{\prime}_{2}\langle\eta\rangle^{\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+k/l}

(with the obvious modifications when
l=1
and thus
q=\infty
). Consequently, the last integral in (
3.46
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{1}}\mathcal{F}(e^{if(\cdot)\eta}\psi_{j}\varphi)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}d\eta\right)^{1/2}\leq C^{\prime}_{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}+2\lfloor r_{1}\rfloor+2k/l}d\eta\right)^{1/2}<\infty.

Employing these bounds in (
3.46
), the inequality (
3.45
) immediately gives

\mathfrak{p}_{r_{1};\varphi,G_{1}}(f^{*}u)\leq\mathfrak{p}(u)+C^{\prime}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi_{j},\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}}(u),\quad u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U),

where
\mathfrak{p}
is a continuous seminorm of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
; the summands are continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(U)
since
(U_{j}\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}))\cap L=\emptyset
,
j=1,\ldots,l
.

Next we show similar bounds for
\mathfrak{p}(f^{*}u)
where
\mathfrak{p}
is an arbitrary continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O)
. Once we show this, we can deduce that
f^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(O)
is continuous when
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
and
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(O)
are equipped with the topologies induced by
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(U)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(O)
respectively, which, in view of Proposition
3.13
, yields that
f^{*}
uniquely extends to a well-defined and continuous map
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(O)
, when
r_{2}-r_{1}>n/2
and
r_{2}>n/2
. Let
\mathfrak{p}
be a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O)
; without loss in generality we can assume that
\mathfrak{p}=\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\cdot,\chi\rangle|
for some bounded subset
B
of
\mathcal{D}(O)
. There exists
K\subset\subset O
such that
B
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}_{K}
. In the same way as above, we find open sets
O_{j}\subseteq O
,
j=1,\ldots,l
, with compact closures in
O
which cover
K
and corresponding open set
U_{j}\subseteq U
and closed cones
V^{\prime}_{j}
,
V_{j}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
,
j=1,\ldots,l
(apply the above construction with
G=\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\}
). As above,
\psi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{j})
,
0\leq\psi_{j}\leq 1
,
j=1,\ldots,l
, denotes a partition of unity on a neighbourhood of
K
, and
\phi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(U_{j})
is such that
\phi_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
f(\overline{O_{j}})
,
j=1,\ldots,l
. For
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
and
\chi\in B
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle f^{*}u,\chi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{l}|\langle f^{*}(\phi_{j}u),\psi_{j}\chi\rangle|\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)||I_{\psi_{j}\chi}(\eta)|d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{l}\int_{V_{j}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)||I_{\psi_{j}\chi}(\eta)|d\eta+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V_{j}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)||I_{\psi_{j}\chi}(\eta)|d\eta.

Employing (
3.43
) with
\xi=0
in the stationary phase method
[
28
, Theorem 7.7.1, p. 216]
, one verifies that for every
N>0
there is
C_{N}>0
such that

(3.47)

|I_{\psi_{j}\chi}(\eta)|\leq C_{N}\langle\eta\rangle^{-N},\quad\eta\in V_{j},\,\chi\in B.

Hence, by employing the same technique as in the proof of the Claim in the proof of Proposition
3.7
, one shows that for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
,

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow[0,\infty),\quad u\mapsto\sup_{\chi\in B}\int_{V_{j}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)||I_{\psi_{j}\chi}(\eta)|d\eta,

is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Furthermore, notice that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash V_{j}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)||I_{\psi_{j}\chi}(\eta)|d\eta\leq\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi_{j},\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}}(u)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|I_{\psi_{j}\chi}(\eta)|^{2}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}}d\eta\right)^{1/2}

and the very last integral is uniformly bounded for all
\chi\in B
since
r_{2}>n/2
and
\sup_{\chi\in B}\|I_{\psi_{j}\chi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}<\infty
. This shows that
\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle f^{*}u,\chi\rangle|
is bounded by a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(U)
and the proof of CASE 1 is complete.

CASE 2:
f
is a local diffeomorphism.

8
8

8

In this case
\mathcal{N}_{f}=f(O)\times\{0\}
and thus
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
for any
L
.
We show that
f^{*}
uniquely extends to a well-defined and continuous mapping
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{f^{*}L}(O)
for each
r\in\mathbb{R}
. Notice that
n=m
. By employing partitions of unity, one can easily show that
f^{*}
uniquely extends to a continuous mapping
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O)
(cf.
[
5
, Subsection 5.2]
). Hence, it suffices to provide bounds for
\mathfrak{p}_{r;\varphi,G_{1}}(f^{*}u)
where
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
\emptyset\neq G_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
are such that
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times G_{1})\cap f^{*}L=\emptyset
. We proceed the same as in CASE 1 to obtain (
3.45
) (of course, with
r
in place of
r_{1}
), but now in the construction of
O_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,l
, we make them sufficiently small so that
f
is a diffeomorphism from an open neighbourhood of
\overline{O_{j}}
onto an open subset of
U_{j}
. As before, the first sum in (
3.45
) is bounded by a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
of
u
. We estimate the second sum as follows. Fix
j\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
. We are going to show that

(3.48)

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}I_{2;j}(\xi)\chi(\xi)d\xi\right|\leq C\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\phi_{j},\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}}(u),\quad\chi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m});

this immediately gives
\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{r}I_{2;j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq C\mathfrak{p}_{r;\phi_{j},\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}}(u)
which completes the proof of the existence, continuity and uniqueness of the extension
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{f^{*}L}(O)
. For simpler notations, set
\varphi_{j}:=\psi_{j}\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O_{j})
. Let
\chi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
and set
\widetilde{\chi}:=\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. Notice that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}I_{2;j}(\xi)\chi(\xi)d\xi\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)|\left|\int_{O_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}}e^{if(x)\eta-ix\xi}\varphi_{j}(x)\chi(\xi)dxd\xi\right|d\eta

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)|\left|\int_{O_{j}}e^{if(x)\eta}\varphi_{j}(x)\langle D\rangle^{r}\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi}(x)dx\right|d\eta.

Pick
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{j})
such that
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\psi_{j}
and write
\varphi_{j}\langle D\rangle^{r}=\varphi_{j}\langle D\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\psi}_{j}+\varphi_{j}\langle D\rangle^{r}(1-\widetilde{\psi}_{j})
. There are
a_{j},a^{\prime}_{j}\in S^{r}_{c}(O_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
so that
\operatorname{Op}(a_{j})=\varphi_{j}\langle D\rangle^{r}\widetilde{\psi}_{j}
and
\operatorname{Op}(a^{\prime}_{j})=\varphi_{j}\langle D\rangle^{r}(1-\widetilde{\psi}_{j})
. By construction, the kernel of
\operatorname{Op}(a_{j})
has compact support in
O_{j}\times O_{j}
. Since pseudo-differential operators have kernels that are smooth outside of the diagonal,
\operatorname{Op}(a^{\prime}_{j})\in\Psi^{-\infty}(O_{j})
; consequently,
a^{\prime}_{j}\in S^{-\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(O_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
which yields
a^{\prime}_{j}\in S^{-\infty}_{c}(O_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
. Choose
\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{j})
so that
\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{\psi}_{j}
and notice that

\displaystyle\Bigg|

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}I_{2;j}(\xi)\chi(\xi)d\xi\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{p}_{r;\phi_{j};\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}}(u)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r}\left|\int_{O_{j}}e^{if(x)\eta}\operatorname{Op}(a_{j})(\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})(x)dx\right|^{2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}

(3.49)

\displaystyle{}\quad+\mathfrak{p}_{r;\phi_{j};\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\operatorname{int}V_{j}}(u)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r}\left|\int_{O_{j}}e^{if(x)\eta}\operatorname{Op}(a^{\prime}_{j})(\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})(x)dx\right|^{2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}.

In the first term we change variables
y=f(x)
. In view of
[
29
, Theorem 18.1.17, p. 81]
,
\operatorname{Op}(a_{j})(\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})\circ f^{-1}=\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{j})((\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})\circ f^{-1})
with
\widetilde{a}_{j}\in S^{r}_{c}(f(O_{j})\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
. We infer

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r}\left|\int_{O_{j}}e^{if(x)\eta}\operatorname{Op}(a_{j})(\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})(x)dx\right|^{2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r}\left|\int_{f(O_{j})}e^{iy\eta}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{j})((\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})\circ f^{-1})(y)|f^{-1\,^{\prime}}(y)|dy\right|^{2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{m}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r}\left|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{j})((\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})\circ f^{-1})|f^{-1\,^{\prime}}|\right)(\eta)\right|^{2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{m/2}\left\|\langle D\rangle^{-r}\left(|f^{-1\,^{\prime}}|\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{j})((\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})\circ f^{-1})\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|(\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{j}\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})\circ f^{-1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq C_{2}\|\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}=(2\pi)^{m/2}C_{2}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})},

where the second to last inequality follows from the fact that
\langle D\rangle^{-r}(|f^{-1\,^{\prime}}|\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{j}))
is a
\Psi
DO with symbol in
S^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
(cf.
[
29
, Theorem 18.1.17, p. 81]
) and hence continuous on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. It remains to show a similar estimate for the last integral in (
3.49
). Pick
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
4k+2r>m
. We change variables
y=f(x)
and infer

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r}\left|\int_{O_{j}}e^{if(x)\eta}\operatorname{Op}(a^{\prime}_{j})(\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})(x)dx\right|^{2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r-4k}\left|\int_{f(O_{j})}e^{iy\eta}(\operatorname{Id}-\Delta)^{k}\left(|f^{-1\,^{\prime}}(y)|\operatorname{Op}(a^{\prime}_{j})(\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})(f^{-1}(y))\right)dy\right|^{2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{1}\left\|(\operatorname{Id}-\Delta)^{k}\left(|f^{-1\,^{\prime}}|\operatorname{Op}(a^{\prime}_{j})(\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})\circ f^{-1}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(f(O_{j}))}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{2}\sup_{|\alpha|\leq 2k}\|\partial^{\alpha}\operatorname{Op}(a^{\prime}_{j})(\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq C^{\prime}_{3}\|\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\chi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}=(2\pi)^{m/2}C^{\prime}_{3}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\chi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})},

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
\partial^{\alpha}\operatorname{Op}(a^{\prime}_{j})
is a pseudo-differential operator with symbol in
S^{-\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
. This completes the proof of (
3.48
).

CASE 3:
f
has constant rank
k\geq 1
and satisfies
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
.
Let
r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
be such that
r_{2}-r_{1}\geq(n-k)/2
and
r_{2}>(n-k)/2
; when
f
is a submersion, we only assume that
r_{2}\geq r_{1}
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, we denote
x=(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})
, with
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{m-k}
. Similarly, for
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we denote
\eta=(\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime\prime})
, with
\eta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-k}
. Furthermore, when it is important but not clear from the context, we will denote by
0_{l}
the zero in
\mathbb{R}^{l}
,
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. By the constant rank theorem
[
37
, Theorem 4.12, p. 81]
, for each
x^{(0)}\in O
there are open neighbourhoods
O_{0}\subseteq O
of
x^{(0)}
and
U_{0}\subseteq U
of
f(x^{(0)})
and diffeomorphisms
\kappa:O_{0}\rightarrow\widetilde{O}_{0}
and
\iota:U_{0}\rightarrow\widetilde{U}_{0}
satisfying
\kappa(x^{(0)})=0\in\widetilde{O}_{0}
and
\iota(f(x^{(0)}))=0\in\widetilde{U}_{0}
such that
f(O_{0})\subseteq U_{0}
and

(3.50)

\hat{f}_{0}(x):=\iota\circ f_{|O_{0}}\circ\kappa^{-1}(x)=(x^{\prime},0_{n-k}),\quad x=(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\in\widetilde{O}_{0};

of course, when
f
is a submersion then
k=n
and
\hat{f}_{0}(x)=x^{\prime}
,
x=(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\in\widetilde{O}_{0}
. We make the following

Claim.
Let
\widetilde{L}
be a closed conic subset of
\widetilde{U}_{0}\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
which satisfies
\widetilde{L}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\hat{f}_{0}}=\emptyset
. Then the map
\hat{f}_{0}^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\widetilde{U}_{0})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\widetilde{O}_{0})
is continuous when
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
and
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\widetilde{O}_{0})
are equipped with the topologies induced by
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{\hat{f}^{*}_{0}\widetilde{L}}(\widetilde{O}_{0})
respectively.

Before we prove the claim, we show how CASE 3 follows from it. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
\emptyset\neq G_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
be such that
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times G_{1})\cap f^{*}L=\emptyset
. Arguing as in CASE 1, one can find open sets
O_{1},\ldots,O_{l}\subseteq O
each with compact closure in
O
, open sets
U_{1},\ldots,U_{l}\subseteq U
and diffeomorphisms
\kappa_{j}:O_{j}\rightarrow\widetilde{O}_{j}
and
\iota_{j}:U_{j}\rightarrow\widetilde{U}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,l
, such that
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subseteq\bigcup_{j=1}^{l}O_{j}
,
f(\overline{O_{j}})\subseteq U_{j}
and
\hat{f}_{j}:=\iota_{j}\circ f_{|O_{j}}\circ\kappa_{j}^{-1}:\widetilde{O}_{j}\rightarrow\widetilde{U}_{j}
is given by (
3.50
). Let
\psi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{j})
,
0\leq\psi_{j}\leq 1
,
j=1,\ldots,l
, be a partition of unity on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
and let
\phi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(U_{j})
be such that
\phi_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
f(\overline{O_{j}})
. Set
\varphi_{j}:=\psi_{j}\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O_{j})
,
f_{j}:=f_{|O_{j}}:O_{j}\rightarrow U_{j}
and
L_{j}:=L\cap(U_{j}\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}))
,
j=1,\ldots,l
. For
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
, we have (since
\phi_{j}u\in\mathcal{D}(U_{j})
)

\mathcal{F}(\varphi f^{*}u)=\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j}f^{*}(\phi_{j}u))=\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{j}\kappa_{j}^{*}\hat{f}_{j}^{*}\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}(\phi_{j}u))

and consequently
\mathfrak{p}_{r_{1};\varphi,G_{1}}(f^{*}u)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathfrak{p}_{r_{1};\varphi_{j},G_{1}}(\kappa_{j}^{*}\hat{f}_{j}^{*}\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}(\phi_{j}u))
. Fix
j\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
. In view of CASE 2,
\kappa_{j}^{*}
and
\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}
uniquely extend to well-defined and continuous mappings
\kappa_{j}^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{\hat{f}_{j}^{*}\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}L_{j}}(\widetilde{O}_{j})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}_{j}L_{j}}(O_{j})
(notice that
L_{j}\cap\mathcal{N}_{f_{j}}=\emptyset
and
\hat{f}_{j}^{*}\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}L_{j}=\kappa_{j}^{-1\,*}f_{j}^{*}L_{j}
) and
\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L_{j}}(U_{j})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}L_{j}}(\widetilde{U}_{j})
. The Claim yields that
\hat{f}_{j}^{*}
uniquely extends to a well-defined and continuous mapping
\hat{f}_{j}^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}L_{j}}(\widetilde{U}_{j})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{\hat{f}_{j}^{*}\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}L_{j}}(\widetilde{O}_{j})
(
\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}L_{j}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\hat{f}_{j}}=\emptyset
in view of (
3.42
)). Consequently, there are
\chi_{j^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{D}(U_{j})
and closed cones
V_{j^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
j^{\prime}=1,\ldots,k^{\prime}
(
k^{\prime}
depends on
j
) satisfying
(\operatorname{supp}\chi_{j^{\prime}}\times V_{j^{\prime}})\cap L_{j}=\emptyset
,
j^{\prime}=1,\ldots,k^{\prime}
, a continuous seminorm
\mathfrak{p}
on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U_{j})
(whence, continuous on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
as well) and
C>0
such that

(3.51)

\mathfrak{p}_{r_{1};\varphi_{j},G_{1}}(\kappa_{j}^{*}\hat{f}_{j}^{*}\iota_{j}^{-1\,*}(\phi_{j}u))\leq C\mathfrak{p}(\phi_{j}u)+C\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{k^{\prime}}\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\chi_{j^{\prime}},V_{j^{\prime}}}(\phi_{j}u);

notice that each term on the right-hand side is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(U)
of
u
(as
\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\chi_{j^{\prime}},V_{j^{\prime}}}(\phi_{j}u)=\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\chi_{j^{\prime}}\phi_{j},V_{j^{\prime}}}(u)
). It remains to show similar bounds for
\mathfrak{p}(f^{*}u)
, where
\mathfrak{p}
is an arbitrary continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O)
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\mathfrak{p}=\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\cdot,\chi\rangle|
for some bounded subset
B
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O)
. There is a compact subset
K
of
O
such that
B
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}_{K}
. Now, as before, one applies a partition of unity together with the Claim and CASE 2 to show analogous bounds for
\mathfrak{p}(u)
,
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(O)
, as in (
3.51
). This completes the proof of CASE 3 and the theorem.

Proof of Claim.
Notice that

(3.52)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\hat{f}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\{((x^{\prime},0_{n-k}),(0_{k},\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}))\in\widetilde{U}_{0}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,\exists x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{m-k},\,(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\in\widetilde{O}_{0}\},

(3.53)

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{0}^{*}\widetilde{L}

\displaystyle=\{((x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}),(\eta^{\prime},0_{m-k}))\in\widetilde{O}_{0}\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})\,|\,\exists\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-k},\,((x^{\prime},0_{n-k}),(\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}))\in\widetilde{L}\}.

Let
G_{0}
be a closed cone in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\widetilde{O}_{0})\backslash\{0\}
be such that
(\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times G_{0})\cap\hat{f}^{*}_{0}\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
; since our goal is to estimate
\mathfrak{p}_{r_{1};\varphi,G_{0}}(\hat{f}^{*}_{0}u)
, we can assume that
G_{0}\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
. For
\xi=(\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})\in G_{0}\cap\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
, a standard compactness argument shows that there are
\varepsilon_{\xi}>0
and an open set
O_{\xi}\subseteq\widetilde{O}_{0}
with compact closure in
\widetilde{O}_{0}
such that
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subseteq O_{\xi}
and
(\overline{O_{\xi}}\times G_{\xi})\cap\hat{f}^{*}_{0}\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
where
G_{\xi}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
is the open cone
\mathbb{R}_{+}(B(\xi^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\xi})\times B(\xi^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon_{\xi}))
; furthermore, when
\xi^{\prime\prime}\neq 0
, we can take
\varepsilon_{\xi}<|\xi^{\prime\prime}|/6
. Of course, when
k=m
,
G_{\xi}=\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\xi,\varepsilon_{\xi})
. We employ another compactness argument to find open cones

G_{j}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}(B(\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}},\varepsilon_{j})\times B(\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime\prime}},\varepsilon_{j}))\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{G}_{j}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}(B(\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}},3\varepsilon_{j})\times B(\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime\prime}},3\varepsilon_{j}))

with
\xi^{(j)}=(\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}},\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime\prime}})\in G_{0}\cap\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
and
\varepsilon_{j}\in(0,1/6)
,
j=1,\ldots,s
, and an open set
\widetilde{O}_{1}\subseteq\widetilde{O}_{0}
with compact closure in
\widetilde{O}_{0}
such that
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times G_{0}\subseteq\widetilde{O}_{1}\times\bigcup_{j=1}^{s}G_{j}
and
(\overline{\widetilde{O}_{1}}\times\bigcup_{j=1}^{s}\overline{\widetilde{G}_{j}})\cap\hat{f}^{*}_{0}\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
. Furthermore, when
\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0
, it holds that
\varepsilon_{j}<|\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime\prime}}|/6
. Again, when
k=m
,
G_{j}=\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\xi,\varepsilon_{j})
and
\widetilde{G}_{j}=\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\xi,3\varepsilon_{j})
. Write
\{1,\ldots,s\}=J_{1}\cup J_{2}
where
J_{1}
contains all indexes
j
such that
\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0
and
J_{2}=\{1,\ldots,s\}\backslash J_{1}
; when
k=m
, we set
J_{1}=\emptyset
. If
J_{1}\neq\emptyset
, there is
0<\varepsilon<1/2
such that
\bigcup_{j\in J_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{j}\subseteq\{(\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\,|\,|\xi^{\prime\prime}|>\varepsilon|\xi^{\prime}|\}=:\widetilde{G}_{0}
; when
J_{1}=\emptyset
, we set
\widetilde{G}_{0}:=\emptyset
. In view of (
3.53
),
(\widetilde{O}_{1}\times\overline{\widetilde{G}_{0}})\cap\hat{f}^{*}_{0}\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
. When
J_{2}\neq\emptyset
, for each
j\in J_{2}
, define the open cones
G^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{k}
as
G^{\prime}_{j}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}},\varepsilon_{j})
and
\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}},3\varepsilon_{j})
. Then
G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}
and
\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}
are open cones in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
such that
G_{j}\subseteq G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}
and, in view of (
3.53
), we have
(\hat{f}_{0}(\overline{\widetilde{O}_{1}})\times\overline{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k})\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
,
j\in J_{2}
. Another compactness argument implies that there is an open set
U_{1}\subseteq\widetilde{U}_{0}
such that
\hat{f}(\overline{\widetilde{O}_{1}})\subseteq U_{1}
and
(U_{1}\times\overline{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k})\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
. When
k<n
, since
\hat{f}_{0}(\operatorname{supp}\varphi)\times(\{0_{k}\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k})\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{\hat{f}_{0}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\hat{f}_{0}}\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
, an analogous compactness argument shows that there are an open set
\widetilde{U}_{1}\subseteq\widetilde{U}_{0}
satisfying
\hat{f}_{0}(\operatorname{supp}\varphi)\subseteq\widetilde{U}_{1}
and points
\eta^{(1)\,^{\prime\prime\prime}},\ldots,\eta^{(l)\,^{\prime\prime\prime}}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-k-1}
defining the open cones
V_{j}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}(B(0_{k},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{j})\times B(\eta^{(j)\,^{\prime\prime\prime}},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{j}))\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
with some
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{j}\in(0,1/2)
,
j=1,\ldots,l
, such that
\hat{f}_{0}(\operatorname{supp}\varphi)\times(\{0_{k}\}\times(\mathbb{R}^{n-k}\backslash\{0_{n-k}\}))\subseteq\widetilde{U}_{1}\times(\bigcup_{j=1}^{l}V_{j})
and
(\widetilde{U}_{1}\times(\bigcup_{j=1}^{l}V_{j}))\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
. It is straightforward to verify that there is
C_{0}>1
such that the open cone
\widetilde{V}_{0}:=\{(\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,|\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}|>C_{0}|\eta^{\prime}|\}
satisfies
\widetilde{U}_{1}\times(\overline{\widetilde{V}_{0}}\backslash\{0_{n}\})\subseteq\widetilde{U}_{1}\times(\bigcup_{j=1}^{l}V_{j})
.

Pick
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(U_{1})
such that
\phi=1
on
