\hat{f}_{0}(\operatorname{supp}\varphi)
; when
k<n
we choose
\phi
such that it also satisfies
\operatorname{supp}\phi\subseteq\widetilde{U}_{1}\cap U_{1}
. For
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\varphi\hat{f}_{0}^{*}u)(\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}(\varphi\hat{f}_{0}^{*}(\phi u))(\xi)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\widetilde{O}_{1}}e^{i\hat{f}_{0}(x)\eta-ix\xi}\varphi(x)\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)dxd\eta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)\int_{\widetilde{O}_{1}}e^{-ix(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})}\varphi(x)dxd\eta

(3.54)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})d\eta

(3.55)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k}}v(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime})\mathcal{F}\varphi(\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})d\eta^{\prime},

where we denoted
v(\eta^{\prime}):=(2\pi)^{-n+k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime\prime})d\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}
; when
k=n
,
\eta^{\prime}=\eta
and we set
v:=\mathcal{F}(\phi u)
. We infer

(3.56)

\mathfrak{p}_{r_{1};\varphi,G_{0}}(\hat{f}_{0}^{*}u)\leq\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}_{0}}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi\hat{f}_{0}^{*}(\phi u))(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r_{1}}d\xi\right)^{1/2}\\
+\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\left(\int_{G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi\hat{f}_{0}^{*}(\phi u))(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r_{1}}d\xi\right)^{1/2}.

We first consider the integral over
\widetilde{G}_{0}
. We only look at the case
k<m
, since when
k=m
,
\widetilde{G}_{0}=\emptyset
. Notice that for every
N>0
there is
C_{N}>0
such that

(3.57)

|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\leq C_{N}\langle\xi^{\prime}\rangle^{-N}\langle\xi^{\prime\prime}\rangle^{-N}\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{-N},\quad(\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})\in\widetilde{G}_{0},\,\eta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}.

When
k=n
, employing this bound together with (
3.54
) and arguing as in the proof of the Claim in the proof of Proposition
3.7
, it is straightforward to show that

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\widetilde{U}_{0})\rightarrow[0,\infty),\quad u\mapsto\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}_{0}}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi\hat{f}_{0}^{*}(\phi u))(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r_{1}}d\xi\right)^{1/2},

is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
. When
k<n
, we employ (
3.54
) and write

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{\widetilde{G}_{0}}

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}(\varphi\hat{f}_{0}^{*}(\phi u))(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r_{1}}d\xi\Bigg)^{1/2}

(3.58)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\left(\int_{\widetilde{V}_{0}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\eta\right)^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle{}\quad+\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\widetilde{V}_{0}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\eta\right)^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}.

Employing (
3.57
), one again shows that

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\widetilde{U}_{0})\rightarrow[0,\infty),\quad u\mapsto\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\widetilde{V}_{0}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\eta\right)^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2},

is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
. To estimate the term in (
3.58
), we employ (
3.57
) with
N=|r_{1}|+r_{2}+n+m+1
and infer

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\left(\int_{\widetilde{V}_{0}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\eta\right)^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{1}\int_{\widetilde{V}_{0}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)|\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{-r_{2}-n-1}d\eta

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{2}\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi,\overline{\widetilde{V}_{0}}}(u)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}}\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{-2r_{2}-2n-2}d\eta\right)^{1/2}\leq C^{\prime}_{3}\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi,\overline{\widetilde{V}_{0}}}(u),

where the last inequality follows from
r_{2}>(n-k)/2
; notice that
\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi,\overline{\widetilde{V}_{0}}}
is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
by the way we defined
\widetilde{V}_{0}
and
\phi
. We showed that the first term in (
3.56
) is bounded by a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
of
u
. Next, we bound each of the summands in the second term in (
3.56
). Fix

. We employ (
3.54
) and estimate as follows:

\displaystyle\left(\int_{G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi\hat{f}_{0}^{*}(\phi u))(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r_{1}}d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\eta\right)^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle{}\quad+\left(\int_{G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}\left(\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{k}\backslash\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j})\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\eta\right)^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}.

Denote the two terms by
I^{\prime}_{j}
and
I^{\prime\prime}_{j}
respectively. We first estimate
I^{\prime}_{j}
. For the moment, when
k<n
, denote
r^{\prime}_{1}:=\max\{r_{1},0\}
and, if
k=n
, set
r^{\prime}_{1}:=r_{1}
. We employ Hölder’s inequality in the inner integral to obtain

I^{\prime}_{j}\leq\Bigg(\int_{G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}\frac{|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}}{\langle\eta\rangle^{2r_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}}}d\eta\right)\\
\cdot\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)|^{2}\langle\eta\rangle^{2r_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\eta\right)d\xi\Bigg)^{1/2}.

When
k<n
, we employ spherical coordinates and the fact
2r_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}-n+k>0
(this follows from
r_{2}>(n-k)/2
and
r_{2}-r_{1}\geq(n-k)/2
) to infer

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}+r^{\prime}_{1}}d\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{n-k-1}d\rho}{(1+|\eta^{\prime}|+\rho)^{2r_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{1}\int_{1+|\eta^{\prime}|}^{\infty}\frac{d\rho}{\rho^{2r_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}-n+k+1}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{-2r_{2}+r^{\prime}_{1}+n-k}

and, as
r_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}\geq(n-k)/2
(again, this follows from
r_{2}>(n-k)/2
and
r_{2}-r_{1}\geq(n-k)/2
), we deduce

\int_{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}\frac{|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}}{\langle\eta\rangle^{2r_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}}}d\eta\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k}}\frac{|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{|r_{1}|}\langle\xi^{\prime\prime}\rangle^{|r_{1}|}}{\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{2r_{2}-2r^{\prime}_{1}-n+k}}d\eta^{\prime}\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{4}

for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
. Notice that this bound also holds when
k=n
, since we assume
r_{2}\geq r_{1}
in this case. Consequently

\displaystyle I^{\prime}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C^{\prime\prime}_{4}}\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)|^{2}\langle\eta\rangle^{2r_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\xi d\eta\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{5}\left(\int_{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)|^{2}\langle\eta\rangle^{2r_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi)|\langle\xi^{\prime}\rangle^{|r_{1}|}\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{r^{\prime}_{1}}\langle\xi^{\prime\prime}\rangle^{|r_{1}|}d\xi d\eta\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{6}\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi,\overline{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}(u);

notice that
\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi,\overline{\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}
is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
by the way we defined
\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}
.

9

9

The bound for
I^{\prime}_{j}
can be also derived by employing the Schur test with weights; the above arguments are essentially the proof of the test. We did not apply it merely to avoid additional unnecessary notational complexity which would come from writing the kernel and the weights.
We now turn our attention to
I^{\prime\prime}_{j}
. Let
\eta^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{R}^{k}\backslash\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j})\backslash\{0_{k}\}
and
\xi^{\prime}\in G^{\prime}_{j}
be arbitrary. Then
|\eta^{\prime}/|\eta^{\prime}|-\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}}|\geq 3\varepsilon_{j}
and there is
\lambda>0
such that
|\xi^{\prime}/\lambda-\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}}|<\varepsilon_{j}
. Since
|\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}}|=1
(as
\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime\prime}}=0
and
(\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}},\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime\prime}})\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
), we have

\left|1-\frac{|\xi^{\prime}|}{\lambda}\right|\leq\left|\xi^{(j)^{\prime}\,}-\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{\lambda}\right|<\varepsilon_{j},\quad\mbox{hence}\quad\left|\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{|\xi^{\prime}|}-\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}}\right|\leq\left|\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{|\xi^{\prime}|}-\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{\lambda}\right|+\left|\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{\lambda}-\xi^{(j)\,^{\prime}}\right|<2\varepsilon_{j}.

Consequently,
|\eta^{\prime}/|\eta^{\prime}|-\xi^{\prime}/|\xi^{\prime}||>\varepsilon_{j}
which gives

\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime}}{|\eta^{\prime}|}-\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{|\eta^{\prime}|}\right|\geq\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime}}{|\eta^{\prime}|}-\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{|\xi^{\prime}|}\right|-\left|\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{|\xi^{\prime}|}-\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{|\eta^{\prime}|}\right|>\varepsilon_{j}-\frac{||\eta^{\prime}|-|\xi^{\prime}||}{|\eta^{\prime}|}\geq\varepsilon_{j}-\left|\frac{\eta^{\prime}}{|\eta^{\prime}|}-\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{|\eta^{\prime}|}\right|.

Thus
\langle\eta^{\prime}-\xi^{\prime}\rangle>(\varepsilon_{j}/2)\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle
and similarly
\langle\eta^{\prime}-\xi^{\prime}\rangle>(\varepsilon_{j}/2)\langle\xi^{\prime}\rangle
; notice that these inequalities are valid even when
\eta^{\prime}=0
. These inequalities show that for every
N>0
there is
C_{N}>0
such that

(3.59)

|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\leq C_{N}\langle\xi^{\prime}\rangle^{-N}\langle\xi^{\prime\prime}\rangle^{-N}\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{-N},\quad\xi^{\prime}\in G^{\prime}_{j},\,\xi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{m-k},\,\eta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\backslash\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j}.

When
k=n
, these bounds yield that the integral
I^{\prime\prime}_{j}
is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
of
u
. Assume that
k<n
. Write

I^{\prime\prime}_{j}\leq\left(\int_{G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}\left(\int_{((\mathbb{R}^{k}\backslash\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j})\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k})\backslash\widetilde{V}_{0}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\eta\right)^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}\\
+\left(\int_{G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}\left(\int_{((\mathbb{R}^{k}\backslash\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j})\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k})\cap\widetilde{V}_{0}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|\langle\xi\rangle^{r_{1}}d\eta\right)^{2}d\xi\right)^{1/2}.

In view of (
3.59
), the first integral is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
of
u
. It remains to bound the second integral; denote it for simplicity by
I^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}
. For ease in writing, we denote the closed cone
((\mathbb{R}^{k}\backslash\widetilde{G}^{\prime}_{j})\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k})\cap\overline{\widetilde{V}_{0}}
by
\widetilde{V}_{1}
. Notice that, by construction,
\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi,\widetilde{V}_{1}}
is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
. We infer

I^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}\leq\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi,\widetilde{V}_{1}}(u)\left(\int_{G^{\prime}_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}\int_{\widetilde{V}_{1}}|\mathcal{F}\varphi(\xi^{\prime}-\eta^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r_{1}}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}}d\eta d\xi\right)^{1/2}=C^{\prime\prime}\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi,\widetilde{V}_{1}}(u),

where we employed (
3.59
) with
N=|r_{1}|+n+m+1
and the assumption
r_{2}>(n-k)/2
. This completes the proof that
\mathfrak{p}_{r_{1};\varphi,G_{0}}(\hat{f}_{0}^{*}u)
is bounded by a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
of
u
. To finish the proof of the claim, it remains to show such bounds for
\mathfrak{p}(\hat{f}_{0}^{*}u)
where
\mathfrak{p}
is an arbitrary continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\widetilde{O}_{0})
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\mathfrak{p}=\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\cdot,\chi\rangle|
for some bounded subset
B
of
\mathcal{D}(\widetilde{O}_{0})
. There exists a compact subset
K
of
\widetilde{O}_{0}
such that
B
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}_{K}
. When
k<n
, in the same way as above, one can find an open set
\widetilde{U}_{K}\subseteq\widetilde{U}_{0}
satisfying
\hat{f}_{0}^{*}(K)\subseteq\widetilde{U}_{K}
and
C_{K}>1
such that
\widetilde{V}_{K}:=\{(\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,|\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}|>C_{K}|\eta^{\prime}|\}
satisfies
(\widetilde{U}_{K}\times\overline{\widetilde{V}_{K}})\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
. Pick
\phi_{0}\in\mathcal{D}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
such that
\phi_{0}=1
on
\hat{f}_{0}^{*}(K)
; when
k<n
we choose
\phi_{0}
so that
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{0}\subseteq\widetilde{U}_{K}
. For
\chi\in B
, we denote
\widetilde{\chi}(x^{\prime}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}\chi(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})dx^{\prime\prime}
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
; when
k=m
we set
\widetilde{\chi}:=\chi
. For every
N>0
there is
C_{N}>0
such that

(3.60)

|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}(\eta^{\prime})|\leq C_{N}\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{-N},\quad\eta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k},\,\chi\in B.

We infer

\displaystyle\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\hat{f}_{0}^{*}u,\chi\rangle|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\hat{f}_{0}^{*}(\phi_{0}u),\chi\rangle|=\sup_{\chi\in B}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{0}u)(\eta)\int_{\widetilde{O}_{0}}e^{ix^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}\chi(x)dxd\eta\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\chi\in B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{0}u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}(\eta^{\prime})|d\eta.

When
k=n
, the bound (
3.60
) shows that the very last quantity is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
of
u
. Assume that
k<n
and write

\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\hat{f}_{0}^{*}u,\chi\rangle|\leq\sup_{\chi\in B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\widetilde{V}_{K}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{0}u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}(\eta^{\prime})|d\eta\\
+\sup_{\chi\in B}\int_{\widetilde{V}_{K}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{0}u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}(\eta^{\prime})|d\eta.

The bound (
3.60
) shows that the first quantity is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\widetilde{U}_{0})
of
u
. We estimate the second quantity as follows:

\sup_{\chi\in B}\int_{\widetilde{V}_{K}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{0}u)(\eta)||\mathcal{F}^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}(\eta^{\prime})|d\eta\leq\mathfrak{p}_{r_{2};\phi_{0},\overline{\widetilde{V}_{K}}}(u)\sup_{\chi\in B}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}(\eta^{\prime})|^{2}\langle\eta\rangle^{-2r_{2}}d\eta\right)^{1/2}.

The integral is uniformly bounded for all
\chi\in B
in view of (
3.60
) and the assumption
r_{2}>(n-k)/2
. This completes the proof of the claim.
∎

Remark 3.23
.

If
f:O\rightarrow U
has constant rank
k=0
then
f^{*}
uniquely extends to a continuous mapping
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(O)
when
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
and
r>n/2
. To verify this, we first point out that the connected components of
O
are open and at most countably many. The assumption on
f
implies that it is constant on every connected component of
O
. Hence, there are at most countably many pairwise disjoint open sets
O_{j}\subseteq O
,
j\in\Lambda
, whose union is
O
and distinct points
y_{j}\in U
,
j\in\Lambda
, such that
f(x)=y_{j}
,
x\in O_{j}
,
j\in\Lambda
(some of the
O_{j}
’s may be unions of some of the components of
O
!). Notice that
\mathcal{N}_{f}=(\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda}\{y_{j}\})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Let
K\subset\subset O
. Denote
\Lambda_{K}:=\{j\in\Lambda\,|\,O_{j}\cap K\neq\emptyset\}
; clearly,
\Lambda_{K}
is finite. Choose
\phi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(U)
,
j\in\Lambda_{K}
, such that
\phi_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
y_{j}
and
(\operatorname{supp}\phi_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap L=\emptyset
. For
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
we have
f^{*}u=\sum_{j\in\Lambda}u(y_{j})\mathbf{1}_{O_{j}}
and consequently, for
l\in\mathbb{N}
, we infer

\displaystyle\sup_{|\alpha|\leq l}\|\partial^{\alpha}(f^{*}u)\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{K}}|\phi_{j}(y_{j})u(y_{j})|\leq\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{K}}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j}u)(\eta)|d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-r}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{K}}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\phi_{j},\mathbb{R}^{n}}(u),

which shows the claim. When
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
, the condition
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
implies that for every
j\in\Lambda
there is an open neighbourhood
U_{j}\subseteq U
of
y_{j}
such that
u\in\mathcal{C}(U_{j})
; indeed, for each
j\in\Lambda
there is
\phi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(U)
as above such that
\phi_{j}u\in H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\subseteq\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Whence, in view of the above and Proposition
3.13
, we deduce
f^{*}u=\sum_{j\in\Lambda}u(y_{j})\mathbf{1}_{O_{j}}
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(U)
.

Applying the theorem with
L:=WF^{r_{2}}(u)
for
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
satisfying
WF^{r_{2}}(u)\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
, we deduce the following result.

Corollary 3.24
.

Let
O
and
U
be open subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
\mathbb{R}^{n}
respectively and let
f:O\rightarrow U
be a smooth map. Given
r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
which satisfy
r_{2}-r_{1}>n/2
and
r_{2}>n/2
, it holds that

(3.61)

WF^{r_{1}}(f^{*}u)\subseteq f^{*}WF^{r_{2}}(u),\quad\mbox{for all}\,\,u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\,\,\mbox{satisfying}\,\,WF^{r_{2}}(u)\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset.

If
f
has constant rank
k\geq 1
, then (
3.61
) holds true when
r_{2}-r_{1}\geq(n-k)/2
and
r_{2}>(n-k)/2
. When
f
is a submersion, (
3.61
) holds true for all
r_{2}\geq r_{1}
. In particular, if
f
is a diffeomorphism then
WF^{r}(f^{*}u)=f^{*}WF^{r}(u)
for all
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
and
r\in\mathbb{R}
.

When
B
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
satisfying
WF^{r_{2}}_{c}(B)\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
, we can apply the theorem with
L:=WF^{r_{2}}_{c}(B)
together with Corollary
3.11
, to deduce the following result.

Corollary 3.25
.

Let
O
and
U
be open subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
\mathbb{R}^{n}
respectively, let
f:O\rightarrow U
be a smooth map and let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Given
r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
which satisfy
r_{2}-r_{1}>n/2
and
r_{2}>n/2
, the following statement holds true:

(*)

if
WF^{r_{2}}_{c}(B)\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
then
B
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{WF^{r_{2}}_{c}(B)}(U)
,
f^{*}B
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}WF^{r_{2}}_{c}(B)}(O)
and
WF^{r_{1}}_{c}(f^{*}B)\subseteq f^{*}WF^{r_{2}}_{c}(B)
.

If
f
has constant rank
k\geq 1
, then the statement
(*)
holds true when
r_{2}-r_{1}\geq(n-k)/2
and
r_{2}>(n-k)/2
. When
f
is a submersion,
(*)
holds true for all
r_{2}\geq r_{1}
. In particular, if
f
is a diffeomorphism then
WF^{r}_{c}(f^{*}B)=f^{*}WF^{r}_{c}(B)
for all
r\in\mathbb{R}
and all bounded subsets
B
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)
.

Finally, we point out that one can consider a variant of Theorem
3.21
for the space
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)
, but we will not need such general facts. However, we will need the special case when
f
is a diffeomorphism.

Corollary 3.26
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{R}
and let
f:O\rightarrow U
be a diffeomorphism between the open sets
O
and
U
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any closed conic subset
L
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
and any compact subset
K
of
U
satisfying
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(L)\subseteq K
, the pullback
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O)
restricts to a topological isomorphism
f^{*}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{f^{*}L;f^{-1}(K)}(O)
. Consequently, it also restricts to a topological isomorphism
f^{*}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{f^{*}W}(O)
for any open conic subset
W
of
U\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
.

Proof.

Since
f^{*}(H^{r}_{K}(U))=H^{r}_{f^{-1}(K)}(O)
as sets,
[
28
, Theorem 8.2.4, p. 263]
implies that
f^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U))=\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{f^{*}L;f^{-1}(K)}(O)
as sets. As
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O)
is continuous, the closed graph and the open mapping theorems for Fréchet spaces imply that
f^{*}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{f^{*}L;f^{-1}(K)}(O)
is a topological isomorphism. The last part is an immediate consequence of this.
∎

4.
The spaces
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}
on manifolds and vector bundles

The diffeomorphism invariance from Theorem
3.21
and Corollary
3.26
allows us to define
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}
on smooth manifolds. Our goal in this section is to show a duality result for these spaces and to characterise the relatively compact subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}
as in the Euclidean case. At the very end, we will show more general version of Theorem
3.21
for pullback by smooth maps of distributional sections of vector bundles.

From now and throughout the rest of the article, we will always employ the Einstein summation convention. Furthermore,
M
will always stand for a smooth
m
-dimensional manifold and we will consistently apply the notations from Subsection
2.1
.

4.1.
The Sobolev compactness wave front set and the topology of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}
on manifolds and vector bundles

We start by recalling the Sobolev wave front set of order
r\in\mathbb{R}
of
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
. The definition we are going to give is in the same spirit as in the Euclidean case; as we pointed out in Section
3
, the original definition of Duistermaat and Hörmander
[
14
, p. 201]
is via pseudo-differential operators but one can easily convince oneself that they are the same by going to a chart and noticing that there they coincide in view of
[
30
, Proposition 8.2.6, p. 189]
. Pick a chart
(O,x)
about
p\in M
and, for
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
, denote

\Sigma^{r}_{p}(u):=\{\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p}\in T^{*}_{p}M\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\Sigma^{r}_{x(p)}(u_{x})\},

where
u_{x}
is the distribution defined in (
2.1
). Corollary
3.24
verifies that
\Sigma^{r}_{p}(u)
does not depend on the chart
(O,x)
that contains
p
. The Sobolev wave front set of order
r
of
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
is defined by

(4.1)

WF^{r}(u):=\{(p,\xi)\in T^{*}M\backslash 0\,|\,\xi\in\Sigma^{r}_{p}(u)\}.

If
\pi_{E}:E\rightarrow M
is a vector bundle of rank
k
, then for a chart
(O,x)
about
p\in M
over which
E
has a local trivialisation
\Phi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
define

\Sigma^{r}_{p}(u):=\{\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p}\in T^{*}_{p}M\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\cup_{j=1}^{k}\Sigma^{r}_{x(p)}(u_{\Phi_{x}}^{j})\},

where
u_{\Phi_{x}}^{1},\ldots,u_{\Phi_{x}}^{k}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x(O))
are the distributions defined in (
2.2
). Employing Corollary
3.24
, one can show that
\Sigma^{r}_{p}(u)
does not depend on the chart
(O,x)
nor on the local trivialisation
\Phi_{x}
. The Sobolev wave front set
WF^{r}(u)
of
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
is defined as in (
4.1
). Notice that
\Sigma^{r}_{p}(u)
is a closed cone in
T^{*}_{p}M\backslash\{0\}
and
WF^{r}(u)
is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
.

Let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
and
(O,x)
a chart on
M
. Denote
B_{x}:=\{u_{x}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x(O))\,|\,u\in B\}
and notice that
B_{x}
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x(O))
. For a chart
(O,x)
about
p\in M
, set

\Sigma^{r}_{c,p}(B):=\{\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p}\in T^{*}_{p}M\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\Sigma^{r}_{c,x(p)}(B_{x})\}.

In view of Corollary
3.25
,
\Sigma^{r}_{c,p}(B)
does not depend on the chart
(O,x)
that contains
p
and, similarly as above, we define the
Sobolev compactness wave front set of order
r
of
B
by

(4.2)

WF^{r}_{c}(B):=\{(p,\xi)\in T^{*}M\backslash 0\,|\,\xi\in\Sigma^{r}_{c,p}(B)\}.

Of course, this coincides with Definition
3.3
when
M
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

Similarly, if
E
is a
k
-vector bundle over
M
and
B
a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
, for any chart
(O,x)
over which
E
has a local trivialisation
\Phi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
, we denote
B_{\Phi_{x}}^{j}:=\{u_{\Phi_{x}}^{j}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x(O))\,|\,u\in B\}
,
j=1,\ldots,k
; of course,
B_{\Phi_{x}}^{1},\ldots,B_{\Phi_{x}}^{k}
are bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x(O))
. For any such chart
(O,x)
about
p\in M
, we define

\Sigma^{r}_{c,p}(B):=\{\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p}\in T^{*}_{p}M\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\cup_{j=1}^{k}\Sigma^{r}_{c,x(p)}(B_{\Phi_{x}}^{j})\}.

As before, employing Corollary
3.25
, one can show that
\Sigma^{r}_{c,p}(B)
does not depend on the chart
(O,x)
that contains
p
nor on the local trivialisation
\Phi_{x}
. We define the
Sobolev compactness wave front set of order
r
of
B
by (
4.2
). Of course,
\Sigma^{r}_{c,p}(B)
is a closed cone in
T^{*}_{p}M\backslash\{0\}
and
WF^{r}_{c}(B)
is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. We point out that if
B=\{u^{(1)},\ldots,u^{(l)}\}
then
WF^{r}_{c}(B)=WF^{r}(u^{(1)})\cup\ldots\cup WF^{r}(u^{(l)})
. Furthermore, as in the Euclidean case,
WF^{r}_{c}(a_{1}B_{1}+a_{2}B_{2})\subseteq WF^{r}_{c}(B_{1})\cup WF^{r}_{c}(B_{2})
for any
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
and any bounded subsets
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
(or, of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
); if in addition
B_{1}\subseteq B_{2}
then
WF^{r}_{c}(B_{1})\subseteq WF^{r}_{c}(B_{2})
.

Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
and
E
a
k
-vector bundle over
M
. For
r\in\mathbb{R}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)

\displaystyle:=\{u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)\,|\,WF^{r}(u)\subseteq L\}\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)

\displaystyle:=\{u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\,|\,WF^{r}(u)\subseteq L\};

of course, when
M
is an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
coincides as a set with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
as defined in (
3.1
) in the Euclidean setting. Our goal is to equip
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
with locally convex topologies. Let
(O,x)
be a chart on
M
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and let
V
be a closed cone in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
such that

(4.3)

\{(p,\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p})\in T^{*}O\,|\,p\in\operatorname{supp}\varphi,\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in V\}\cap L=\emptyset;

notice that the first set is the inverse image of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\times V
under the chart induced local trivialisation of
T^{*}M
over
O
(i.e.,
\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
(p,\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p})\mapsto(p,\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})
). Then, when
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
we have
(\operatorname{supp}(\varphi\circ x^{-1})\times V)\cap WF^{r}(u_{x})=\emptyset
and Corollary
3.5
yields that

(4.4)

\mathfrak{p}^{x}_{r;\varphi,V}(u):=\left(\int_{V}|\mathcal{F}((\varphi\circ x^{-1})u_{x})(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}<\infty,\quad u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M).

We equip
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
with the locally convex topology induced by all continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
together with all seminorms
\mathfrak{p}^{x}_{r;\varphi,V}
for all charts
(O,x)
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
as above. It is straightforward to check that if
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
satisfies
\mathfrak{p}^{x}_{r;\varphi,V}(u)<\infty
for all
\mathfrak{p}^{x}_{r;\varphi,V}
as above then
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
.

Analogously, given a chart
(O,x)
over which
E
trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
, a function
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and a closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
which satisfy (
4.3
), it holds that
(\operatorname{supp}(\varphi\circ x^{-1})\times V)\cap WF^{r}(u_{\Phi_{x}}^{j})=\emptyset
,
j=1,\ldots,k
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
. Consequently, Corollary
3.5
gives

(4.5)

\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}(u):=\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\left(\int_{V}|\mathcal{F}((\varphi\circ x^{-1})u_{\Phi_{x}}^{j})(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\right)^{1/2}<\infty,\quad u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E).

We equip
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
with the locally convex topology induced by all continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
together with all seminorms
\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}
for all charts
(O,x)
over which
E
locally trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and all
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
as above. Again, it is straightforward to check that if
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
satisfies
\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}(u)<\infty
for all
\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}
as above then
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
.

Remark 4.1
.

Notice that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{T^{*}M\backslash 0}(M)=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{T^{*}M\backslash 0}(M;E)=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
topologically. Also, it is straightforward to verify that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\emptyset}(M)=H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\emptyset}(M;E)=H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
topologically.

The following result will allow us to transfer the topological properties we showed in the Euclidean case to the case of manifolds and vector bundles. From now, we only state and prove the claims in the vector bundle case since the manifold case can be viewed as a special case of it (cf. Subsection
2.1
); we will keep both cases in the definitions for better clarity.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
\pi_{E}:E\rightarrow M
be a vector bundle of rank
k
and let
L
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Let
\{(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})\}_{\mu\in\Lambda}
be a family of coordinate charts on
M
which cover
M
such that
E
locally trivialises over each
O_{\mu}
via
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow O_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
,
\mu\in\Lambda
. Let
\kappa_{\mu}
,
\mu\in\Lambda
, be the total local trivialisation of
T^{*}M
over
O_{\mu}
:

(4.6)

\kappa_{\mu}:\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times\mathbb{R}^{m},\quad\kappa_{\mu}(p,\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p})=(x_{\mu}(p),\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m}).

Then for each bounded subset
B
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
,
WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq L
if and only if for all
\mu\in\Lambda
and for all
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
and closed cones
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
which satisfy (
4.3
), it holds that

(4.7)

\sup_{u\in B}\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\int_{V,\,|\xi|>R}|\mathcal{F}((\varphi\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})u_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}^{j})(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r}d\xi\rightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad R\rightarrow\infty.

Furthermore, for each
\mu\in\Lambda
,
L_{\mu}:=\kappa_{\mu}(\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(O_{\mu})\cap L)
is a closed conic subset of
x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
and the map

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k},\,\,u\mapsto\mathbf{f}_{u},\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{u}(\mu):=(u_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}^{1},\ldots,u_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}^{k}),\,\mu\in\Lambda,

is a well-defined topological imbedding whose image is closed and complemented in
\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k}
.

Proof.

The proof that
WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq L
is equivalent to (
4.7
) is straightforward and we omit it (cf. Lemma
3.4
). For the proof of the second part, we denote
u_{\mu}^{j}:=u_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}^{j}
and
\Phi_{\mu}:=\Phi_{x_{\mu}}
,
\mu\in\Lambda
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
,
j=1,\ldots,k
. The fact that
L_{\mu}
is a closed conic subset of
x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
is trivial. The map (
4.8
) is well-defined in view of the first part (applied to a singleton) and the proof of its continuity is straightforward (recall that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
,
u\mapsto u_{\mu}^{j}
, is continuous). Let
(\varphi_{\mu})_{\mu\in\Lambda}
be a smooth partition of unity subordinated to
(O_{\mu})_{\mu\in\Lambda}
. A standard argument employing this partition of unity immediately yields the injectivity of (
4.8
).

To show that (
4.8
) is a topological imbedding whose image is complemented in
\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k}
, we consider the map

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}:\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E),\,\,\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{f})=u_{\mathbf{f}},\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\langle u_{\mathbf{f}},\psi\rangle:=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda}\langle\mathbf{f}(\mu)^{j},(\varphi_{\mu}\psi_{\mu,j})\circ x_{\mu}^{-1}\rangle,\,\,\psi\in\Gamma_{c}(E^{\vee}),\,\psi_{|O_{\mu}}=\psi_{\mu,j}\sigma_{\mu}^{j},

and
(\sigma_{\mu}^{1},\ldots,\sigma_{\mu}^{k})
is the local frame for
E^{\vee}
over
O_{\mu}
induced by
\Phi_{\mu}
. It is straightforward to check that the map is well-defined and continuous. We claim that
\mathcal{R}
restricts to a well-defined and continuous map

(4.10)

\mathcal{R}:\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E).

Let
(O,x)
be a chart on
M
over which
E
locally trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
. Denote by
\kappa
the total local trivialisation of
T^{*}M
over
O
:

(4.11)

\kappa:\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(O)\rightarrow x(O)\times\mathbb{R}^{m},\quad\kappa(p,\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p})=(x(p),\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m}).

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfy (
4.3
). Let
\Lambda_{0}\subseteq\Lambda
be the finite set for which it holds that
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu}\cap\operatorname{supp}\varphi\neq\emptyset
,
\mu\in\Lambda_{0}
. Let
\mathbf{f}\in\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k}
and set
v_{\mu}^{j}:=\mathbf{f}(\mu)^{j}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
,
\mu\in\Lambda
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
. In view of (
2.4
), we have

\sigma^{j}=(|(x\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})^{\prime}|\circ x_{\mu})\tau_{\mu,l}^{j}\sigma_{\mu}^{l}\quad\mbox{on}\quad O_{\mu}\cap O,

where
(\sigma^{1},\ldots,\sigma^{k})
is the local frame for
E^{\vee}
over
O
induced by
\Phi_{x}
and
\tau_{\mu}=(\tau_{\mu,l}^{j})_{j,l}:O_{\mu}\cap O\rightarrow\operatorname{GL}(k,\mathbb{C})
is the transition map given by
\Phi_{x}\circ\Phi_{\mu}^{-1}(p,z)=(p,\tau_{\mu}(p)z)
,
p\in O_{\mu}\cap O
,
z\in\mathbb{C}^{k}
. Set
\phi_{\xi}:=e^{-i\,\cdot\,\xi}(\varphi\circ x^{-1})\in\mathcal{D}(x(O))
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, and notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}((\varphi\circ x^{-1})(u_{\mathbf{f}})^{j}_{\Phi_{x}})(\xi)

\displaystyle=\langle(u_{\mathbf{f}})^{j}_{\Phi_{x}},\phi_{\xi}\rangle=\langle u_{\mathbf{f}},(\phi_{\xi}\circ x)\sigma^{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}\langle v_{\mu}^{l},((\varphi_{\mu}\tau_{\mu,l}^{j})\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})(\phi_{\xi}\circ x\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})|(x\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})^{\prime}|\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}\langle(x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}v_{\mu}^{l},((\varphi_{\mu}\tau_{\mu,l}^{j})\circ x^{-1})\phi_{\xi}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}\mathcal{F}\left(((\varphi\varphi_{\mu}\tau_{\mu,l}^{j})\circ x^{-1})(x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}v_{\mu}^{l}\right)(\xi).

This implies that

(4.12)

\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}(u_{\mathbf{f}})\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{r;(\varphi\varphi_{\mu}\tau_{\mu,l}^{j})\circ x^{-1},V}((x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}v_{\mu}^{l}).

For each
\mu\in\Lambda_{0}
, we set

L_{\mu,O}:=\kappa_{\mu}(\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(O_{\mu}\cap O)\cap L)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{L}_{\mu,O}:=\kappa(\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(O_{\mu}\cap O)\cap L).

Of course,
L_{\mu,O}
and
\widetilde{L}_{\mu,O}
are closed conic subsets of
x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}\cap O)\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
and
x(O_{\mu}\cap O)\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
respectively. It is straightforward to check that
(x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}L_{\mu,O}=\widetilde{L}_{\mu,O}
, whence Theorem
3.21
yields that

(x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu,O}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}\cap O))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\widetilde{L}_{\mu,O}}(x(O_{\mu}\cap O))

is well-defined and continuous. Hence, for each
\mu\in\Lambda_{0}
and
j,l\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
, there are
C>0
, a continuous seminorm
\mathfrak{p}
on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}\cap O))
,
\widetilde{\phi}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\phi}_{J}\in\mathcal{D}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}\cap O))
and closed cones
\widetilde{V}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{V}_{J}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfying
(\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{\phi}_{j^{\prime}}\times\widetilde{V}_{j^{\prime}})\cap L_{\mu,O}=\emptyset
,
j^{\prime}=1,\ldots,J
, (of course, these depend on
\mu
,
j
and
l
) such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{r;(\varphi\varphi_{\mu}\tau_{\mu,l}^{j})\circ x^{-1},V}((x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}v_{\mu}^{l})

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(v_{\mu}^{l})+C\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{J}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\widetilde{\phi}_{j^{\prime}},\widetilde{V}_{j^{\prime}}}(v_{\mu}^{l})

\displaystyle\leq C\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}(v_{\mu}^{l})+C\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{J}\mathfrak{p}_{r;\widetilde{\phi}_{j^{\prime}},\widetilde{V}_{j^{\prime}}}(v_{\mu}^{l}),

where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}
is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
(since the restriction mapping
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}\cap O))
is continuous). In view of (
4.12
), this implies that (
4.10
) is well-defined and continuous (the required bounds for
\mathfrak{p}(u_{\mathbf{f}})
,
\mathfrak{p}
a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
, follow from the continuity of (
4.9
)). It is straightforward to check that
\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{f}_{u})=u
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
; i.e.,
\mathcal{R}
is a left inverse of (
4.8
). This immediately implies that (
4.8
) is a topological imbedding and that its image is complemented in
\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k}
. The image of (
4.8
) is closed since complemented subspaces always are.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

In the proof, we showed that for any partition of unity subordinated to
(O_{\mu})_{\mu\in\Lambda}
, the mapping (
4.10
) is well-defined continuous left inverse of (
4.8
). The analogous mapping for
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
is

\displaystyle\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M),\,\,\mathbf{f}\mapsto u_{\mathbf{f}},\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\langle u_{\mathbf{f}},\psi\rangle:=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda}\langle\mathbf{f}(\mu),(\varphi_{\mu}\psi_{\mu})\circ x_{\mu}^{-1}\rangle,\,\,\psi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM),\,\psi_{|O_{\mu}}=\psi_{\mu}\lambda^{x_{\mu}}.

Remark 4.4
.

The proposition immediately implies that
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
(resp.,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
) belongs to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
(resp.,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
) if and only if the seminorms (
4.5
) (resp., (
4.4
)) are finite when
(O,x)
runs through the charts of a fixed atlas of
M
such that
E
locally trivialises over each of the charts. Similarly, the topology of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
(resp.,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
) is given by the continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
(resp.,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
) together with the seminorms (
4.5
) (resp., (
4.4
)) when
(O,x)
runs through the charts of a fixed atlas of
M
such that
E
locally trivialises over each of the charts. In particular, this shows that when
M
is an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, the topology on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
is the same as the one we defined in Section
3
.

Remark 4.5
.

As a consequence of the proposition and Remark
3.9
, we infer that
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
,
(\chi,u)\mapsto\chi u
, is well-defined and hypocontinuous.

Since we can take
\{(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})\}_{\mu\in\Lambda}
to be countable, the proposition together with Corollary
3.10
,
[
35
, Theorem 5 and Theorem 6, p. 299]
and
[
36
, Theorem 1, p. 61, and Theorem 6, p. 62]
immediately yield the following result.

Corollary 4.6
.

The space
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
is complete, semi-reflexive and strictly webbed (in the sense of De Wilde).

Employing the above proposition together with Corollary
3.11
and Tychonoff’s theorem, we obtain the characterisation of the relatively compact sets in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
we announced in the introduction.

Corollary 4.7
.

Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
and let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
. Then
WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq L
if and only if
B
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
.

Corollary 4.8
.

The bounded subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
are metrisable when equipped with the induced topology. Consequently, if
\{u_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
, then there exists a subsequence
(u_{j_{k}})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
which converges in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
.

Proof.

It suffices to show the claim for
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(O)
, for
O
an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, because of Proposition
4.2
(by taking the cover
\{(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})\}_{\mu\in\Lambda}
to be countable). The claim for
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(O)
immediately follows from Proposition
3.7
in view of
[
50
, Theorem 1.7, p. 128]
since the weak and strong topologies coincide on the bounded subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O)
.
∎

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
\pi_{E}:E\rightarrow M
be a vector bundle of rank
k
and
\{(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})\}_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
a countable family of coordinate charts on
M
which cover
M
. Assume that
E
locally trivialises over each
O_{\mu}
via
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow O_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and let
(s_{\mu,1},\ldots,s_{\mu,k})
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, be the local frame of
E
over
O_{\mu}
induced by
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Let
(\varphi_{\mu})_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be a smooth partition of unity subordinated to
(O_{\mu})_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
. For each
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, let
(\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be a sequence of operators in
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})),\mathcal{D}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})))
which satisfies the properties as in the conclusion of Proposition
3.13
. Then the operators

(4.13)

P_{n}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E),\quad P_{n}(u):=\sum_{\mu=1}^{n}\varphi_{\mu}(\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}^{j})\circ x_{\mu}s_{\mu,j},\quad n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},

are well-defined, continuous and
P_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E))
. Furthermore, for any closed conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
, the set
\{P_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))
and
P_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))
. In particular,
\Gamma_{c}(E)
is sequentially dense in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
.

Proof.

For simpler notation, denote
u_{\mu}^{j}:=u_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}^{j}
and
\Phi_{\mu}:=\Phi_{x_{\mu}}
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
,
j=1,\ldots,k
. Let
(\sigma^{1}_{\mu},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{\mu})
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, be the frame induced by
\Phi_{\mu}
on
E^{\vee}
over
O_{\mu}
; notice that
\sigma^{j}_{\mu}(s_{\mu,l})=\delta^{j}_{l}\lambda^{x_{\mu}}
on
O_{\mu}
. Denote by
\kappa_{\mu}
the coordinate induced total local trivialisation (
4.6
) of
T^{*}M
over
O_{\mu}
and set
L_{\mu}:=\kappa_{\mu}(\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(O_{\mu})\cap L)
. Clearly (
4.13
) is well-defined and its continuity follows immediately from the continuity of
\widetilde{P}_{\mu,j}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
,
\mu,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. We show that for each fixed
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
,
P_{n}u\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
. Let
B
be a bounded subset of
\Gamma_{c}(E^{\vee})
. There is a compact subset
K
of
M
such that
B
is a bounded subset of
\Gamma_{K}(E^{\vee})
. There is
n_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu}\cap K=\emptyset
,
\mu>n_{0}
. For
\psi\in B
, write
\psi_{|O_{\mu}}=\psi_{\mu,j}\sigma^{j}_{\mu}
. When
n\geq n_{0}+1
, we have

\displaystyle\langle P_{n}u-u,\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{n}\langle(\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{j})\circ x_{\mu}s_{\mu,j},\varphi_{\mu}\psi_{\mu,j^{\prime}}\sigma^{j^{\prime}}_{\mu}\rangle-\sum_{\mu=1}^{n_{0}}\langle u,\varphi_{\mu}\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{n_{0}}\langle\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{j}-u_{\mu}^{j},(\varphi_{\mu}\psi_{\mu,j})\circ x_{\mu}^{-1}\rangle.

Since
\{(\varphi_{\mu}\psi_{\mu,j})\circ x_{\mu}^{-1}\,|\,\psi\in B\}
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
, we deduce
\sup_{\psi\in B}|\langle P_{n}u-u,\psi\rangle|\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Hence
P_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in the topology of simple convergence on
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E))
. The Banach-Steinhaus theorem
[
50
, Theorem 4.5, p. 85]
together with the fact that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
is Montel now yield that
P_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E))
. The latter implies that
\{P_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E))
. Hence, given a closed conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
, in order to prove that
\{P_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))
it suffices to show that the seminorms (
4.5
) are uniformly bounded when
u
varies in a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
. Let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
, let
(O,x)
be a coordinate chart on
M
over which
E
locally trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and the closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfy (
4.3
). We denote by
\tau_{\mu}=(\tau_{\mu,l}^{j})_{j,l}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O_{\mu}\cap O)\rightarrow\operatorname{GL}(k,\mathbb{C})
the transition map given by
\Phi_{x}\circ\Phi_{\mu}^{-1}(p,z)=(p,\tau_{\mu}(p)z)
,
p\in O_{\mu}\cap O
,
z\in\mathbb{C}^{k}
. Define
\phi_{\xi}:=e^{-i\,\cdot\,\xi}(\varphi\circ x^{-1})
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
; clearly
\phi_{\xi}\in\mathcal{D}(x(O))
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
. There is
n_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu}\cap\operatorname{supp}\varphi=\emptyset
,
\mu>n_{0}
. For
n\geq n_{0}+1
and
u\in B
, we employ (
2.4
) to infer

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}((\varphi\circ x^{-1})(P_{n}u)^{j}_{\Phi_{x}})(\xi)

\displaystyle=\langle(P_{n}u)^{j}_{\Phi_{x}},\phi_{\xi}\rangle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{n}\langle(\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{t})\circ x_{\mu}s_{\mu,t},(\phi_{\xi}\circ x)\varphi_{\mu}\sigma^{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{n_{0}}\langle(\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{t})\circ x_{\mu}s_{\mu,t},(\phi_{\xi}\circ x)(|(x\circ x^{-1}_{\mu})^{\prime}|\circ x_{\mu})\varphi_{\mu}\tau^{j}_{\mu,l}\sigma^{l}_{\mu}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{n_{0}}\langle\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{l},(\phi_{\xi}\circ x\circ x^{-1}_{\mu})((\varphi_{\mu}\tau^{j}_{\mu,l})\circ x^{-1}_{\mu})|(x\circ x^{-1}_{\mu})^{\prime}|\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{n_{0}}\langle(x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{l},\phi_{\xi}((\varphi_{\mu}\tau^{j}_{\mu,l})\circ x^{-1})\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{n_{0}}\mathcal{F}\left(((\varphi\varphi_{\mu}\tau^{j}_{\mu,l})\circ x^{-1})(x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{l}\right)(\xi).

We deduce

\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}(P_{n}u)\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\sum_{\mu=1}^{n_{0}}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{r;(\varphi\varphi_{\mu}\tau^{j}_{\mu,l})\circ x^{-1},V}((x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{l}).

With
\kappa
as in (
4.11
), we set
L_{\mu,O}:=\kappa_{\mu}(\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(O_{\mu}\cap O)\cap L)
and
\widetilde{L}_{\mu,O}:=\kappa(\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(O_{\mu}\cap O)\cap L)
. Then
(x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}L_{\mu,O}=\widetilde{L}_{\mu,O}
and Theorem
3.21
yields that
(x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu,O}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}\cap O))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\widetilde{L}_{\mu,O}}(x(O_{\mu}\cap O))
is continuous. Since the map
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
,
u\mapsto u^{l}_{\mu}
, is continuous, the set
\{\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{l}\,|\,u\in B,\,n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\}
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
and consequently in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu,O}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}\cap O))
as well. The above now implies that
\sup_{n\geq n_{0}+1}\sup_{u\in B}\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,L}(P_{n}u)<\infty
which completes the proof for the boundedness of
\{P_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))
. It remains to show that
P_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))
. Since we show that the convergence holds in
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E))
, it suffices to show that for each precompact subset
B
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
,
\sup_{u\in B}\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}(P_{n}u-u)\rightarrow 0
where
\varphi
and
V
are as above. Similarly as above, for all
u\in B
and
n\geq n_{0}+1
, we have

\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}(P_{n}u-u)\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\sum_{\mu=1}^{n_{0}}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{r;(\varphi\varphi_{\mu}\tau^{j}_{\mu,l})\circ x^{-1},V}((x_{\mu}\circ x^{-1})^{*}(\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}u_{\mu}^{l}-u_{\mu}^{l})).

Employing the same reasoning as before, one shows that
\sup_{u\in B}\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}(P_{n}u-u)\rightarrow 0
which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

If for each
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
\{\widetilde{P}_{\mu,n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
are defined as in Proposition
3.13
, then it is straightforward to verify that for each
u\in\Gamma^{0}(E)
,
P_{n}u\rightarrow u
in
\Gamma^{0}(E)
.

Remark 4.11
.

The density we just proved immediately shows that the following continuous inclusions are also dense:

(4.14)

\displaystyle H_{\operatorname{loc}}^{r}(M)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M),\quad H_{\operatorname{loc}}^{r}(M;E)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E);

(4.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(M)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{L}(M)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(M;E)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{L}(M;E),\quad\mbox{when}\,\,r_{2}\geq r_{1}.

4.2.
The dual of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)

Our next goal is to find the strong dual of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M)
and of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
. As in the Euclidean case, for an open conic subset
W
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
and
r\in\mathbb{R}
we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)

\displaystyle:=\{u\in H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M)\,|\,WF(u)\subseteq W\}\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)

\displaystyle:=\{u\in H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)\,|\,WF(u)\subseteq W\}.

Additionally, for a closed conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
and a compact set
K\subseteq M
satisfying
\pi_{T^{*}M}(L)\subseteq K
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M)

\displaystyle:=\{u\in H^{r}_{K}(M)\,|\,WF(u)\subseteq L\}\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)

\displaystyle:=\{u\in H^{r}_{K}(M;E)\,|\,WF(u)\subseteq L\}.

Of course, when
M
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)
coincide as sets with their Euclidean counterparts we defined in Subsection
3.2
. As in the Euclidean case, we first define locally convex topologies on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)
and then define the topology on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)
as inductive limits of these spaces.

Let
(O,x)
be a chart on
M
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and let
\emptyset\neq V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
be a closed cone such that (
4.3
) holds true. When
u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M)
, we have
(\operatorname{supp}(\varphi\circ x^{-1})\times V)\cap WF(u_{x})=\emptyset
and hence

(4.16)

\mathfrak{q}^{x}_{\nu;\varphi,V}(u):=\sup_{\xi\in V}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}|\mathcal{F}((\varphi\circ x^{-1})u_{x})(\xi)|<\infty,\quad u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M),\,\nu>0.

We equip
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M)
with the locally convex topology induced by (any) norm on
H^{r}_{K}(M)
together with all seminorms
\mathfrak{q}^{x}_{\nu;\varphi,V}
, for all
\nu>0
, all charts
(O,x)
and all
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and
\emptyset\neq V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
as above. Analogously, given a chart
(O,x)
over which
E
trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
, a function
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and a closed cone
\emptyset\neq V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
which satisfy (
4.3
), it holds that
(\operatorname{supp}(\varphi\circ x^{-1})\times V)\cap WF(u_{\Phi_{x}}^{j})=\emptyset
,
j=1,\ldots,k
,
u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)
, and thus

(4.17)

\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi,V}(u):=\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\sup_{\xi\in V}\langle\xi\rangle^{\nu}|\mathcal{F}((\varphi\circ x^{-1})u_{\Phi_{x}}^{j})(\xi)|<\infty,\quad u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E),\,\nu>0.

We equip
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)
with the locally convex topology induced by (any) norm on
H^{r}_{K}(M;E)
together with all seminorms
\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi,V}
, for all
\nu>0
, all charts
(O,x)
over which
E
locally trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and all
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and
\emptyset\neq V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
as above.

Proposition 4.12
.

Let
\pi_{E}:E\rightarrow M
be a vector bundle of rank
k
,
K\subset\subset M
and
L
a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
which satisfy
\pi_{T^{*}M}(L)\subseteq K
. Let
\{(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})\}_{\mu\in\Lambda}
be a finite family of coordinate charts on
M
which cover
K
such that
E
locally trivialises over
O_{\mu}
via
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow O_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
,
\mu\in\Lambda
. Let
\kappa_{\mu}
,
\mu\in\Lambda
, be the total local trivialisation of
T^{*}M
over
O_{\mu}
as in (
4.6
). Let
\varphi_{\mu}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
,
\mu\in\Lambda
, be nonnegative and such that
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu}\subseteq O_{\mu}
and
\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda}\varphi_{\mu}=1
on a neighbourhood of
K
. For each
\mu\in\Lambda
,
K_{\mu}:=x_{\mu}(K\cap\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu})
is a compact subset of
x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})
and
L_{\mu}:=\kappa_{\mu}(\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu})\cap L)
is a closed conic subset of
x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
which satisfies
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(L_{\mu})\subseteq K_{\mu}
. The distribution
u\in H^{r}_{K}(M;E)
belongs to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)
if and only if
\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}_{\nu;\varphi,V}(u)<\infty
for all
\nu>0
,
\mu\in\Lambda
and for all
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
and closed cones
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
which satisfy (
4.3
). Furthermore, the map

(4.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)\rightarrow\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu};K_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k},\,\,u\mapsto\mathbf{f}_{u},\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{u}(\mu):=((\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})u_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}^{1},\ldots,(\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})u_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}^{k}),\,\mu\in\Lambda,

is a well-defined topological imbedding whose image is closed and complemented in
\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu};K_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Proposition
4.2
. The only notable differences are that instead of Theorem
3.21
, one now employs Corollary
3.26
and, instead of (
4.10
), one now shows that

(4.19)

\displaystyle\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu};K_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E),\,\,\mathbf{f}\mapsto u_{\mathbf{f}},\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\langle u_{\mathbf{f}},\psi\rangle:=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda}\langle\mathbf{f}(\mu)^{j},\psi_{\mu,j}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1}\rangle,\,\,\psi\in\Gamma_{c}(E^{\vee}),\,\psi_{|O_{\mu}}=\psi_{\mu,j}\sigma_{\mu}^{j},

is a well-defined continuous left inverse of (
4.18
).
∎

Remark 4.13
.

The proposition implies that the topology of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M)
) is given by (any) norm on
H^{r}_{K}(M;E)
(resp.,
H^{r}_{K}(M)
) together with the seminorms (
4.17
) (resp., (
4.16
)) when
(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})
,
\mu\in\Lambda
, are as in the proposition. In particular, if
M
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, the topology on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M)
is the same as in the Euclidean case (employ this with the global trivial chart).

In view of Proposition
3.14
, we immediately deduce the following result.

Corollary 4.14
.

The space
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)
is a reflexive Fréchet space.

We define locally convex topologies on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)
in the same way as in the Euclidean case. Namely, first we notice that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)=\bigcup_{(L,K)\in\mathfrak{W}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)=\bigcup_{(L,K)\in\mathfrak{W}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)
, where
\mathfrak{W}
is the set of all pairs
(L,K)
with
K\subset\subset M
and
L
a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
satisfying
L\subseteq W
and
\pi_{T^{*}M}(L)\subseteq K
and then we define locally convex topologies on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)
by

\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
(L,K)\in\mathfrak{W}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
(L,K)\in\mathfrak{W}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E);

as before,
\mathfrak{W}
is a directed set by inclusion and the linking mappings in the inductive limits are the canonical inclusions. As in the Euclidean case, one can find a sequence
(L_{j},K_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\subseteq\mathfrak{W}
which satisfies (
3.28
) with
M
in place of
U
; whence

\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
j\rightarrow\infty\end{subarray}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L_{j};K_{j}}(M)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
j\rightarrow\infty\end{subarray}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L_{j};K_{j}}(M;E)\quad\mbox{topologically}

(arguing by contradiction, it is straightforward to check that if
L\subseteq W
is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
such that
\pi_{T^{*}M}(L)
is compact, then there is
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
L\subseteq\operatorname{int}L_{j}
). Consequently,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)
are
(LF)
-spaces and thus both barrelled and bornological. Of course, if
M
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, then the topology on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)
is the same as in the Euclidean case.

Remark 4.15
.

Arguing as in Remark
3.15
, one shows the following topological identities:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{\emptyset;K}(M;E)=\Gamma_{K}(E)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(K)\backslash 0;K}(M;E)=H^{r}_{K}(M;E),\quad\mbox{for any}\,\,K\subset\subset M;

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{\emptyset}(M;E)=\Gamma_{c}(E),\quad\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{T^{*}M\backslash 0}(M;E)=H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E).

Remark 4.16
.

Analogously as in Remark
3.16
one shows that
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)\times\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)
,
(\chi,u)\mapsto\chi u
, is continuous and
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)\times\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)
,
(\chi,u)\mapsto\chi u
, is hypocontinuous.

As in the Euclidean setting, the identity operator on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)
can be approximated by regularising operators in the topology of precompact convergence.

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
\pi_{E}:E\rightarrow M
be a vector bundle of rank
k
.

(i)

Let
K
and
\widetilde{K}
be compact sets in
M
satisfying
K\subseteq\operatorname{int}\widetilde{K}
and let
L
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
such that
\pi_{T^{*}M}(L)\subseteq K
. There are continuous operators
P_{n}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E_{\operatorname{int}\widetilde{K}})
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, such that
P_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E),\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;\widetilde{K}}(M;E))
.

(ii)

Let
W
be an open conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. There are continuous operators
P_{n}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E)
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, such that
P_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E))
. In particular,
\Gamma_{c}(E)
is sequentially dense in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)
.

Proof.

To prove
(i)
, pick finite number of charts
(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
, which cover
K
such that
O_{\mu}\subseteq\operatorname{int}\widetilde{K}
and
E
locally trivialises over
O_{\mu}
via
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow O_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
. For each
\mu
, let
(s_{\mu,1},\ldots,s_{\mu,k})
be the frame of
E
over
O_{\mu}
induced by
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}
. Choose nonnegative
\varphi_{\mu}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
, such that
\sum_{\mu=1}^{l}\varphi_{\mu}=1
on a neighbourhood of
K
. Choose
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\subseteq\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
as in Proposition
3.13
and pick
n_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
\operatorname{supp}\chi_{n}+x_{\mu}(\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu})\subseteq x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})
, for all
n\geq n_{0}
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
. For
n\geq n_{0}
, we define

P_{n}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E_{\operatorname{int}\widetilde{K}}),\quad P_{n}(u):=\sum_{\mu=1}^{l}(\chi_{n}*((\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})u^{j}_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}))\circ x_{\mu}s_{\mu,j}.

It is straightforward to check that
P_{n}
is well-defined and continuous. In view of the Banach-Steinhaus theorem, to verify that
P_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E),\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;\widetilde{K}}(M;E))
, it suffices to show that for each
u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;K}(M;E)
,
P_{n}(u)\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L;\widetilde{K}}(M;E)
. This can be done similarly as in the proof of Proposition
4.9
(cf. Proposition
3.17
).

We now address
(ii)
. Let
(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, be relatively compact charts on
M
such that
E
locally trivialises over
O_{\mu}
via
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow O_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
,
x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})=B(0,2)
and
O^{\prime}_{\mu}:=x_{\mu}^{-1}(B(0,1))
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, cover
M
. Take a partition of unity
(\varphi_{\mu})_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
subordinated to
(O^{\prime}_{\mu})_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
. Pick
(L_{j},K_{j})\in\mathfrak{W}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, which satisfy (
3.28
) with
M
in place of
U
. For each
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, pick
\psi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{int}K_{j+1})
such that
0\leq\psi_{j}\leq 1
and
\psi_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
K_{j}
. Let
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\subseteq\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
be as in Proposition
3.13
and notice that
\operatorname{supp}\chi_{n}+x_{\mu}(\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu})\subseteq x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})
, for all
n,\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. We define

P_{n}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E),\,P_{n}(u):=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\left(\chi_{n}*\left(((\psi_{n}\varphi_{\mu})\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})u^{l}_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}\right)\right)\circ x_{\mu}s_{\mu,l},\,\,n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

It is straightforward to check that
P_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, are well-defined and continuous. Let
u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)
be arbitrary but fixed. There is
j_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L_{j_{0}};K_{j_{0}}}(M;E)
. Set
\Lambda_{j_{0}}:=\{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\,|\,\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu}\cap K_{j_{0}}\neq\emptyset\}
; of course,
\Lambda_{j_{0}}
is finite. For
n\geq j_{0}
, it holds that

P_{n}(u)=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{j_{0}}}(\chi_{n}*((\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})u^{l}_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}))\circ x_{\mu}s_{\mu,l},

and, in view of the proof of
(i)
, the right-hand side tends to
u
in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L_{j_{0}};K_{j^{\prime}_{0}}}(M;E)
as
n\rightarrow\infty
where
j^{\prime}_{0}>j_{0}
is large enough so that
\bigcup_{\mu\in\Lambda_{j_{0}}}O_{\mu}\subseteq\operatorname{int}K_{j^{\prime}_{0}}
(such
j^{\prime}_{0}
exists since the
O_{\mu}
’s are relatively compact). We deduce that
P_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname{Id}
in the topology of simple convergence on
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E))
and, as
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)
is barrelled, the Banach-Steinhaus theorem
[
50
, Theorem 4.5, p. 85]
verifies that the convergence also holds in
\mathcal{L}_{p}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E))
.
∎

Remark 4.18
.

Proposition
4.17
immediately shows that the following continuous inclusions are also dense:

(4.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(M)\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M)\subseteq H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M),\quad\Gamma_{c}(E)\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{W}(M;E)\subseteq H^{r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E);

(4.21)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r_{2}}_{W}(M)\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime r_{1}}_{W}(M)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{E}^{\prime r_{2}}_{W}(M;E)\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime r_{1}}_{W}(M;E),\quad\mbox{when}\,\,r_{2}\geq r_{1}.

Theorem 4.19
.

Let
\pi_{E}:E\rightarrow M
be a vector bundle of rank
k
and let
L
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Then

(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M))^{\prime}_{b}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(M;DM)\quad\mbox{and}\quad(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}_{b}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(M;E^{\vee})\quad\mbox{topologically}.

Proof.

We only consider the bundle-valued case. In view of (
4.14
),
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}\subseteq H^{-r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E^{\vee})
. First we show that
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}_{b}
is an
(LF)
-space. Proposition
4.2
together with Theorem
3.20
imply that
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}_{b}
is topologically isomorphic to a complemented subspace of a countable locally convex direct sum of
(LF)
-spaces (the strong dual of a topological product is the locally convex direct sum of the strong duals; see
[
35
, p. 287]
). Since a countable direct sum of
(LF)
-spaces is an
(LF)
-space and a quotient of an
(LF)
-space by a closed subspace is again an
(LF)
-space, we deduce that
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}_{b}
is an
(LF)
-space. Thus, in view of the open mapping theorem for
(LF)
-spaces
[
36
, Theorem 4, p. 43]
, to prove the desired result it suffices to show that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(M;E^{\vee})=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}
as sets and the inclusion
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(M;E^{\vee})\subseteq(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}_{b}
is continuous.

First we show that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(M;E^{\vee})\subseteq(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}_{b}
continuously. Let
\widetilde{K}\subset\subset M
and let
\widetilde{L}
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
satisfying
\pi_{T^{*}M}(\widetilde{L})\subseteq\widetilde{K}
and
\widetilde{L}\subseteq\check{L}^{c}
. Pick a finite number of relatively compact coordinate charts
(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
, which cover
\widetilde{K}
and such that
E
trivialises over
O_{\mu}
via
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow O_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
. Let
(s_{\mu,1},\ldots,s_{\mu,k})
be the frame for
E
over
O_{\mu}
induced by
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}
and let
(\sigma^{1}_{\mu},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{\mu})
be the induced frame for
E^{\vee}
over
O_{\mu}
. For
v\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E^{\vee})
, let
v_{\mu,j}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
be the distribution
\langle v_{\mu,j},\phi\rangle:=\langle v,\phi\circ x_{\mu}s_{\mu,j}\rangle
,
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
. Pick nonnegative
\varphi_{\mu}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
, such that
\sum_{\mu=1}^{l}\varphi_{\mu}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\widetilde{K}
. Let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(M;E^{\vee})
. Then,

(4.22)

\langle v,\psi\rangle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{l}\langle v,\varphi_{\mu}\psi^{j}_{\mu}s_{\mu,j}\rangle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{l}\langle(\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})v_{\mu,j},\psi^{j}_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1}\rangle,\quad\psi\in\Gamma_{c}(E),\,v\in B.

In view of Proposition
4.12
,
B_{\mu,j}:=\{(\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})v_{\mu,j}\,|\,v\in B\}
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L}_{\mu};\widetilde{K}_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
, for every
\mu
and
j
with
\widetilde{K}_{\mu}
and
\widetilde{L}_{\mu}
as in Proposition
4.12
. Theorem
3.20
and Lemma
3.18

(ii)
show that
B_{\mu,j}
is an equicontinuous subset of
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})))^{\prime}
with
L_{\mu}
as in Proposition
4.2
. Since
\Gamma_{c}(E)
is dense in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
,
u\mapsto u_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}^{j}
, is continuous (cf. Proposition
4.2
), (
4.22
) verifies that
B
is an equicontinuous subset of
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}
. Hence
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(M;E^{\vee})\subseteq(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}_{b}
and the inclusion maps bounded sets into bounded sets; whence, it is continuous since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(M;E^{\vee})
is Fréchet. We deduce that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(M;E^{\vee})\subseteq(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}_{b}
continuously.

It remains to show that
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(M;E^{\vee})
. For
v\in(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}\backslash\{0\}
, (
4.14
) implies
v\in H^{-r}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E^{\vee})
. For the compact set
\operatorname{supp}v
, we choose
(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})
and
\varphi_{\mu}
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
, as above. We make the following

Claim.
Let
(O,x)
be a chart on
M
over which
E
locally trivialises via
\Phi:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and let
(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k})
be the induced frame for
E
over
O
. Set
L_{O,x}:=\kappa(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(O)\cap L)
where
\kappa
is the total local trivialisation of
T^{*}M
over
O
(cf. (
4.11
)). For
v\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E^{\vee})
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
, let
v_{\Phi,j}
be the distribution on
x(O)
defined by
\langle v_{\Phi,j},\phi\rangle:=\langle v,\phi\circ x\,s_{j}\rangle
,
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(x(O))
. Then for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
, the map
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{\prime}_{b}\rightarrow(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{O,x}}(x(O)))^{\prime}_{b}
,
v\mapsto(\varphi\circ x^{-1})v_{\Phi,j}
, is well-defined and continuous.

We first show how the Claim implies the desired result. The Claim together with Theorem
3.20
immediately imply that
(\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})v_{\mu,j}\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}_{\mu}^{c}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
,
j=1,\ldots,k
, with
L_{\mu}
as in Proposition
4.2
. Set

\widetilde{K}:=\bigcup_{\mu,j}x_{\mu}^{-1}(\operatorname{supp}((\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})v_{\mu,j}))\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{L}:=\bigcup_{\mu,j}\kappa_{\mu}^{-1}(WF((\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})v_{\mu,j})).

Then
\widetilde{K}=\operatorname{supp}v
and
\widetilde{L}
is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
satisfying
\widetilde{L}\subseteq\check{L}^{c}
and
\pi_{T^{*}M}(\widetilde{L})\subseteq\widetilde{K}
. Furthermore,
(\varphi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})v_{\mu,j}\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L}_{\mu};\widetilde{K}_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
with
\widetilde{K}_{\mu}
and
\widetilde{L}_{\mu}
as in Proposition
4.12
. Notice that (
4.22
) is valid for
v
and the right-hand side is exactly the map (
4.19
) (with
E^{\vee}
in place of
E
); whence, the proof of Proposition
4.12
implies
v\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(M;E^{\vee})
and the proof is complete.

It remains to show the Claim. In the same way as in the proof of Proposition
4.2
, one shows that for each
j_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
, the continuous map

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(x(O))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E),\,\,u\mapsto\widetilde{u}\,\,\mbox{with}\,\,\langle\widetilde{u},\psi\rangle:=\langle u,(\varphi\psi_{j_{0}})\circ x^{-1}\rangle,\,\psi\in\Gamma_{c}(E^{\vee}),\,\psi_{|O}=\psi_{j}\sigma^{j},

restricts to a well-defined and continuous map
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L_{O,x}}(x(O))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
(
(\sigma^{1},\ldots,\sigma^{k})
is the frame for
E^{\vee}
over
O
induced by
\Phi
). It is straightforward to verify that its transpose is the map in the Claim which implies the validity of the Claim.
∎

Later, we are going to need the following technical result. Its proof follows immediately by applying Theorem
4.19
, Lemma
3.18

(ii)
, Proposition
4.12
and Proposition
4.2
and we omit it.

Lemma 4.20
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{K}\subset\subset M
and let the closed conic subsets
L
and
\widetilde{L}
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
satisfy
\pi_{T^{*}M}(\widetilde{L})\subseteq\widetilde{K}
and
\widetilde{L}\subseteq L^{c}
. Then every bounded subset of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{\widetilde{L};\widetilde{K}}(M;E)
is equicontinuous with respect to the duality
\langle\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L^{c}}(M;E),\mathcal{D}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}}(M;E^{\vee})\rangle
.

4.3.
The topology of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
is compatible with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E)

We now show that the Hörmander space
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E):=\{u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\,|\,WF(u)\subseteq L\}
is the projective limit of the spaces
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
,
r\in\mathbb{R}
. The topology of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E)
is given by the continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
together with all seminorms
\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi,V}
(see (
4.17
)) for all
\nu>0
, all charts
(O,x)
over which
E
locally trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi^{-1}_{E}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and all
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and closed cones
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfying (
4.3
); we refer to
[
8
,
5
]
for its topological properties.

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Then
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E)=\bigcap_{r\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longleftarrow\\
r\rightarrow\infty\end{subarray}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\quad\mbox{topologically},

where the linking mappings in the projective limit are the canonical inclusions (
4.15
).

Proof.

Denote the projective limit by
\mathfrak{P}
. Clearly,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E)\subseteq\mathfrak{P}
continuously. To show the opposite inclusion, it suffices to show that each seminorm
\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi,V}(u)
,
u\in\mathfrak{P}
, is bounded by a continuous seminorms on some
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
. Employing a standard compactness argument, we find relatively compact open sets
O_{1},\ldots,O_{q}
which cover
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
and satisfy
\overline{O_{j}}\subseteq O
,
j=1,\ldots,q
, and for each
O_{j}
we find open cones
V_{j,h}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\eta^{(j,h)},\varepsilon_{j,h})
,
h=1,\ldots,\mu_{j}
, for some
\eta^{(j,h)}\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
and
\varepsilon_{j,h}>0
, such that
V\backslash\{0\}\subseteq\bigcup_{h=1}^{\mu_{j}}V_{j,h}
and

\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p})\in T^{*}O_{j}\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\overline{V_{j,h}}\}\cap L=\emptyset,\quad h=1,\ldots,\mu_{j},\,j=1,\ldots,q.

Pick nonnegative
\psi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{j})
,
j=1,\ldots,q
, such that
\sum_{j=1}^{q}\psi_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
. Notice that
\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi,V}(u)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{q}\max_{1\leq h\leq\mu_{j}}\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi\psi_{j},V_{j,h}}(u)
. The Sobolev imbedding theorem
[
1
, Theorem 4.12, p. 85]
implies that there is
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi\psi_{j},V_{j,h}}(u)

\displaystyle\leq C\max_{1\leq l\leq k}\max_{|\alpha|\leq 1+m/2}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}\left(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\nu}\mathcal{F}\left(((\varphi\psi_{j})\circ x^{-1})u^{l}_{\Phi_{x}}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(V_{j,h})}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\max_{1\leq l\leq k}\max_{|\alpha|\leq 1+m/2}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\nu}\mathcal{F}(\phi_{j,\alpha}u^{l}_{\Phi_{x}}))\|_{L^{2}(V_{j,h})},

where we denoted
\phi_{j,\alpha}(t)=t^{\alpha}(\varphi\psi_{j})(x^{-1}(t))
,
t\in x(O_{j})
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
.
10
10

10

The choice of
V_{j,h}
was so that they satisfy the domain conditions for the Sobolev imbedding theorem.
Hence,
\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi\psi_{j},V_{j,h}}(u)\leq C^{\prime}\max_{|\alpha|\leq 1+m/2}\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\phi_{j,\alpha}\circ x,V_{j,h}}(u)
and the proof is complete.
∎

Remark 4.22
.

The proposition implies the well-known identity
WF(u)=\overline{\bigcup_{r\in\mathbb{R}}WF^{r}(u)}\backslash 0
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
. Indeed, the inclusion “
\supseteq
” is trivial and the opposite inclusion follows by applying Proposition
4.21
with
L:=\overline{\bigcup_{r\in\mathbb{R}}WF^{r}(u)}\backslash 0
.

4.4.
Pullback by smooth maps on vector bundles

We are now ready to show our main result on the pullback.

Theorem 4.23
.

Let
f:M\rightarrow N
be a smooth map between the manifolds
M
and
N
with dimensions
m
and
n
respectively and let
L
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}N\backslash 0
which satisfies
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
.

(i)

The pullback
