f^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(N)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
,
f^{*}(u)=u\circ f
, uniquely extends to a well-defined and continuous mapping
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(N)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(M)
when
r_{2}-r_{1}>n/2
and
r_{2}>n/2
. If
f
has constant rank
l\geq 1
, then this is valid when
r_{2}-r_{1}\geq(n-l)/2
and
r_{2}>(n-l)/2
. When
f
is a submersion,
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(N)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(M)
is well-defined and continuous even when
r_{2}\geq r_{1}
. Consequently, if
f
is a diffeomorphism, then
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(N)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{f^{*}L}(M)
is a topological isomorphism for each
r\in\mathbb{R}
.

(ii)

Let
(E,\pi_{E},N)
be a vector bundle of rank
k
. The pullback
f^{*}:\Gamma(E)\rightarrow\Gamma(f^{*}E)
,
f^{*}(u)(p)=(p,u\circ f(p))
,
p\in M
, uniquely extends to a well-defined and continuous mapping
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(N;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(M;f^{*}E)
when
r_{2}-r_{1}>n/2
and
r_{2}>n/2
. If
f
has constant rank
l\geq 1
, then this is valid when
r_{2}-r_{1}\geq(n-l)/2
and
r_{2}>(n-l)/2
. When
f
is a submersion,
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(N;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(M;f^{*}E)
is well-defined and continuous even when
r_{2}\geq r_{1}
. Consequently, if
f
is a diffeomorphism, then
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(N;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{f^{*}L}(M;f^{*}E)
is a topological isomorphism for each
r\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

We only show
(ii)
as the proof of
(i)
is similar. The uniqueness of the extension follows from Proposition
4.9
. To show the existence, pick a cover of charts
\{(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})\}_{\mu\in\Lambda}
of
M
and corresponding charts
(U_{\mu},y_{\mu})
,
\mu\in\Lambda
, in
N
such that
f(O_{\mu})\subseteq U_{\mu}
,
\mu\in\Lambda
, and
E
locally trivialises over
U_{\mu}
via
\Phi_{\mu}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(U_{\mu})\rightarrow U_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
,
\mu\in\Lambda
(of course
U_{\mu}
,
\mu\in\Lambda
, only cover
f(M)
). Denote,
\hat{f}_{\mu}:=y_{\mu}\circ f_{|O_{\mu}}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1}:x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow y_{\mu}(U_{\mu})
,
\mu\in\Lambda
; if
f
has constant rank
l
then the same holds for
\hat{f}_{\mu}
,
\mu\in\Lambda
, as well. For each
\mu\in\Lambda
, denote by
\kappa_{\mu}
and
\iota_{\mu}
the total local trivialisations of
T^{*}M
and
T^{*}N
over
O_{\mu}
and
U_{\mu}
induced by
x_{\mu}
and
y_{\mu}
respectively (cf. (
4.6
)). Set
(f^{*}L)_{\mu}:=\kappa_{\mu}(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(O_{\mu})\cap f^{*}L)
and
L_{\mu}:=\iota_{\mu}(\pi_{T^{*}N}^{-1}(U_{\mu})\cap L)
and notice that (cf. (
3.42
))

(4.23)

\hat{f}_{\mu}^{*}L_{\mu}=(f^{*}L)_{\mu}\quad\mbox{and}\quad L_{\mu}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\hat{f}_{\mu}}=\emptyset,\quad\mu\in\Lambda.

Let
(\widetilde{U}_{\nu},\widetilde{y}_{\nu})
,
\nu\in\Theta
, be charts on
N
over which
E
locally trivialises and which cover
N\backslash\bigcup_{\mu\in\Lambda}U_{\mu}
(
\Theta=\emptyset
if
N=\bigcup_{\mu\in\Lambda}U_{\mu}
). Let
11
11

11

Here and throughout the rest of the article we employ the principle of nullary Cartesian product
A\times\prod_{\alpha\in\emptyset}B_{\alpha}=A
.

\mathcal{I}_{N}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(N;E)\rightarrow\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L_{\mu}}(y_{\mu}(U_{\mu}))^{k}\times\prod_{\nu\in\Theta}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L_{\nu}}(\widetilde{y}_{\nu}(\widetilde{U}_{\nu}))^{k}
be the map (
4.8
) for
N
and the cover
\{(U_{\mu},y_{\mu})\}_{\mu\in\Lambda}\cup\{(\widetilde{U}_{\nu},\widetilde{y}_{\nu})\}_{\nu\in\Theta}
. Consider the map

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}:\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L_{\mu}}(y_{\mu}(U_{\mu}))^{k}\times\prod_{\nu\in\Theta}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L_{\nu}}(\widetilde{y}_{\nu}(\widetilde{U}_{\nu}))^{k}\rightarrow\prod_{\mu\in\Lambda}\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{(f^{*}L)_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))^{k},

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}(\mathbf{f})(\mu)^{j}=\hat{f}^{*}_{\mu}(\mathbf{f}(\mu)^{j}),\quad\mu\in\Lambda,\,j\in\{1,\ldots,k\};

in view of Theorem
3.21
and (
4.23
), the map is well-defined and continuous. Pick a partition of unity
(\varphi_{\mu})_{\mu\in\Lambda}
subordinated to
(O_{\mu})_{\mu\in\Lambda}
and denote by
\mathcal{R}_{M}
the map (
4.10
) with
f^{*}L
,
f^{*}E
and
r_{1}
in place of
L
,
E
and
r
respectively. We infer that
\mathcal{R}_{M}\circ\mathbf{F}\circ\mathcal{I}_{N}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(N;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(M;f^{*}E)
is well-defined and continuous. It is straightforward to verify that
\mathcal{R}_{M}\circ\mathbf{F}\circ\mathcal{I}_{N}(u)(p)=(p,u\circ f(p))
,
p\in M
, for
u\in\Gamma(E)
; whence, the desired extension is
f^{*}=\mathcal{R}_{M}\circ\mathbf{F}\circ\mathcal{I}_{N}
.
∎

Remark 4.24
.

Assume the same as in Theorem
4.23

(ii)
. Let
(O,x)
and
(U,y)
be charts on
M
and
N
such that
E
trivialises over
U
via
\Phi:\pi_{E}^{-1}(U)\rightarrow U\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and
f(O)\subseteq U
and let
\kappa
and
\iota
be the total local trivialisations of
T^{*}M
and
T^{*}N
over
O
and
U
induced by
x
and
y
respectively. Denoting
L_{U,y}:=\iota(\pi_{T^{*}N}^{-1}(U)\cap L)
,
(f^{*}L)_{O,x}:=\kappa(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(O)\cap f^{*}L)
and
\hat{f}_{x,y}:=y\circ f_{|O}\circ x^{-1}:x(O)\rightarrow y(U)
, it holds that
\hat{f}_{x,y}^{*}L_{U,y}=(f^{*}L)_{O,x}
and
L_{U,y}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\hat{f}_{x,y}}=\emptyset
. Consequently, Theorem
3.21
implies that
\hat{f}_{x,y}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L_{U,y}}(y(U))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{(f^{*}L)_{O,x}}(x(O))
is well defined and continuous. Furthermore

(4.24)

(f^{*}u)_{(f^{*}\Phi)_{x}}^{j}=\hat{f}^{*}_{x,y}(u_{\Phi_{y}}^{j}),\quad u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(N;E),\,j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}.

It is straightforward to verify (
4.24
) for
u\in\Gamma(E)
and the general case follows by density.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.23

(i)
, the analogous statement is that
(f^{*}u)_{x}=\hat{f}_{x,y}^{*}(u_{y})
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(N)
.

Remark 4.25
.

If
f:M\rightarrow N
has constant rank
0
, then we can argue as in Remark
3.23
to show that there are at most countably many pairwise disjoint open sets
O_{j}\subseteq M
,
j\in\Lambda
, whose union is
M
and distinct points
q_{j}\in N
,
j\in\Lambda
, such that
f(p)=q_{j}
,
p\in O_{j}
,
j\in\Lambda
. Notice that
\mathcal{N}_{f}=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda}(\{q_{j}\}\times T^{*}_{q_{j}}N)
. If
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
and
r>n/2
, we can reason similarly as in Remark
3.23
to show that
f^{*}
uniquely extends to a continuous mapping
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(N;E)\rightarrow\Gamma(f^{*}E)
. Furthermore, each
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(N;E)
is continuous on a neighbourhood around
q_{j}
for every
j\in\Lambda
and
f^{*}u(p)=(p,u(q_{j}))
,
p\in O_{j}
,
j\in\Lambda
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(N;E)
(cf. Proposition
4.9
and Remark
4.10
).

As a direct consequence of Proposition
4.9
and Remark
4.10
, we have the following useful result.

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
f:M\rightarrow N
be a smooth map between the manifolds
M
and
N
with dimensions
m
and
n
respectively, let
L

be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}N\backslash 0
and let
(E,\pi_{E},N)
be a vector bundle. If
f^{*}:\Gamma(E)\rightarrow\Gamma(f^{*}E)
extends to a well-defined and continuous map
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(N;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;f^{*}E)
for some
r\in\mathbb{R}
, then for any
u\in\Gamma^{0}(E)\cap\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(N;E)
it holds that
f^{*}u\in\Gamma^{0}(f^{*}E)
and
f^{*}u(p)=(p,u\circ f(p))
,
p\in M
.

5.
Action of
\Psi
DOs on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
and its dual

In this section, we study the continuity properties of pseudo-differential operators when acting on the spaces
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
,
r\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
r,r^{\prime},r_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
r^{\prime}\geq r_{0}
. Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
and
W
an open conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Let
E
and
F
be two vector bundles over
M
. Let
A\in\Psi^{r^{\prime}}(M;E,F)
be properly supported, of order
r_{0}
in
L^{c}
and of order
-\infty
in
W
. Then
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L\cap W^{c}}(M;F)
is well-defined and continuous. Furthermore,
A
maps bounded subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
into relatively compact subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\widetilde{r}}_{L\cap W^{c}}(M;F)
for every
\widetilde{r}<r-r_{0}
.

Proof.

The case when
L=W^{c}=T^{*}M\backslash 0
is trivial. Assume this is not the case, i.e.
L\cap W^{c}
is not the whole
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Let
E
and
F
have ranks
k^{\prime}
and
k
respectively. We first prove the continuity of
A
. Since
\Gamma_{c}(E)
is dense in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
, it suffices to show that
A:\Gamma_{c}(E)\rightarrow\Gamma(F)
is continuous when
\Gamma_{c}(E)
and
\Gamma(F)
are equipped with the topologies induced by
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L\cap W^{c}}(M;F)
respectively. As
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;F)
is continuous, to prove the latter, it suffices to show that every seminorm (
4.5
) of
Au\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L\cap W^{c}}(N;F)
,
u\in\Gamma_{c}(E)
, is bounded by a sum of continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
of
u
. Let
(O,x)
be a chart over which
F
trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi_{F}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
V\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
, be such that
\widetilde{L}:=\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p})\in T^{*}O\,|\,p\in\operatorname{supp}\varphi,\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in V\}
has empty intersection with
L\cap W^{c}
; in view of Remark
4.4
, we can assume that
E
also trivialises over
O
via some
\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k^{\prime}}
. We want to estimate
\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi,V}(Au)
, for
u\in\Gamma_{c}(E)
. Notice that
\widetilde{L}\cap L\subseteq W
. Hence, we can employ a standard compactness argument to find relatively compact open sets
O_{1},\ldots,O_{n}
which cover
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
and satisfy
\overline{O_{q}}\subseteq O
,
q=1,\ldots,n
, and for each
O_{q}
we find open cones
V_{q,h},V^{\prime}_{q,h},V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
h=1,\ldots\mu_{q}
, such that
\overline{V_{q,h}}\subseteq V^{\prime}_{q,h}\cup\{0\}
,
\overline{V^{\prime}_{q,h}}\subseteq V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}\cup\{0\}
,
\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\overline{V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}}\neq\emptyset
,
V\backslash\{0\}\subseteq\bigcup_{h=1}^{\mu_{q}}V_{q,h}
and at least one of the following holds

(*)

\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p})\in T^{*}O\,|\,p\in\overline{O_{q}},\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\overline{V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}}\backslash\{0\}\}\subseteq W
;

(**)

\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p})\in T^{*}O\,|\,p\in\overline{O_{q}},\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\overline{V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}}\backslash\{0\}\}\subseteq L^{c}
.

Let
J_{q;W}
be the set of all
h\in\{1,\ldots,\mu_{q}\}
for which
(*)
holds true, while
J_{q;L^{c}}
be the set of all
h\in\{1,\ldots,\mu_{q}\}
for which
(**)
holds true;
J_{q;W}
and
J_{q;L^{c}}
may not be disjoint, also, for each
q\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, one of these may be empty but can not be both. There are
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}\subseteq S^{r^{\prime}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x(O)\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
and smoothing operators
\widetilde{T}^{l}_{j}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x(O))\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(x(O))
,
j=1,\ldots,k^{\prime}
,
l=1,\ldots,k
, such that

(A\chi)_{|O}=\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j})(\chi^{j}\circ x^{-1})\circ x\,s_{l}+\widetilde{T}^{l}_{j}(\chi^{j}\circ x^{-1})\circ x\,s_{l},\quad\chi=\chi^{j}e_{j}\in\Gamma_{c}(E_{O}),

where
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{k^{\prime}})
and
(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k})
are the local frames over
O
for
E
and
F
induced by
\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}
and
\Phi_{x}
respectively. Arguing by compactness, we see that
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}\subseteq S^{r_{0}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x(O_{q})\times V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h})
,
q=1,\ldots,n
,
h\in J_{q;L^{c}}
, and
\{\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\}_{l,j}\subseteq S^{-\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x(O_{q})\times V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h})
,
q=1,\ldots,n
,
h\in J_{q;W}
. Pick nonnegative
\psi_{q},\chi_{q}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{q})
,
q=1,\ldots,n
, such that
\sum_{q=1}^{n}\psi_{q}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
and
\chi_{q}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\psi_{q}
,
q=1,\ldots,n
. Let
u\in\Gamma_{c}(E)
;
u_{|O}=u^{j}e_{j}
. Denote
u^{j}_{x}:=u^{j}\circ x^{-1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(x(O))
,
\varphi_{x}:=\varphi\circ x^{-1}\in\mathcal{D}(x(O))
,
\psi_{q,x}:=\psi_{q}\circ x^{-1}\in\mathcal{D}(x(O_{q}))
,
\chi_{q,x}:=\chi_{q}\circ x^{-1}\in\mathcal{D}(x(O_{q}))
. Notice that

\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi,V}(Au)\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq k}\tilde{I}^{l}(u)+\sum_{q=1}^{n}\max_{1\leq l\leq k}\tilde{I}^{l}_{q}(u)+\sum_{q=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{\mu_{q}}(\max_{1\leq l\leq k}\tilde{I}^{l}_{q,h}(u)+\max_{1\leq l\leq k}I^{l}_{q,h}(u)),

with

(5.1)

\displaystyle\tilde{I}^{l}(u)

\displaystyle:=\left(\int_{\xi\in V,\,|\xi|<1}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{x}(Au)^{l}_{\Phi_{x}})(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(r-r_{0})}d\xi\right)^{1/2},

(5.2)

\displaystyle\tilde{I}^{l}_{q}(u)

\displaystyle:=\left(\int_{\xi\in V,\,|\xi|\geq 1}\left|\mathcal{F}\left(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\left(A((1-\chi_{q})u)\right)^{l}_{\Phi_{x}}\right)(\xi)\right|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(r-r_{0})}d\xi\right)^{1/2},

(5.3)

\displaystyle\tilde{I}^{l}_{q,h}(u)

\displaystyle:=\left(\int_{\xi\in V_{q,h},\,|\xi|\geq 1}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\widetilde{T}^{l}_{j}(\chi_{q,x}u^{j}_{x}))(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(r-r_{0})}d\xi\right)^{1/2},

(5.4)

\displaystyle I^{l}_{q,h}(u)

\displaystyle:=\left(\int_{\xi\in V_{q,h},\,|\xi|\geq 1}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j})(\chi_{q,x}u^{j}_{x}))(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(r-r_{0})}d\xi\right)^{1/2}.

Since
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(N;F)
is continuous, it is straightforward to show that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
,
f\mapsto\widetilde{I}^{l}(f)
, is a seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
which is bounded on bounded subsets (cf. the proof of the Claim in Proposition
3.7
) and hence it is continuous since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
is bornological. Since the kernel of
A
is smooth outside of the diagonal, the operator
v\mapsto\psi_{q}A((1-\chi_{q})v)
has a smooth kernel and thus (
5.2
) is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
of
u
. The operators
\widetilde{T}^{l}_{j}
are smoothing and consequently (
5.3
) is also a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
of
u
. To estimate
I^{l}_{q,h}(u)
, we proceed as follows. Pick
b_{q,h},b^{\prime}_{q,h},b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
such that
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\otimes b_{q,h}\in S^{r-r_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
,
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\otimes b^{\prime}_{q,h}\in S^{-r}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
and
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\otimes b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}\in S^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
and they satisfy the following:

(i)

0\leq b_{q,h}\leq|\cdot|^{r-r_{0}}
,
0\leq b^{\prime}_{q,h}\leq|\cdot|^{-r}
and
0\leq b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}\leq|\cdot|^{r}
on
\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\}
;

(ii)

\operatorname{supp}b_{q,h}\subseteq V^{\prime}_{q,h}\backslash\overline{B(0,1/2)}
and
b_{q,h}(\xi)=|\xi|^{r-r_{0}}
when
\xi\in\overline{V_{q,h}}\backslash B(0,1)
;

(iii)

\operatorname{supp}b^{\prime}_{q,h}\subseteq V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}\backslash\overline{B(0,1/2)}
,
\operatorname{supp}b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}\subseteq V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}\backslash\overline{B(0,1/2)}
and both
b^{\prime}_{q,h}(\xi)=|\xi|^{-r}
and
b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}(\xi)=|\xi|^{r}
when
\xi\in\overline{V^{\prime}_{q,h}}\backslash B(0,1)

(e.g., take
\chi,\widetilde{\chi}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
such that
0\leq\chi,\widetilde{\chi}\leq 1
,
\chi=1
on
\overline{V_{q,h}}\cap\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
and
\operatorname{supp}\chi\subseteq V^{\prime}_{q,h}
,
\widetilde{\chi}=1
on
\overline{B(0,1/2)}
and
\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{\chi}\subseteq B(0,1)
, and define
b_{q,h}(\xi):=(1-\widetilde{\chi}(\xi))\chi(\xi/|\xi|)|\xi|^{r-r_{0}}
;
b^{\prime}_{q,h}
and
b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}
can be constructed analogously). Notice that

(5.5)

\displaystyle I^{l}_{q,h}(u)

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)^{m/2}2^{|r-r_{0}|}\|b_{q,h}(D)\operatorname{Op}(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}(1-b^{\prime}_{q,h}b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}))(\chi_{q,x}u^{j}_{x})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}

(5.6)

\displaystyle{}\quad+(2\pi)^{m/2}2^{|r-r_{0}|}\|b_{q,h}(D)\operatorname{Op}(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}b^{\prime}_{q,h})b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}(D)(\chi_{q,x}u^{j}_{x})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}.

By construction,
b_{q,h}(D)\operatorname{Op}(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}(1-b^{\prime}_{q,h}b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}))
is an operator with symbol in
S^{-\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
and hence it is a continuous mapping from
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
into
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. Whence, the term in (
5.5
) is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
of
u
. When
h\in J_{q;W}
,
b_{q,h}(D)\operatorname{Op}(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}b^{\prime}_{q,h})
is an operator with symbol in
S^{-\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
. Consequently,
b_{q,h}(D)\operatorname{Op}(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}b^{\prime}_{q,h})b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}(D)
is a continuous mapping from
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
into
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
which implies that (
5.6
) is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
of
u
. When
h\in J_{q;L^{c}}
, the operator
b_{q,h}(D)\operatorname{Op}(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}b^{\prime}_{q,h})
has symbol in
S^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
, hence it is continuous on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. This implies

\|b_{q,h}(D)\operatorname{Op}(\varphi_{x}\psi_{q,x}\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}b^{\prime}_{q,h})b^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}(D)(\chi_{q,x}u^{j}_{x})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq C\mathfrak{p}^{\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}}_{r;\chi_{q},\overline{V^{\prime\prime}_{q,h}}}(u).

Since
h\in J_{q;L^{c}}
, the right-hand side is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
and the proof is complete.

To verify the second part, in view of the above, we only need to show that the inclusion
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L\cap W^{c}}(M;F)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime\widetilde{r}}_{L\cap W^{c}}(M;F)
maps bounded into relatively compact sets. For a bounded subset
B
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L\cap W^{c}}(M;F)
it is straightforward to show that
WF^{\widetilde{r}}_{c}(B)\subseteq L\cap W^{c}
. Corollary
4.7
implies that
B
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\widetilde{r}}_{L\cap W^{c}}(M;F)
and the proof of the proposition is complete.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

If we only know that the
\Psi
DO
A
is of order
r_{0}
in
L^{c}
, then we can apply the proposition with
W=\emptyset
to deduce that
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)
is well-defined and continuous and
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime\widetilde{r}}_{L}(M;F)
maps bounded into relatively compact sets when
\widetilde{r}<r-r_{0}
.

Remark 5.3
.

The second part is a generalisation of the Rellich’s lemma to the spaces
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
,
r\in\mathbb{R}
. Indeed, taking
L=W=\emptyset
and
E=F
, one infers that the inclusion mapping
H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)\rightarrow H^{r_{1}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
,
r_{2}>r_{1}
, maps bounded into relatively compact sets. Since the Banach space
H^{r_{2}}_{K}(M;E)
, with
K\subset\subset M
, is a closed subspace of
H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
, its unit ball is bounded in
H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
, and the proposition implies that it is relatively compact in
H^{r_{1}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
; this is exactly the Rellich’s lemma.

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
A\in\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
be properly supported and of order
-\infty
in the open conic subset
W
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Then
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{W^{c}}(M;F)
is well-defined and continuous. Furthermore, if
L
is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
, then
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L\cap W^{c}}(M;F)
is well-defined and continuous.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\widetilde{r}}_{T^{*}M\backslash 0}(M;E)
,
\widetilde{r}\in\mathbb{R}
, to show the first part we employ Proposition
5.1
with
L=T^{*}M\backslash 0
and we deduce that
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime\widetilde{r}-r}_{W^{c}}(M;F)
is well-defined and continuous for all
\widetilde{r}\in\mathbb{R}
. Now, the claim follows from Proposition
4.21
. The proof of the second part is analogous and we omit it.
∎

Corollary 5.5
.

Let
A\in\Psi^{r_{0}}(M;E,F)
be of order
-\infty
in the open conic subset
W
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Assume that the kernel of
A
has compact support in
M\times M
and let
K\subseteq M
be its projection on the first component. Then for every closed conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
satisfying
L\subseteq W
and every
r\in\mathbb{R}
,
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{W^{c}\cap\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(K);K}(M;F)
is well-defined and continuous. Furthermore,
A
maps bounded subsets of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
into relatively compact subsets of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\widetilde{r}}_{W^{c}\cap\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(K);K}(M;F)
for all
\widetilde{r}<r-r_{0}
.

Proof.

Denote
\widetilde{L}:=W^{c}\cap\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(K)
. Proposition
5.1
verifies that
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow H^{r-r_{0}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;F)
is well-defined and continuous. Since
\operatorname{supp}Au\subseteq K
and the topology of
H^{r-r_{0}}_{K}(M;F)
is the same as the one induced by
H^{r-r_{0}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;F)
, we deduce that
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow H^{r-r_{0}}_{K}(M;F)
is well-defined and continuous. This completes the proof of the continuity of
A
when
W=\emptyset
(cf. Remark
4.1
and Remark
4.15
). Assume that
W\neq\emptyset
. As
\Gamma_{c}(E)
is dense in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
, to show the continuity of
A
it remains to estimate
\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi,V}(u)
,
u\in\Gamma_{c}(E)
, where
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
V\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
, satisfy (
4.3
) with
\widetilde{L}
in place of
L
and
(O,x)
is a chart on
M
over which
E
and
F
locally trivialise (we denoted by
\Phi_{x}
the local trivialisation of
F
). We employ a standard compactness argument to find relatively compact open sets
O_{1},\ldots,O_{n}
which cover
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
and satisfy
\overline{O_{q}}\subseteq O
,
q=1,\ldots,n
, and for each
O_{q}
we find open cones
V_{q,h}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
h=1,\ldots\mu_{q}
, such that
V\backslash\{0\}\subseteq\bigcup_{h=1}^{\mu_{q}}V_{q,h}
and at least one of the following holds

(*)

\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p})\in T^{*}O_{q}\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\overline{V_{q,h}}\backslash\{0\}\}\subseteq W
;

(**)

\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p})\in T^{*}O_{q}\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\overline{V_{q,h}}\}\cap\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(K)=\emptyset
.

Denote by
J_{q;W}
the set of all
h\in\{1,\ldots,\mu_{q}\}
for which
(*)
holds. Pick nonnegative
\psi_{q}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{q})
,
q=1,\ldots,n
, such that
\sum_{q=1}^{n}\psi_{q}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
and notice that

\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi,V}(Au)\leq\sum_{q=1}^{n}\sum_{h\in J_{q;W}}\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi\psi_{q},\overline{V_{q,h}}}(Au)

since
\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi\psi_{q},\overline{V_{q,h}}}(Au)=0
when
h\not\in J_{q,W}
. Corollary
5.4
yields that
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{W^{c}}(M;F)
is well-defined and continuous, and thus each term
\mathfrak{q}^{\Phi_{x}}_{\nu;\varphi\psi_{q},\overline{V_{q,h}}}(Au)
,
h\in J_{q,W}
, is bounded by a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
of
u
which completes the proof of the first part of the corollary.

To show the second part, let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
. Proposition
5.1
shows that it is relatively compact in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\widetilde{r}+r_{0}}_{L}(M;E)
when
\widetilde{r}<r-r_{0}
(cf. Remark
5.2
). Hence
A(B)
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\widetilde{r}}_{\widetilde{L};K}(M;F)
in view of the first part of the corollary and the proof is complete.
∎

Proposition
5.1
allows us to show the following improvement of
[
29
, Theorem 18.1.31, p. 90]
.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
r,r_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
L
a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Let
E
and
F
be vector bundles of rank
k
over
M
and let
A\in\Psi^{r_{0}}(M;E,F)
be properly supported and satisfying
\operatorname{Char}A\subseteq L
. For each
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
is equivalent to
Au\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)
. Furthermore, the topology of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
is generated by the continuous seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
together with all seminorms
u\mapsto\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi,V}(Au)
, with
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)
and
(O,x)
a chart on
M
over which
F
trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi_{F}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and
V
a closed cone in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
which satisfy (
4.3
).

Remark 5.7
.

When
A
is as in the proposition, the fact
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\Longleftrightarrow Au\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)
follows from
[
29
, Theorem 18.1.31, p. 90]
; the novelty in the proposition is that one can generate the topology of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
as described. This is, in fact, an a priori estimate for
A
: for every continuous seminorm
\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}
on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
there are
\varphi_{\mu}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
and
V_{\mu}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
, as in the proposition, a constant
C>0
and a bounded subset
B
of
\Gamma_{c}(E^{\vee})
such that

\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}(u)\leq C\sup_{\psi\in B}|\langle u,\psi\rangle|+C\sum_{\mu=1}^{l}\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi_{\mu},V_{\mu}}(Au),\quad u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E).

When
L=\emptyset
and
A
is elliptic, this boils down to the widely known fact about a priori estimate for
A:H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)\rightarrow H^{r-r_{0}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;F)
.

Proof of Proposition
5.6
.

The claim is trivial when
L=T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Assume that
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. In view of Proposition
5.1
, if
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
then
Au\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)
and the seminorms
u\mapsto\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{x}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi,V}(Au)
are well defined and continuous on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
. Let
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
be such that
Au\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)
. Let
(O,x)
be a chart on
M
over which
E
trivialises via
\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(O)\backslash\{0\}
and the closed cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
V\backslash\{0\}\neq\emptyset
, satisfy (
4.3
). Our goal is to bound
\mathfrak{p}^{\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}(u)
by seminorms of
u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
and seminorms of
Au
as in the proposition; in view of Remark
4.4
, we can assume that
F
also trivialises over
(O,x)
via
\Phi_{x}:\pi_{F}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
. Lemma
2.1
together with a standard compactness argument imply that there are open sets
O^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,O^{\prime}_{n}
with compact closure in
O
which cover
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
and, for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, open cones
V_{j,h},V^{\prime}_{j,h}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\}
,
h=1,\ldots,\mu_{j}
, and properly supported
\Psi
DOs
A^{\prime}_{j,h}\in\Psi^{-r_{0}}(M;F,E)
,
h=1,\ldots,\mu_{j}
, such that
\overline{V_{j,h}}\subseteq V^{\prime}_{j,h}\cup\{0\}
,
V\backslash\{0\}\subseteq\bigcup_{h=1}^{\mu_{j}}V_{j,h}
,
W^{\prime}_{j,h}:=\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p})\in T^{*}O^{\prime}_{j}\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in V^{\prime}_{j,h}\}
does not intersect
L
and
R^{\prime}_{j,h}:=A^{\prime}_{j,h}A-\operatorname{Id}\in\Psi^{0}(M;E,E)
is of order
-\infty
in
W^{\prime}_{j,h}
,
h=1,\ldots,\mu_{j}
. Pick
\varphi_{j}\in\mathcal{D}(O^{\prime}_{j})
,
j=1,\ldots,n
, such that
0\leq\varphi_{j}\leq 1
and
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
. Notice that

(5.7)

\mathfrak{p}^{\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}}_{r;\varphi,V}(u)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{\mu_{j}}\mathfrak{p}^{\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}}_{r;\varphi\varphi_{j},\overline{V_{j,h}}}(R^{\prime}_{j,h}u)+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{\mu_{j}}\mathfrak{p}^{\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}}_{r;\varphi\varphi_{j},\overline{V_{j,h}}}(A^{\prime}_{j,h}Au).

Proposition
5.1
yields that
R^{\prime}_{j,h}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{W^{\prime c}_{j,h}}(M;E)
is well-defined and continuous (apply it with
L=T^{*}M\backslash 0
and
W=W^{\prime}_{j,h}
; cf. Remark
4.1
) and thus
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
,
u\mapsto\mathfrak{p}^{\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}}_{r;\varphi\varphi_{j},\overline{V_{j,h}}}(R^{\prime}_{j,h}u)
, is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
. Proposition
5.1
also shows that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
,
v\mapsto\mathfrak{p}^{\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}}_{r;\varphi\varphi_{j},\overline{V_{j,h}}}(A^{\prime}_{j,h}v)
, is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)
. Hence
\mathfrak{p}^{\widetilde{\Phi}_{x}}_{r;\varphi\varphi_{j},\overline{V_{j,h}}}(A^{\prime}_{j,h}Au)
is bounded from above by a finite sum of seminorms of
Au
as in the proposition together with a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
of
u
. This shows that
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
and that the seminorms in the proposition generate the topology of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
.
∎

As a consequence, we show that the Sobolev compactness wave front set satisfies analogous bounds as the Sobolev wave front set
[
29
, Theorem 18.1.31, p. 90]
.

Corollary 5.8
.

Let
r,r_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
E
and
F
be vector bundles over
M
of rank
k
, let
B
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
and let
A\in\Psi^{r_{0}}(M;E,F)
be properly supported. Then

(5.8)

WF^{r-r_{0}}_{c}(A(B))\subseteq WF^{r}_{c}(B)\subseteq WF^{r-r_{0}}_{c}(A(B))\cup\operatorname{Char}A.

Proof.

The first inclusion in (
5.8
) follows from Corollary
4.7
and Proposition
5.1
by taking
L=WF^{r}_{c}(B)
. To show the second inclusion, set
L:=WF^{r-r_{0}}_{c}(A(B))\cup\operatorname{Char}A
. Corollary
4.7
verifies that
A(B)
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)
and Proposition
5.6
yields that
B\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
. In view of Corollary
4.7
, it suffices to show that
B
is relatively compact, i.e. totally bounded, in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
. Let
U
be a neighbourhood of zero in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
; in view of Proposition
5.6
, without loss in generality, we can assume that

U=\{u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\,|\,\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{1}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi_{1},V_{1}}(Au)<\varepsilon,\ldots,\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{n}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi_{n},V_{n}}(Au)<\varepsilon,\mathfrak{p}(u)<\varepsilon\}

for some
\varepsilon>0
, where
\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{j}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi_{j},V_{j}}
,
j=1,\ldots,n
, are seminorms on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)
of the form (
4.5
) and
\mathfrak{p}
is a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
. Since
A(B)
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r-r_{0}}_{L}(M;F)
, there are
u_{1},\ldots,u_{q}\in B
such that for every
u\in B
there is
u_{j}
so that
\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{l}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi_{l},V_{l}}(Au_{j}-Au)<\varepsilon/2
. Since
(u_{j}+U_{0})\cap B
,
j=1,\ldots,q
, with
U_{0}:=\{u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\,|\,\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\mathfrak{p}^{\Phi_{l}}_{r-r_{0};\varphi_{l},V_{l}}(Au)<\varepsilon/2\}
, is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
(as
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
is Montel), there is
B_{j}:=\{u_{j}+u_{j,1},\ldots,u_{j}+u_{j,t_{j}}\}\subseteq(u_{j}+U_{0})\cap B
so that for each
u\in(u_{j}+U_{0})\cap B
there is
u_{j}+u_{j,l}\in B_{j}
such that
\mathfrak{p}(u-u_{j}-u_{j,l})<\varepsilon
. Set
B_{0}:=\bigcup_{j=1}^{q}B_{j}
. It is straightforward to show that
B\subseteq B_{0}+U
which completes the proof of the corollary.
∎

We end the section with the following consequence of the Hörmander’s construction of a distribution with prescribed wave front set
[
28
, Theorem 8.1.4, p. 255]
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
O
be an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. For every
r\in\mathbb{R}
and every closed conic subset
L
of
O\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
, there exists
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O)
such that
WF^{r}(u)=WF(u)=L
.

Proof.

It suffices to show the claim when
O=\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
L\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
for otherwise we can apply this case to the closure of
L
in
\mathbb{R}^{m}\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
. For such
L
, let
u
be the distribution constructed in the proof of
[
28
, Theorem 8.1.4, p. 255]
which satisfies
WF(u)=L
. We claim that
WF^{2m^{2}}(u)=L
. Clearly, is suffices to show that
L\subseteq WF^{2m^{2}}(u)
(cf. Remark
4.22
). By carefully examining the proof of
[
28
, Theorem 8.1.4, p. 255]
, one sees that for every
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
the smooth function
\mathcal{F}(\psi u)
satisfies
\mathcal{F}(\psi u)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
and for every
(x_{0},\xi_{0})\in L
and
\chi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
which equals
1
on a neighbourhood of
x_{0}
, there is a sequence
(x_{j},\theta_{j})\in L
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, with
\theta_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
, which converges to
(x_{0},\xi_{0}/|\xi_{0}|)
and

|\mathcal{F}(\chi u)(k_{j}^{3}\theta_{j})|\geq k_{j}^{-m-2}/2,\,\,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},\quad\mbox{where}\quad k_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,k_{j+1}>k_{j}.

Notice that the first property implies
\partial_{l}\mathcal{F}(\psi u)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
,
l=1,\ldots,m
, for all
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. Let
(x_{0},\xi_{0})\in L
and assume that
(x_{0},\xi_{0})\not\in WF^{2m^{2}}(u)
. There is
\chi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
which equals
1
on a neighbourhood of
x_{0}
and an open cone
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
containing
\xi_{0}
such that
\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{2m^{2}}\mathcal{F}(\chi u)\|_{L^{2}(V)}<\infty
. Let
(x_{j},\theta_{j})\in L
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, be as above. There is
j_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
B(k_{j}^{3}\theta_{j},k_{j}^{-m-3})\subseteq V
when
j\geq j_{1}
. We Taylor expand
\mathcal{F}(\chi u)
at
k_{j}^{3}\theta_{j}
up to order
0
and employ the above properties of
\mathcal{F}(\chi u)
to deduce that

|\mathcal{F}(\chi u)(\xi)-\mathcal{F}(\chi u)(k_{j}^{3}\theta_{j})|\leq m|\xi-k_{j}^{3}\theta_{j}|\max_{1\leq l\leq m}\|\partial_{l}\mathcal{F}(\chi u)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})},\quad\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{m},\,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

Hence, there is
j_{2}>j_{1}
such that
|\mathcal{F}(\chi u)(\xi)|\geq k_{j}^{-m-2}/4
when
\xi\in B(k_{j}^{3}\theta_{j},k_{j}^{-m-3})
, for all
j\geq j_{2}
. For any
j\geq j_{2}
, we infer

\infty>\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{2m^{2}}\mathcal{F}(\chi u)\|_{L^{2}(V)}^{2}\geq\frac{(k_{j}^{3}-1)^{4m^{2}}}{16k_{j}^{2m+4}}\int_{B(k_{j}^{3}\theta_{j},k_{j}^{-m-3})}d\xi=c_{0}\frac{(k_{j}^{3}-1)^{4m^{2}}}{16k_{j}^{m^{2}+5m+4}},

where
c_{0}
is the volume of the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. This is a contradiction since the right hand side tends to
\infty
as
j\rightarrow\infty
and the proof of
WF^{2m^{2}}(u)=L
is complete. For general
r\in\mathbb{R}
, pick properly supported and elliptic
A\in\Psi^{2m^{2}-r}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
and apply (
5.8
) and
[
29
, Theorem 18.1.28, p. 89]
to deduce
WF^{r}(Au)=WF^{2m^{2}}(u)=L=WF(u)=WF(Au)
.
∎

6.
Applications

We now showcase the theory we developed so far on two important and related concepts in the theory of PDEs and microlocal analysis: the existence of microlocal defect measures and the compensated compactness theorem. Our improvement primarily lies in that we can consider distributions whose Sobolev regularity is known only in parts of the cotangent bundle; of course, the results will provide information only in those parts. Both results are intrinsically connected with the
L^{2}
-inner product and we will have to work with sesquilinear forms. Since we aim for geometric extensions, we need to recall facts about anti-dual bundles. Most of these are widely known or easy to check, but, to the best of our knowledge, there are no standard notations for hardly any of them; we use the opportunity to fix the notation for the main results.

For a l.c.s.
X
, we denote by
X^{*}
its anti-dual (i.e. conjugate dual): it is the space of all continuous anti-linear (i.e. conjugate linear) functionals on
X
. As before,
X^{*}_{b}
will stand for
X^{*}
equipped with the strong dual topology.

Given two complex vector bundles
E
and
F
over
M
, we denote by
\overline{L}(E,F)
the complex vector bundle whose fibre at
p
is the space of anti-linear (conjugate linear) maps
\overline{\mathcal{L}}(E_{p},F_{p})
. We denote by
E^{*}
the anti-dual bundle of
E
, i.e.
E^{*}:=\overline{L}(E,\mathbb{C}_{M})
, while
E^{\#}
stands for the functional anti-dual bundle of
E
, i.e.
E^{\#}:=\overline{L}(E,DM)
. The bundle
E
is canonically isomorphic to
E^{\#\,\#}
via the isomorphism
e\mapsto(e^{*}\mapsto\overline{e^{*}(e)})
; we will always identify these two bundles via this isomorphism (similarly as we identify
E
with
E^{\vee\,\vee}
). Given
T\in L(E,F)_{p}
, we denote by
T^{\#}
the element of
L(F^{\#},E^{\#})_{p}
defined by
T^{\#}(f^{*})(e)=f^{*}(Te)
,
e\in E_{p}
,
f^{*}\in F^{\#}_{p}
. Employing the above identification, we have
T=T^{\#\,\#}
. For
T\in L(E,F)_{p}
, we define
T^{*}\in L(F^{*},E^{*})_{p}
analogously, and, employing the identifications
E=E^{*\,*}
and
F=F^{*\,*}
defined in the same way as above, we have
T^{*\,*}=T
.

There is a canonical anti-linear isomorphism
\iota_{E}:E\rightarrow E^{\#\,\vee}=L(E^{\#},DM)
given by
\iota_{E}(e)(e^{*}):=e^{*}(e)
,
e\in E_{p}
,
e^{*}\in E^{\#}_{p}
. (Although we will never use it, we point out that the conjugate bundle
\overline{E}
is canonically isomorphic to
E^{\#\,\vee}
via
e\mapsto(e^{*}\mapsto e^{*}(e))
.) The map
\iota_{E}
induces an anti-linear topological isomorphism
\Gamma_{c}(E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E^{\#\,\vee})
by applying it pointwise, which we again denote by
\iota_{E}
. Its transpose
12
12

12

If
T:X\rightarrow Y
is a continuous anti-linear map, then one can define two transposes:
T_{1}:Y^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{*}
,
T_{1}(y^{\prime})(x):=y^{\prime}(Tx)
, and
T_{2}:Y^{*}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
,
T_{2}(y^{*})(x):=y^{*}(Tx)
; both of them are linear and continuous when the spaces are equipped with their respective strong topologies. Here,
\iota_{0,E}
is the first of these transposes.

\iota_{0,E}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E^{\#})\rightarrow(\Gamma_{c}(E))^{*}_{b}
is a linear topological isomorphism. We denote by
(\cdot,\cdot)
the sesquilinear form that comes from the anti duality of
\Gamma_{c}(E)
and
(\Gamma_{c}(E))^{*}_{b}
; i.e.
(\iota_{0,E}(u),\varphi)=\langle u,\iota_{E}(\varphi)\rangle
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E^{\#})
,
\varphi\in\Gamma_{c}(E)
. It is straightforward to verify that

(\iota_{0,E}(f),\varphi)=\int_{M}\sqsubset f,\varphi\sqsupset,\quad f\in L^{1}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{\#}),\,\varphi\in\Gamma_{c}(E),\,\,\mbox{where}\,\,\sqsubset f,\varphi\sqsupset_{p}:=f_{p}(\varphi_{p}).

Furthermore,
\iota_{E}
uniquely extends to an anti-linear topological isomorphism
\iota_{E}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E^{\#\,\vee})
: pick a countable family of charts
\{(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})\}_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
as in Proposition
4.2
and a partition of unity
(\varphi_{\mu})_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
subordinated to it and define (after identifying
E^{\#}
with
(E^{\#})^{\vee\,\vee}
as standard)

\langle\iota_{E}(u),\psi\rangle:=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\langle\overline{u^{j}_{\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}},(\varphi_{\mu}\psi_{\mu,j})\circ x^{-1}_{\mu}\rangle,\quad u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E),\,\,\psi\in\Gamma_{c}(E^{\#}),\,\psi_{|O_{\mu}}=\psi_{\mu,j}\widetilde{\sigma}^{j}_{\mu},

where
(\widetilde{\sigma}^{1}_{\mu},\ldots,\widetilde{\sigma}^{k}_{\mu})
is the frame for
E^{\#}
induced by the trivialisation
\Phi_{x_{\mu}}
of
E
over
O_{\mu}
, namely
(\widetilde{\sigma}^{j}_{\mu})_{p}:=\Psi_{x_{\mu},p}^{-1}\circ\overline{\epsilon}^{j}\circ\Phi_{x_{\mu},p}
with
\Phi_{x_{\mu},p}
and
\Psi_{x_{\mu},p}
the linear isomorphisms induced by the local trivialisations of
E
and
DM
over
O_{\mu}
and
\overline{\epsilon}^{j}
the anti-linear map
\mathbb{C}^{k}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
z\mapsto\overline{z^{j}}
.
A more elegant way to define the extension of
\iota_{E}
is as follows. Notice that
\iota_{E^{\#}}:E^{\#}\rightarrow E^{\vee}
(we identify
E^{\#\,\#\,\vee}
with
E^{\vee}
) is given by
\iota_{E^{\#}}(e^{*})(e)=\overline{e^{*}(e)}
,
e\in E_{p}
,
e^{*}\in E^{\#}_{p}
(the conjugation comes from the identification of
E
with
E^{\#\,\#}
) and it induces an anti-linear topological isomorphism
\iota_{E^{\#}}:\Gamma_{c}(E^{\#})\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E^{\vee})
. The desired extension of
\iota_{E}
is
\langle\iota_{E}(u),\psi\rangle:=\overline{\langle u,\iota_{E^{\#}}(\psi)\rangle}
,
\psi\in\Gamma_{c}(E^{\#})
,
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
(after identifying
E^{\#}
with
(E^{\#})^{\vee\,\vee}
). In any case,
\iota_{E}
restricts to an anti-linear topological isomorphism
\iota_{E}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\check{L}}(M;E^{\#\,\vee})
for any closed conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Hence, in view of Theorem
4.19
,
\iota_{0,E}
restricts to a linear topological isomorphism
\iota_{0,E}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{L^{c}}(M;E^{\#})\rightarrow(\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E))^{*}_{b}
. When
E
is a complexification of a real vector bundle,
E
has a natural operation of conjugation which induces conjugation on
E^{\vee}
and
E^{\#}
by
\overline{e^{\prime}}(e)=\overline{e^{\prime}(\overline{e})}
and
\overline{e^{*}}(e)=\overline{e^{*}(\overline{e})}
respectively which, in turn, allows us to identify
E^{\vee}
with
E^{\#}
via
e^{\prime}\mapsto(e\mapsto\overline{\overline{e^{\prime}}(e)}=e^{\prime}(\overline{e}))
(this is not the same as
\iota_{E^{\#}}^{-1}
since
\iota_{E^{\#}}^{-1}
is anti-linear!). This yields that
\iota_{E}
is just conjugation in this case (since
E=E^{\vee\,\vee}
); furthermore for
\iota_{0,E}
we have
(\iota_{0,E}(u),\varphi)=\langle u,\overline{\varphi}\rangle
which implies that
(\cdot,\cdot)
is induced by the standard
L^{2}
-sesquilinear form. However, when
E
is not a complexification of a real vector bundle,
E
does not possess conjugation compatible with its almost complex structure (see
[
43
, Proposition 2, p. 39]
) and one can not make these identifications.

If
A\in\Psi^{r}(M;E,F)
is properly supported, then its adjoint map with respect to the sesquilinear forms from the respective anti-dualities is defined by
A_{0}:(\Gamma_{c}(F))^{*}_{b}\rightarrow(\Gamma_{c}(E))^{*}_{b}
,
(A_{0}u,\varphi):=(u,A\varphi)
,
u\in(\Gamma_{c}(F))^{*}_{b}
,
\varphi\in\Gamma_{c}(E)
. It is customary to intertwine
A_{0}
with the
\iota_{0}
-maps so it becomes a map on the distribution spaces, i.e.
A^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;F^{\#})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E^{\#})
,
A^{*}:=\iota_{0,E}^{-1}A_{0}\iota_{0,F}
, and call
A^{*}
the adjoint of
A
instead (the notation
A^{*}
is also suggestive for this, although not precise since
A_{0}
is the true adjoint); we will always employ this definition and notation for
A^{*}
throughout the rest of the article. Then
A^{*}\in\Psi^{r}(M;F^{\#},E^{\#})
,
A^{*}
is properly supported and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r}(A^{*})=\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r}(A)^{\#}
, where for
v\in\Gamma(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F))
, we denote
v^{\#}\in\Gamma(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}L(F^{\#},E^{\#}))
,
v^{\#}(p,\xi)(f^{*})e:=f^{*}(v(p,\xi)e)
,
e\in E_{p}
,
f^{*}\in F^{\#}_{p}
; i.e.
v^{\#}(p,\xi)=v(p,\xi)^{\#}
(of course, after identifying
\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}L(F^{\#},E^{\#})_{(p,\xi)}=\{(p,\xi)\}\times\mathcal{L}(F^{\#}_{p},E^{\#}_{p})
with
\mathcal{L}(F^{\#}_{p},E^{\#}_{p})
). If in addition
A\in\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
then
A^{*}\in\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;F^{\#},E^{\#})
.

For
r\in\mathbb{R}
and
W
an open conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
, we denote by
\Gamma_{\hom,r}(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)_{W})
the space of smooth sections
a:W\rightarrow\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)_{W}
which are positively homogeneous of degree
r
on
W
, i.e.
\mathfrak{pr}(a(p,t\xi))=t^{r}\mathfrak{pr}(a(p,\xi))
,
(p,\xi)\in W
,
t>0
, where
\mathfrak{pr}
is the smooth bundle homomorphism
\mathfrak{pr}:\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}L(E,F)\rightarrow L(E,F)
,
\mathfrak{pr}((p,\xi),T)=T
for
((p,\xi),T)\in\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}L(E,F)_{(p,\xi)}=\{(p,\xi)\}\times\mathcal{L}(E_{p},F_{p})
. The principal symbol map
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r}
induces a surjective linear map
\widetilde{\sigma}^{r}:\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)\rightarrow\Gamma_{\hom,r}(\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}L(E,F)_{T^{*}M\backslash 0})
;
for
A\in\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
,
\widetilde{\sigma}^{r}(A)
is such that it becomes an element of
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r}(A)
once we modify it on (any) open neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}
of the zero section in
T^{*}M
such that
\mathcal{U}\cap\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(K)
is relatively compact for every
K\subset\subset M
. The kernel of
\widetilde{\sigma}^{r}
is
\Psi^{r-1}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
. We denote by
\Psi_{\operatorname{phg},c}^{r}(M;E,F)
the space of all
\Psi
DOs in
\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
which have compactly supported kernel. When
r=0
,
\widetilde{\sigma}^{0}
induces a surjective linear map
\sigma^{0}:\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)\rightarrow\Gamma(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F))
, where
\pi_{S^{*}M}:S^{*}M\rightarrow M
is the cosphere bundle and the latter space is the space of smooth sections
S^{*}M\rightarrow\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)
of the pullback vector bundle
\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)
. The kernel of
\sigma^{0}
is
\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
. For properly supported
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
, it holds that
\sigma^{0}(A^{*})=\sigma^{0}(A)^{\#}
with the operation
#
defined as above but now on
\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}L(E,F)
. Notice that
\sigma^{0}
restricts to a surjective linear map
\sigma^{0}:\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c}(M;E,F)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F))
whose kernel is
\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg},c}(M;E,F)
. If
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c}(M;E,F)
and
B\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;F,H)
is properly supported, then
BA
also has kernel with compact support and
\sigma^{0}(BA)=\sigma^{0}(B)\sigma^{0}(A)
; an analogous statement holds true when composing operators in the other direction. When
W
is an open subset of
S^{*}M
, we denote by
\Gamma_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)_{W})
and
\Gamma_{c}^{0}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)_{W})
the spaces of all smooth and continuous compactly supported sections
W\rightarrow\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)_{W}
respectively. These spaces are endowed with their respective strict
(LF)
- and strict
(LB)
-space topologies. If
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
is of order
r^{\prime}<0
at
(p,\xi)\in T^{*}M\backslash 0
(hence of order
\lfloor r^{\prime}\rfloor
at
(p,\xi)
), then
\sigma^{0}(A)=0
in a neighbourhood of
(p,[\xi])\in S^{*}M
where
(p,[\xi])
is the image of
(p,\xi)
under the natural map
T^{*}M\backslash 0\rightarrow S^{*}M
. For an open conic subset
W
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
and
r\in\mathbb{R}
, we denote

\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg},c,W}(M;E,F):=\{A\in\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg},c}(M;E,F)\,|\,A\,\,\mbox{is of order}\,\,-\infty\,\,\mbox{at every point of}\,\,W^{c}\}.

If
A\in\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg},c,W}(M;E,F)
, then
A^{*}\in\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg},c,W}(M;F^{\#},E^{\#})
. Notice that
\sigma^{0}
restricts to a well-defined linear map

(6.1)

\sigma^{0}:\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,W}(M;E,F)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)_{[W]});

here and throughout the rest of the article we denote by
[L]
the image of the conic subset
L\subseteq T^{*}M\backslash 0
under the natural map
T^{*}M\backslash 0\rightarrow S^{*}M
. We point out that (
6.1
) is surjective. More generally, given
a\in\Gamma(\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}L(E,F))
with
\operatorname{supp}a\subseteq[W]
, there is a properly supported
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
such that
A
is of order
-\infty
on
W^{c}
and
\sigma^{0}(A)=a
. The kernel of (
6.1
) is
\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg},c,W}(M;E,F)
. If
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,W}(M;E,F)
and
B\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;F,H)
is properly supported then
BA\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,W}(M;E,H)
; an analogous statement holds true when composing operators in the other direction. Notice that all of the above holds even when
W=\emptyset
if we interpret
\Gamma_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,F)_{[W]})
as the space of smooth sections with compact support in
[W]=\emptyset
which consists only of the zero section.

6.1.
Generalisation of the microlocal defect measures of Gérard and Tartar

We devote this subsection to generalising the concept of microlocal defect measures introduced by Gérard
[
19
, Theorem 1]
and Tartar
[
53
, Theorem 1.1]
to sequences in the space
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)
where
L
is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
; i.e. sequences whose elements are only known to be
L^{2}_{\operatorname{loc}}
outside of
L
. The two main ingredients that will allow us to show this fact is the duality from Theorem
4.19
, which we will constantly tacitly apply it from now on, and the generalisations of the Rellich’s lemma we showed in Section
5
.

Before we start, we point out the following consequence of the above considerations. Given
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
, Corollary
5.5
implies that
A:\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime 0}_{L^{c}}(M;E^{\#})
is well-defined and continuous and thus
(\iota_{0,E}(Au),v)
,
u,v\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)
, is well-defined. Furthermore
(\iota_{0,E}(A^{*}u),v)=\overline{(\iota_{0,E}(Av),u)}
,
u,v\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)
.

We need the following variant of
[
19
, Lemma 1.2]
; throughout the rest of the article,
\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})
stands for the space of
k\times k
complex matrices.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
O
be an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
L
a closed conic subsets of
O\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
. Let
u,u_{n}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, be such that
\{u_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
and
u_{n}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
. If
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
is such that
\sigma^{0}(A)
is positive semi-definite at every point, then

(6.2)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Im}(A(u_{n}-u),u_{n}-u)=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Re}(A(u_{n}-u),u_{n}-u)\geq 0.

Proof.

The claim is trivial when
L=O\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
. Assume that
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. The proof of the first equality is the same as in
[
19
, Lemma 1.2]
, but, instead of the Rellich lemma, one employs Corollary
5.5
. The proof of the second inequality is also similar to the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 1.2]
; we point out only the notable differences. Denote
a:=\sigma^{0}(A)\in\mathcal{D}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
, and for arbitrary but fixed
\delta>0
set
b:=(\delta I+a)^{1/2}
and
b^{\prime}:=b-\delta^{1/2}I
with
I
being the identity matrix; notice that
\operatorname{supp}b^{\prime}=\operatorname{supp}a
. Pick
B^{\prime}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
such that
\sigma^{0}(B^{\prime})=b^{\prime}
. The difference is that we define
B
as
B:=A_{0}(\delta^{1/2}\operatorname{Id}+B^{\prime})
where we choose
A_{0}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
such that
\mathcal{D}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))\ni\sigma^{0}(A_{0})=a_{0}I
with
a_{0}
smooth, nonnegative and equal to
1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}a
. Of course
B\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
and
\sigma^{0}(B)=a_{0}b=a_{0}(\delta I+a)^{1/2}
and consequently
\sigma^{0}(B^{*}B)=\delta a_{0}^{2}I+a_{0}^{2}a=\delta a_{0}^{2}I+a=\sigma^{0}(\delta A_{0}^{*}A_{0}+A)
. Hence, there is
R\in\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
such that
B^{*}B=\delta A_{0}^{*}A_{0}+A+R
. In view of Corollary
5.5
,
A_{0}^{*}A_{0},B^{*}B:\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime 0}_{L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
are well-defined and continuous. Notice that
(B^{*}B\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})=(B\widetilde{u},B\widetilde{u})\geq 0
,
\widetilde{u}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
. This trivially holds when
\widetilde{u}\in\mathcal{D}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
and the general case follows by density (as
\mathcal{E}^{\prime 0}_{L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
); of course the same holds with
A_{0}
in place of
B
. Employing this, in the same way as in
[
19
, Lemma 1.2]
, we deduce the second inequality in the lemma.
∎

We denote by
\mathcal{K}(M;\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
the space of continuous functions with compact support with values in
\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})
and equipped with its standard
(LB)
-space topology; when
k=1
(the complex-valued case) we simply write
\mathcal{K}(M)
. A Radon measure is a continuous functional on
\mathcal{K}(M;\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
(cf.
[
4
, Chapter 3]
). The Radon measure
\vartheta\in(\mathcal{K}(M;\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})))^{\prime}
is said to be positive if
\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle\geq 0
for every
\psi\in\mathcal{K}(M;\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
which is positive semi-definite at every point. Every
\vartheta\in(\mathcal{K}(M;\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})))^{\prime}
is a matrix of elements of
(\mathcal{K}(M))^{\prime}
, i.e
\vartheta=(\vartheta^{j,l})_{j,l}
,
\vartheta^{j,l}\in(\mathcal{K}(M))^{\prime}
, and, for
\psi=(\psi_{j,l})_{j,l}\in\mathcal{K}(M;\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
, the dual pairing is
\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle=\langle\vartheta^{j,l},\psi_{j,l}\rangle
(of course, it looks like the trace of the product of
\vartheta
with the transpose of
\psi
since that is the dual pairing on the matrices). Each
\vartheta\in(\mathcal{K}(M;\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})))^{\prime}
can be viewed as an element of
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{K}(M),\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
by defining
\vartheta(\phi)=(\langle\vartheta^{j,l},\phi\rangle)_{j,l}
,
\phi\in\mathcal{K}(M)
, and this gives topological isomorphism between the strong dual
(\mathcal{K}(M;\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})))^{\prime}_{b}
and
\mathcal{L}_{b}(\mathcal{K}(M);\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
; some author choose to define the matrix valued Radon measures as
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{K}(M),\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
(see
[
19
]
). In this case, the measure is said to be positive if for every nonnegative
\phi\in\mathcal{K}(M)
, the matrix
(\langle\vartheta^{j,l},\phi\rangle)_{j,l}
is positive semi-definite (see
[
15
,
19
,
49
]
). Employing similar technique as in the proof of
[
19
, Proposition A.1]
, one can show that this definition of positiveness coincides with the one we give above. We specifically choose to define
(\mathcal{K}(M;\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})))^{\prime}
as the space of matrix-valued Radon measures because it is better suited for a generalisation to the vector bundle case; we intentionally put the indices in
(\vartheta^{j,l})_{j,l}
up to hint at the geometrical picture given in the main theorem (if one thinks of the elements of
E
as vectors,
\vartheta
will be a
2
-vector filed, instead of an
(1,1)
-tensor field).

We first show our result on the existence of microlocal defect measures in the Euclidean setting. The extensive analysis we have done in the previous sections will now pay off by allowing us to mimic the main ideas of
[
19
]
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
O
be an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
L
a closed conic subset of
O\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
. Let
u,u_{n}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, be such that
\{u_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
and
u_{n}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
. Then there is a subsequence
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and a positive Radon measure
\vartheta\in(\mathcal{K}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})))^{\prime}
such that

(6.3)

\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(A(u_{n_{j}}-u),u_{n_{j}}-u)=\langle\vartheta,\sigma^{0}(A)\rangle,\quad A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k}),

and
\vartheta
satisfies
\langle\vartheta,\psi^{*}\rangle=\overline{\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle}
,
\psi\in\mathcal{K}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
. Furthermore,
\vartheta
is unique in the following sense: if
\vartheta^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{K}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})))^{\prime}
is such that (
6.3
) is valid for
\vartheta^{\prime}
, then
\vartheta^{\prime}=\vartheta
.

Proof.

The claim is trivial when
L=O\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
. Assume that
L^{c}\neq\emptyset
. Since for every
a\in\mathcal{D}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
there is
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
such that
\sigma^{0}(A)=a
, the uniqueness follows from density. We show the existence. For brevity in notation, set
v_{n}:=u_{n}-u
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. One can find closed conic subsets
\widetilde{L}_{j}\neq\emptyset
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, of
O\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
such that
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\widetilde{L}_{j})
is compact and
\widetilde{L}_{j}\subseteq\operatorname{int}\widetilde{L}_{j+1}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and
\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\widetilde{L}_{j}=L^{c}
(cf. (
3.28
)). Hence
[\widetilde{L}_{j}]
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, are compact. Since the closure of
\mathcal{D}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j+1}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
in
\mathcal{C}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j+1}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
contains
\mathcal{C}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
, there is a countable dense subset
D_{j}
of
\mathcal{D}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j+1}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
such that its closure in
\mathcal{C}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j+1}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
contains
\mathcal{C}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
. Set
D:=\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}D_{j}
and denote
\widetilde{D}:=\operatorname{span}(D\cup\{a\,|\,a^{*}\in D\})
. Let
a\in D
and pick
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
such that
\sigma^{0}(A)=a
. Since
\{v_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
,
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|(Av_{n},v_{n})|<\infty
and hence there is a subsequence
(v_{n_{l}})_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
such that
(Av_{n_{l}},v_{n_{l}})
converges to some
\vartheta(a)\in\mathbb{C}
. In view of Corollary
5.5
, the same holds for any other
\widetilde{A}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
satisfying
\sigma^{0}(\widetilde{A})=a
. By employing diagonal extraction, we can assume the subsequence
(v_{n_{l}})_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is the same for all
a\in D
. Employing linearity and involutions, we deduce the existence of a linear functional

\vartheta:\widetilde{D}\rightarrow\mathbb{C},\quad\vartheta(a)=\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}(Av_{n_{l}},v_{n_{l}}),\,\,\mbox{with}\,\,\sigma^{0}(A)=a,

satisfying
\vartheta(a^{*})=\overline{\vartheta(a)}
. We equip
\widetilde{D}
with the topology induced by
\mathcal{K}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
. We claim that
\vartheta
is continuous. For each
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, set
\widetilde{D}_{j}:=\widetilde{D}\cap\mathcal{C}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
and equip it with the topology induced by
\mathcal{C}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
. By construction,
\mathcal{C}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
is contained in the closure of
\widetilde{D}_{j+1}
in
\mathcal{C}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j+1}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
and
[
33
, Corollary 1, p. 164]
implies that
\displaystyle\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
j\rightarrow\infty\end{subarray}}\widetilde{D}_{j}=\widetilde{D}
topologically; whence, it suffices to show that
\vartheta
is continuous on
\widetilde{D}_{j}
for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. For every
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, pick nonnegative
b_{j}\in\mathcal{D}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j+1}]}([L^{c}])
such that
b_{j}=1
on a neighbourhood of
[\widetilde{L}_{j}]
and choose
B_{j}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
such that
\sigma^{0}(B_{j})=b_{j}I
, where
I
is the identity matrix. Let
a\in\widetilde{D}_{j}
be self-adjoint at every point and denote
r_{a}:=\|a\|_{\mathcal{C}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))}
. Pick
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
such that
\sigma^{0}(A)=a
. Lemma
6.1
is applicable for
r_{a}B_{j}-A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
, so we infer
\vartheta(a)\leq C_{j}r_{a}
with
C_{j}:=\sup_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|(B_{j}v_{n_{l}},v_{n_{l}})|<\infty
(of course,
\vartheta(a)\in\mathbb{R}
). Applying this with
-a
in place of
a
, we deduce
|\vartheta(a)|\leq C_{j}r_{a}
. When
a\in\widetilde{D}_{j}
is arbitrary, we apply the above to
(a+a^{*})/2\in\widetilde{D}_{j}
and
(a-a^{*})/(2i)\in\widetilde{D}_{j}
to conclude
|\vartheta(a)|\leq 2C_{j}r_{a}
. Consequently,
\vartheta:\widetilde{D}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is continuous; whence
\vartheta\in(\mathcal{K}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})))^{\prime}
.

To show (
6.3
), let
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
. There is
j\geq 2
such that
a:=\sigma^{0}(A)\in\mathcal{D}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
. By construction, there are
a_{l}\in D_{j-1}
,
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, such that
a_{l}\rightarrow a
in
\mathcal{D}_{[\widetilde{L}_{j}]}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
. Pick
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
such that
0\leq\chi\leq 1
,
\chi(\xi)=0
when
|\xi|\leq 1/4
and
\chi(\xi)=1
when
|\xi|\geq 1/2
. Define
\widetilde{a}(x,\xi)=\chi(\xi)a(x,\xi/|\xi|)
and
\widetilde{a}_{l}(x,\xi)=\chi(\xi)a_{l}(x,\xi/|\xi|)
,
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
x\in O
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
. Of course,
\widetilde{a},\widetilde{a}_{l}\in S^{0}_{c}(O\times\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
,
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Notice that both
\{B_{j}v_{n_{h}}\}_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and
\{B^{*}_{j}v_{n_{h}}\}_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
are bounded subsets of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime 0}_{L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k})
and thus also of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
(see (
4.20
)). Consequently

|(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})-(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{l})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})|\\
=|(\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}-\widetilde{a}_{l})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},B^{*}_{j}v_{n_{h}})|\leq C\sup_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq q}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}\partial^{\beta}_{x}(\widetilde{a}-\widetilde{a}_{l})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2m};\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))},\,\,\,h,l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},

and, as
\widetilde{a}_{l}\rightarrow\widetilde{a}
in
S^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2m};\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
, we deduce

\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})-(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{l})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})|=0.

For
\varepsilon>0
there is
l_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
l\geq l_{\varepsilon}
it holds that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{l})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})-\varepsilon\leq\operatorname{Re}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})\leq\operatorname{Re}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{l})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})+\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{l})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})-\varepsilon\leq\operatorname{Im}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})\leq\operatorname{Im}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{l})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})+\varepsilon.

Since
\sigma^{0}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{l})B_{j})=a_{l}
, by what we proved above, we infer
(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{l})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})\rightarrow\langle\vartheta,a_{l}\rangle
, as
h\rightarrow\infty
, and consequently

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}\langle\vartheta,a_{l}\rangle-\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{h\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Re}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{h\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Re}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})\leq\operatorname{Re}\langle\vartheta,a_{l}\rangle+\varepsilon,\quad l\geq l_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\langle\vartheta,a_{l}\rangle-\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{h\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Im}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{h\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Im}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})\leq\operatorname{Im}\langle\vartheta,a_{l}\rangle+\varepsilon,\quad l\geq l_{\varepsilon}.

We take the limit as
l\rightarrow\infty
. Since
\langle\vartheta,a_{l}\rangle\rightarrow\langle\vartheta,a\rangle
and as
\varepsilon>0
was arbitrary, we deduce

\lim_{h\rightarrow\infty}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})=\langle\vartheta,a\rangle.

As
\sigma^{0}(B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j})=a=\sigma^{0}(A)
, we have
B_{j}\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a})B_{j}-A\in\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
and Corollary
5.5
yields that
(Av_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})\rightarrow(\vartheta,a)
. Now, Corollary
5.5
also shows that
(A^{\prime}v_{n_{h}},v_{n_{h}})\rightarrow(\vartheta,a)
for any other
A^{\prime}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(O;\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k})
satisfying
\sigma^{0}(A^{\prime})=a
. This completes the proof of (
6.3
).

Finally,
\langle\vartheta,\psi^{*}\rangle=\overline{\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle}
,
\psi\in\mathcal{K}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
, holds true since it is true for
\psi\in\widetilde{D}
. Similarly,
\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle\geq 0
holds true for all
\psi\in\mathcal{D}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
which are positive semi-definite at every point in view of (
6.3
) and Lemma
6.1
and the general case when
\psi\in\mathcal{K}([L^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))
and it is positive semi-definite at every point follows by density.
∎

We say that
T\in\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}L(E,E^{\#})_{(p,[\xi])}
is
positive semi-definite
if
T=T^{\#}
and
T(e)(e)
is a nonnegative density on
T_{p}M
for all
e\in E_{p}
(of course, after identifying
\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}L(E,E^{\#})_{(p,[\xi])}=\{(p,[\xi])\}\times\mathcal{L}(E_{p},E^{\#}_{p})
with
\mathcal{L}(E_{p},E^{\#}_{p})
), i.e.
T(e)(e)(\mathrm{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{v}_{m})\in[0,\infty)
for all
\mathrm{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{v}_{m}\in T_{p}M
. The main result of the subsection is the following theorem.

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
L
be a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
and let
u,u_{n}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. If
\{u_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)
and
u_{n}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
, then there is a subsequence
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and
\vartheta\in(\Gamma^{0}_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]}))^{\prime}
such that

(6.4)

\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(\iota_{0,E}(A(u_{n_{j}}-u)),u_{n_{j}}-u)=\langle\vartheta,\sigma^{0}(A)\rangle,\quad A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#}),

and
\vartheta
satisfies
\langle\vartheta,\psi^{\#}\rangle=\overline{\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle}
,
\psi\in\Gamma^{0}_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]})
, and
\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle\geq 0
if
\psi\in\Gamma^{0}_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]})
is positive semi-definite at every point. Furthermore,
\vartheta
is unique in the following sense: if (
6.4
) is valid for
\vartheta^{\prime}\in(\Gamma^{0}_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]}))^{\prime}
then
\vartheta^{\prime}=\vartheta
.

Proof.

The proof of the uniqueness is the same is in Proposition
6.2
. We show the existence. For brevity in notation, set
v_{n}:=u_{n}-u
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Pick a countable locally finite family of relatively compact coordinate charts
(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, that cover
M
such that, for each
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
E
locally trivialises over
O_{\mu}
via
\Phi_{\mu}:=\Phi_{x_{\mu}}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow O_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
. We denote by
(s_{\mu,1},\ldots,s_{\mu,k})
and
(\sigma^{1}_{\mu},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{\mu})
the local frames induced by
\Phi_{\mu}
for
E
and
E^{\#}
over
O_{\mu}
respectively; of course
\sigma^{j}_{\mu}(zs_{\mu,l})=\overline{z}\delta^{j}_{l}\lambda^{x_{\mu}}
,
z\in\mathbb{C}
. Denoting by
(s^{\prime 1}_{\mu},\ldots,s^{\prime k}_{\mu})
the frame for
E^{\prime}
dual to
(s_{\mu,1},\ldots,s_{\mu,k})
, we see that
(s^{\prime j}_{\mu}\otimes\sigma^{l}_{\mu})_{j,l}
is a local frame for
L(E,E^{\#})
over
O_{\mu}
; for
e\in E_{p}
,
(s^{\prime j}_{\mu}\otimes\sigma^{l}_{\mu})_{p}(e)=s^{\prime j}_{\mu,p}(e)\sigma^{l}_{\mu,p}\in E^{\#}_{p}
. As standard,
(\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}_{\mu}\otimes\sigma^{l}_{\mu}))_{j,l}
is the pullback local frame for
\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}L(E,E^{\#})
over
\pi^{-1}_{S^{*}M}(O_{\mu})
. For each
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, set
L_{\mu}:=\kappa_{\mu}(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(O_{\mu})\cap L)
with
\kappa_{\mu}
as in (
4.6
);
L_{\mu}
is a closed conic subset of
x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
. Denote
v^{j}_{n,\mu}:=v^{j}_{n,\Phi_{x_{\mu}}}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O))
,
j=1,\ldots,k
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
; see Proposition
4.2
. Proposition
6.2
is applicable to any subsequence of
\widetilde{v}_{n,\mu}:=(v^{1}_{n,\mu},\ldots,v^{k}_{n,\mu})\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L_{\mu}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu});\mathbb{C}^{k})
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, for each
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. We apply the proposition together with a standard diagonal argument to find a subsequence
(v_{n_{h}})_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and positive Radon measures
\widetilde{\vartheta}_{\mu}=(\widetilde{\vartheta}^{l,j}_{\mu})_{l,j}\in(\mathcal{K}([(L_{\mu})^{c}];\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})))^{\prime}
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, such that

(6.5)

\lim_{h\rightarrow\infty}(\widetilde{A}\widetilde{v}_{n_{h},\mu},\widetilde{v}_{n_{h},\mu})=\langle\widetilde{\vartheta}_{\mu},\sigma^{0}(\widetilde{A})\rangle,\quad\widetilde{A}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,(L_{\mu})^{c}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu});\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{k}).

We are going to glue together the
\widetilde{\vartheta}_{\mu}
’s to a continuous functional on
\Gamma^{0}_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]})
. Choose a smooth partition of unity
(\chi_{\mu})_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
subordinated to
(O_{\mu})_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and, for each
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, pick nonnegative
\chi^{\prime}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
such that
\chi^{\prime}_{\mu}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\chi_{\mu}
. For brevity in notation, we set
\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}:=\chi_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1}
and
\widetilde{\chi}^{\prime}_{\mu}:=\chi^{\prime}_{\mu}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1}
. Let
\hat{\kappa}_{\mu}
be the diffeomorphism

(6.6)

\hat{\kappa}_{\mu}:\pi_{S^{*}M}^{-1}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times\mathbb{S}^{m-1},\,\hat{\kappa}_{\mu}(p,[\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p}]):=(x_{\mu}(p),\hat{\xi}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\xi}_{m}),

where
\hat{\xi}_{j}dx^{j}|_{p}\in[\xi_{j}dx^{j}|_{p}]
is such that
(\hat{\xi}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\xi}_{m})\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
. We define

\displaystyle\vartheta:\Gamma_{c}^{0}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]})\rightarrow\mathbb{C},\,\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle:=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\langle\widetilde{\vartheta}^{l,j}_{\mu},\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}\,\psi_{\mu,l,j}\circ\hat{\kappa}^{-1}_{\mu}\rangle,\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\psi\in\Gamma^{0}_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]}),\,\psi_{|\pi_{S^{*}M}^{-1}(O_{\mu})}=\psi_{\mu,l,j}\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}_{\mu}\otimes\sigma^{l}_{\mu}).

It is straightforward to verify that
\vartheta
is well-defined and continuous (notice that
\operatorname{supp}(\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}\,\psi_{\mu,l,j}\circ\hat{\kappa}_{\mu}^{-1})\subset\subset[(L_{\mu})^{c}]
). The fact
\langle\vartheta,\psi^{\#}\rangle=\overline{\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle}
follows from the fact that
\psi^{\#}_{|\pi^{-1}_{S^{*}M}(O_{\mu})}=\overline{\psi_{\mu,j,l}}\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}_{\mu}\otimes\sigma^{l}_{\mu})
and the corresponding fact about
\widetilde{\vartheta}_{\mu}
from Proposition
6.2
. Furthermore, if
\psi
is positive semi-definite at every point, then
\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle\geq 0
since
\widetilde{\vartheta}_{\mu}
,
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, are positive Radon measures.

It remains to show that
\vartheta
satisfies (
6.4
). Let
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E;E^{\#})
and set
\hat{a}:=\sigma^{0}(A)\in\Gamma_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]})
. Fix an open neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}
of the zero section in
T^{*}M
such that
\mathcal{U}\cap\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(K)
is relatively compact for any
K\subset\subset M
and if
(p,\xi)\not\in\mathcal{U}
, then
(p,t\xi)\not\in\mathcal{U}
, for all
t>1
; e.g.
\mathcal{U}:=\bigcup_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}_{\mu}|_{p})\in T^{*}O_{\mu}\,|\,\chi_{\mu}(p)>0,\,\sum_{j=1}^{m}\xi_{j}^{2}<1\}
. We modify the pullback of
\hat{a}
under the natural map
T^{*}M\backslash 0\rightarrow S^{*}M
in
\mathcal{U}
so as to become a smooth section
a:T^{*}M\rightarrow\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}L(E,E^{\#})
. Then
a\in S^{0}_{\operatorname{loc}}(T^{*}M;E,E^{\#})
and
a\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{0}(A)
. For each
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we have
a_{|T^{*}O_{\mu}}=\kappa^{*}_{\mu}(a_{\mu,l,j})\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}_{\mu}\otimes\sigma^{l}_{\mu})
with
a_{\mu,l,j}\in S^{0}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
,
l,j=1,\ldots,k
, and
(\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}_{\mu}\otimes\sigma^{l}_{\mu}))_{j,l}
is the pullback local frame for
\pi_{T^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})
over
T^{*}O_{\mu}
. Of course,
a_{\mu,l,j}
is positively homogeneous of degree
0
outside of
\kappa_{\mu}(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(O_{\mu})\cap\mathcal{U})
. For each
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, there are
\{\widetilde{a}_{\mu,l,j}\}_{l,j}\subseteq S^{0}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
such that
\widetilde{a}_{\mu,l,j}
,
l,j=1,\ldots,k
, are of order
-\infty
in a conic neighbourhood of every point of
L_{\mu}
and
\widetilde{T}_{\mu,l,j}\in\Psi^{-\infty}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
,
l,j=1,\ldots,k
, such that

(A\varphi)_{|O_{\mu}}=\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{a}_{\mu,l,j})(\varphi^{j}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})\circ x_{\mu}\,\sigma^{l}_{\mu}+\widetilde{T}_{\mu,l,j}(\varphi^{j}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})\circ x_{\mu}\,\sigma^{l}_{\mu},\quad\varphi=\varphi^{j}s_{\mu,j}\in\Gamma_{c}(E_{O_{\mu}}).

Then
a_{\mu,l,j}-\widetilde{a}_{\mu,l,j}\in S^{-1}_{\operatorname{loc}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
, which, in view of the properties of
a_{\mu,l,j}
, implies that
a_{\mu,l,j}=0
in
W_{\mu}\backslash\kappa_{\mu}(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(O_{\mu})\cap\mathcal{U})
for some open conic neighbourhood
W_{\mu}
of
L_{\mu}
in
x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
. We infer

(A\varphi)_{|O_{\mu}}=\operatorname{Op}(a_{\mu,l,j})(\varphi^{j}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})\circ x_{\mu}\,\sigma^{l}_{\mu}+\widetilde{Q}_{\mu,l,j}(\varphi^{j}\circ x_{\mu}^{-1})\circ x_{\mu}\,\sigma^{l}_{\mu},\quad\varphi=\varphi^{j}s_{\mu,j}\in\Gamma_{c}(E_{O_{\mu}}),

with
\widetilde{Q}_{\mu,l,j}\in\Psi^{-1}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
and
\widetilde{Q}_{\mu,l,j}
is of order
-\infty
in
L_{\mu}
,
l,j=1,\ldots,k
. Denote

\Lambda_{0}:=\{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\,|\,O_{\mu}\cap\pi_{S^{*}M}(\operatorname{supp}\hat{a})\neq\emptyset\,\,\mbox{or}\,\,O_{\mu}\cap\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\operatorname{supp}(\mbox{kernel of}\,A))\neq\emptyset\};

\Lambda_{0}
is finite. Notice that

(\iota_{0,E}(Av_{n_{h}}),v_{n,h})=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}(\iota_{0,E}(\chi_{\mu}A(\chi^{\prime}_{\mu}v_{n_{h}})),v_{n_{h}})+\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}(\iota_{0,E}(\chi_{\mu}A((1-\chi^{\prime}_{\mu})v_{n_{h}})),v_{n_{h}}).

Since the kernel of
A
is smooth outside of the diagonal, the operator
\psi\mapsto\chi_{\mu}A((1-\chi^{\prime}_{\mu})\psi)
has smooth compactly supported kernel and thus it is a continuous map
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E^{\#})
. Consequently, the second sum tends to
0
as
h\rightarrow\infty
. For the first sum, we infer

\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}(\iota_{0,E}(\chi_{\mu}A(\chi^{\prime}_{\mu}v_{n_{h}})),v_{n_{h}})\\
=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}(\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}a_{\mu,l,j})(\widetilde{\chi}^{\prime}_{\mu}v_{n_{h},\mu}^{j}),v_{n_{h},\mu}^{l})+\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}(\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}\widetilde{Q}_{\mu,l,j}(\widetilde{\chi}^{\prime}_{\mu}v_{n_{h},\mu}^{j}),v_{n_{h},\mu}^{l}).

In view of Corollary
5.5
, the second sum tends to zero as
h\rightarrow\infty
. There is
b_{\mu,l,j}\in S^{0}_{c}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu})\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
such that
\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}a_{\mu,l,j})\widetilde{\chi}^{\prime}_{\mu}=\operatorname{Op}(b_{\mu,l,j})
. Notice that
\operatorname{Op}(b_{\mu,l,j})\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,(L_{\mu})^{c}}(x_{\mu}(O_{\mu}))
. Since
\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}a_{\mu,l,j}=\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}a_{\mu,l,j}\widetilde{\chi}^{\prime}_{\mu}\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{0}(\operatorname{Op}(b_{\mu,l,j}))
, from the way we defined
a
, we infer
\sigma^{0}(\operatorname{Op}(b_{\mu,l,j}))=\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}\hat{a}_{\mu,l,j}\circ\hat{\kappa}_{\mu}^{-1}
, where
\hat{a}_{\mu,l,j}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{-1}(O_{\mu}))
are defined by
\hat{a}_{|\pi_{S^{*}M}^{-1}(O_{\mu})}=\hat{a}_{\mu,l,j}\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}_{\mu}\otimes\sigma^{l}_{\mu})
. In view of (
6.5
), we deduce

(6.7)

\lim_{h\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{0}}(\operatorname{Op}(\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}a_{\mu,l,j})(\widetilde{\chi}^{\prime}_{\mu}v_{n_{h},\mu}^{j}),v_{n_{h},\mu}^{l})=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\langle\widetilde{\vartheta}^{l,j}_{\mu},\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu}\hat{a}_{\mu,l,j}\circ\hat{\kappa}_{\mu}^{-1}\rangle=\langle\vartheta,\hat{a}\rangle,

which verifies (
6.4
). This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

Strictly speaking,
\vartheta
is not a measure because it acts on sections. However, once we choose coordinates and a trivialisation of the bundle it becomes a measure. To wit, the fact
\langle\vartheta,\psi\rangle\geq 0
for all
\psi
positive semi-definite at every point implies that any coordinate representation of
\vartheta
is a matrix-valued positive Radon measure: Let
(x,O)
be any coordinate chart over which
E
trivialises via
\Phi:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
, let
(\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}\otimes\sigma^{l}))_{j,l}
be the local frame for
\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})
over
O
induced by
\Phi
(defined as in the proof of the theorem) and denote
L_{O}:=\kappa(\pi_{T^{*}M}^{-1}(O)\cap L)
with
\kappa
the total local trivialisation of
T^{*}M
over
O
. Then,
\widetilde{\vartheta}:=(\widetilde{\vartheta}^{l,j})_{l,j}
is a matrix-valued positive Radon measure on
[(L_{O})^{c}]
, where
\widetilde{\vartheta}^{l,j}\in(\mathcal{K}([(L_{O})^{c}]))^{\prime}
is defined by
\langle\widetilde{\vartheta}^{l,j},\phi\rangle:=\langle\vartheta,\phi\circ\hat{\kappa}\,\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}\otimes\sigma^{l})\rangle
with
\hat{\kappa}
as in (
6.6
).

The support of
\vartheta
contains all information about the directions in
[L^{c}]
in which the convergence
u_{n_{j}}\rightarrow u
is not in
L^{2}
-sense.

Corollary 6.5
.

Let
L
,
u
and
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
