satisfy the assumptions in Theorem
6.3
and let
\vartheta
and the subsequence
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be as in the claims of the theorem. Let
L^{(\vartheta)}
be the inverse image of
\operatorname{supp}\vartheta
under the natural map
T^{*}M\backslash 0\rightarrow S^{*}M
. Then

(6.8)

L^{(\vartheta)}\subseteq WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})\subseteq L\cup L^{(\vartheta)},

L\cup L^{(\vartheta)}
is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
and
u_{n_{j}}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L\cup L^{(\vartheta)}}(M;E)
.

Remark 6.6
.

Since (by definition!)
L^{(\vartheta)}\subseteq L^{c}
, (
6.8
) is equivalent to

(6.9)

L^{(\vartheta)}=WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})\backslash L.

Although
\vartheta
carries similar information as the Sobolev compactness wave front set
WF^{0}_{c}
, it can be a convenient tool in practice as we show in the next subsection.

Proof of Corollary
6.5
.

Corollary
4.7
shows that
\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})}(M;E)
and, since
u_{n_{j}}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
,

(6.10)

u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})}(M;E)\quad\mbox{and}\quad u_{n_{j}}\rightarrow u\quad\mbox{in}\quad\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})}(M;E).

Set
L^{\prime}:=L\cup L^{(\vartheta)}
. The proof that
L^{\prime}
is closed in
T^{*}M\backslash 0
is straightforward
13
13

13

This fact is not redundant since
L^{(\vartheta)}
is closed in
L^{c}
but not necessarily in
T^{*}M\backslash 0
.
. Once we show (
6.8
), (
6.10
) would imply
u_{n_{j}}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L^{\prime}}(M;E)
.

First we show the second inclusion in (
6.8
). We may assume
L^{\prime c}\neq\emptyset
. Let
(p_{0},\xi_{0})\not\in L^{\prime}
,
\xi_{0}\neq 0
. There is a chart
(O,x)
about
p_{0}
over which
E
locally trivialises via
\Phi_{x}:\pi_{E}^{-1}(O)\rightarrow O\times\mathbb{C}^{k}
and open cones
V,V^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
such that
\overline{V}\subseteq V^{\prime}\cup\{0\}
,
\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\overline{V^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
,

\displaystyle(p_{0},\xi_{0})\in\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p})\in T^{*}O\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in V\}\quad\mbox{and}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\{(p,\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p})\in T^{*}O\,|\,(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m})\in\overline{V^{\prime}}\}\subseteq L^{\prime c}.

Pick
\phi,\widetilde{\phi}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
such that
0\leq\phi,\widetilde{\phi}\leq 1
,
\phi=1
on
\overline{V}\cap\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
,
\operatorname{supp}\phi\subseteq V^{\prime}
,
\widetilde{\phi}=1
on
\overline{B(0,1/4)}
and
\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{\phi}\subseteq B(0,1/2)
. Define
b(\xi):=(1-\widetilde{\phi}(\xi))\phi(\xi/|\xi|)
;
b
is smooth with support in
V^{\prime}
, positively homogeneous of order
0
when
|\xi|\geq 1/2
and equal to
1
on
\overline{V}\backslash B(0,1/2)
. Pick nonnegative
\chi,\chi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}(O)
such that
\chi=1
on a neighbourhood of
p_{0}
and
\chi^{\prime}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\chi
and define

A:\Gamma_{c}(E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E^{\#}),\,A\varphi:=\sum_{l=1}^{k}\chi\operatorname{Op}(b)((\chi^{\prime}\varphi^{l})\circ x^{-1})\circ x\,\sigma^{l},\quad\varphi\in\Gamma_{c}(E),\,\varphi_{|O}=\varphi^{l}s_{l},

where
(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k})
and
(\sigma^{1},\ldots,\sigma^{k})
are the local frames for
E
and
E^{\#}
over
O
induced by
\Phi_{x}
. Clearly,
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{\prime c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
and
(p_{0},\xi_{0})\not\in\operatorname{Char}A
. We also define

A^{\prime}:\Gamma_{c}(E)\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(E),\,A^{\prime}\varphi:=\sum_{l=1}^{k}\chi\operatorname{Op}(b)((\chi^{\prime}\varphi^{l})\circ x^{-1})\circ x\,s_{l},\quad\varphi\in\Gamma_{c}(E),\,\varphi_{|O}=\varphi^{l}s_{l}.

Of course,
A^{\prime}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{\prime c}}(M;E,E)
and
(p_{0},\xi_{0})\not\in\operatorname{Char}A^{\prime}
. We claim that

(6.12)

(\iota_{0,E}(Av),A^{\prime}v)=\sum_{l=1}^{k}\|\chi\circ x^{-1}\operatorname{Op}(b)((\chi^{\prime}\circ x^{-1})v^{l}_{\Phi_{x}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}^{2},\quad v\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E),

with
v^{l}_{\Phi_{x}}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L_{O}}(x(O))
as in (
2.2
) and
L_{O}
as in Remark
6.4
(cf. Proposition
4.2
). Both the left and the right hand side are well-defined in view of Corollary
5.5
and the continuous inclusions
\mathcal{E}^{\prime 0}_{L^{\prime c}}(M;E)\subseteq L^{2}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L^{\prime}}(M;E)
; notice that the right-hand side is the square of a norm on
L^{2}_{\operatorname{supp}\chi}(M;E)
of
A^{\prime}v\in L^{2}_{\operatorname{supp}\chi}(M;E)
. By plugging in the definitions of
A
and
A^{\prime}
, it is easy to see that (
6.12
) hods true for
v\in\Gamma_{c}(E)
. The general case follows from the fact that
\Gamma_{c}(E)
is sequentially dense in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)
(Proposition
4.9
) and Corollary
5.5
. Denoting
v_{n_{j}}:=u_{n_{j}}-u
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, Theorem
6.3
gives

(\iota_{0,E}(Av_{n_{j}}),A^{\prime}v_{n_{j}})=(\iota_{0,E}(A^{\prime*}Av_{n_{j}}),v_{n_{j}})\rightarrow\langle\vartheta,\sigma^{0}(A^{\prime*}A)\rangle=\langle\vartheta,\sigma^{0}(A^{\prime})^{\#}\sigma^{0}(A)\rangle=0

since
\operatorname{supp}\sigma^{0}(A)\cap\operatorname{supp}\vartheta=\emptyset=\operatorname{supp}\sigma^{0}(A^{\prime})\cap\operatorname{supp}\vartheta
. Employing (
6.12
), we deduce that
A^{\prime}v_{n_{j}}\rightarrow 0
in
L^{2}_{\operatorname{comp}}(M;E)
and thus
WF^{0}_{c}(A^{\prime}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}))=\emptyset
in view of Corollary
4.7
. Corollary
5.8
now yields
WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})\subseteq\operatorname{Char}A^{\prime}
and thus
(p_{0},\xi_{0})\not\in WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})
.

To show the first inclusion in (
6.8
), let
(p_{0},\xi_{0})\not\in WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})
,
\xi_{0}\neq 0
. If
(p_{0},\xi_{0})\in L
, then
(p_{0},\xi_{0})\not\in L^{(\vartheta)}
. Assume that
(p_{0},\xi_{0})\not\in L^{\prime\prime}:=L\cup WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})
. We claim that
\langle\vartheta,a\rangle=0
,
a\in\Gamma_{c}(\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{\prime\prime c}]})
; this would imply that
(p_{0},\xi_{0})\not\in L^{(\vartheta)}
(by density). For
a\in\Gamma_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{\prime\prime c}]})
, pick
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{\prime\prime c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
such that
\sigma^{0}(A)=a
. Corollary
5.5
together with (
6.10
) yield that
Au_{n_{j}}\rightarrow Au
in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime 0}_{L^{\prime\prime c}}(M;E^{\#})
and Theorem
6.3
gives
\langle\vartheta,a\rangle=0
.
∎

Remark 6.7
.

When
L=\emptyset
, (
6.8
) boils down to
WF^{0}_{c}(\{u_{n_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})=L^{(\vartheta)}
; this result was shown in
[
18
]
. Even in the case
L=\emptyset
, the corollary improves this result by identifying that
u_{n_{j}}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L^{(\vartheta)}}(M;E)
. In this case, the result is optimal in the following sense: if
u_{n_{j}}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L^{\prime}}(M;E)
for some closed conic subset
L^{\prime}
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
, then Corollary
4.7
together with this result yield that
L^{(\vartheta)}\subseteq L^{\prime}
.

6.2.
The compensated compactness theorem

We are now going to employ the microlocal defect measures from Theorem
6.3
to generalise the Gérard-Murat-Tartar theorem on compensated compactness
[
44
,
45
,
51
,
19
]
. We start with a technical lemma.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
L
,
u
and
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
satisfy the assumptions in Theorem
6.3
and let
\vartheta
and
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be as in the claims of the theorem. For every
a\in\Gamma_{c}(\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]})
, there exists a subsequence of
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
, denoted again by
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
, such that for every
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
satisfying
\sigma^{0}(A)=a
it holds that

(6.13)

\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(\iota_{0,E}(Au),u_{n_{j}})=(\iota_{0,E}(Au),u)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(\iota_{0,E}(Au_{n_{j}}),u_{n_{j}})\in\mathbb{C}.

Proof.

Let
a\in\Gamma_{c}(\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]})
and pick
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
so that
\sigma^{0}(A)=a
. In view of Corollary
5.5
,
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|(\iota_{0,E}(Au),u_{n})|<\infty
and
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|(\iota_{0,E}(Au_{n}),u_{n})|<\infty
so there is a subsequence of
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
from Theorem
6.3
, which we again denote by
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
, such that both
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(\iota_{0,E}(Au),u_{n_{j}})
and
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(\iota_{0,E}(Au_{n_{j}}),u_{n_{j}})
exist in
\mathbb{C}
. If
A^{\prime}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
is any other
\Psi
DO satisfying
\sigma^{0}(A^{\prime})=a
, then both of the limits exist for
A^{\prime}
with the same subsequence in view of Corollary
5.5
employed with
A^{\prime}-A\in\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
(for
(\iota_{0,E}(A^{\prime}u_{n_{j}}),u_{n_{j}})
, it is straightforward to show that
(\iota_{0,E}((A^{\prime}-A)u_{n_{j}}),u_{n_{j}})\rightarrow(\iota_{0,E}((A^{\prime}-A)u),u)
). It remains to show the first identity in (
6.13
). Take a sequence
\{\psi_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\subseteq\Gamma_{c}(E)
which converges to
u
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)
(cf. Proposition
4.9
). Notice that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(Au),u_{n_{j}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(A(u-\psi_{l})),u_{n_{j}})+\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(A\psi_{l}),u_{n_{j}})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|(\iota_{0,E}(A(u-\psi_{l})),u_{n})|+\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(A\psi_{l}),u_{n_{j}}).

Hence
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(Au),u_{n_{j}})\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|(\iota_{0,E}(A(u-\psi_{l})),u_{n})|+\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(A\psi_{l}),u)
and consequently (cf. Corollary
5.5
)
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(Au),u_{n_{j}})\leq\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(Au),u)
. Doing the same for
-A
and
\operatorname{Im}(\iota_{0,E}(\pm Au),u_{n_{j}})
, we deduce the first identity in (
6.13
).
∎

For a closed conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
and
r\in\mathbb{R}
, we define the l.c.s.
H^{r}_{L;\operatorname{loc}}(M;E):=H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)\cap\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E)
equipped with the topology induced by all continuous seminorms on
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E)
. Clearly,
H^{r}_{L;\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
is complete. It is continuously and densely included in both
H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{L}(M;E)
(since
\Gamma_{c}(E)
is dense in both spaces) and
H^{r}_{T^{*}M\backslash 0;\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)=H^{r}_{\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)
and
H^{r}_{\emptyset;\operatorname{loc}}(M;E)=\Gamma(E)
. For
\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
fixed, set
K:=\operatorname{supp}\chi
and notice that the following map is well-defined and continuous:

(6.14)

H^{r}_{L;\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{*})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime r}_{L\cap\pi^{-1}_{T^{*}M}(K);K}(M;E^{\#}),\quad u\mapsto\chi u.

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{R}
and let
L
and
\widetilde{L}
be two closed conic subsets of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
which satisfy
\widetilde{L}\subseteq L^{c}
. Then

\mathcal{P}:H^{-r}_{\widetilde{L};\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{*})\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M),\quad\langle\mathcal{P}(u,v),\chi\rangle:=\langle\chi u,\iota_{E}(v)\rangle,\,\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM),

where the last duality is
\langle\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{L^{c}}(M;E^{\#}),\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\check{L}}(M;E^{\#\,\vee})\rangle
, is well-defined hypocontinuous sesquilinear map that restricts to the sesquilinear map

(6.15)

\Gamma(E^{*})\times\Gamma(E)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M),\quad(\varphi,\psi)\mapsto(p\mapsto\varphi_{p}(\psi_{p})).

Proof.

In view of (
6.14
),
\chi u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{L^{c}}(M;E^{\#})
and hence
\langle\chi u,\iota_{E}(v)\rangle
is well-defined since
\iota_{E}(v)\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\check{L}}(M;E^{\#\,\vee})
. To show the continuity of
\mathcal{P}(u,v):\Gamma_{c}(DM)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
it suffices to show that it maps bounded sets into bounded sets since
\Gamma_{c}(DM)
is bornological. Fix a bounded subset
B
of
\Gamma_{c}(DM)
. There is
K\subset\subset M
such that
B
is a bounded subset of
\Gamma_{K}(DM)
. Pick relatively compact charts
(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})
,
\mu=1,\ldots,l
, which cover
K
and nonnegative
\varphi_{\mu}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
such that
\sum_{\mu=1}^{l}\varphi_{\mu}=1
on a neighbourhood of
K
and choose
\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
so that
\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\varphi_{\mu}
. For
\chi\in B
, write
\chi_{|O_{\mu}}=\chi_{\mu}\lambda^{x_{\mu}}
,
\chi_{\mu}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(O_{\mu})
, and notice that

(6.16)

\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi u,\iota_{E}(v)\rangle|\leq\sum_{\mu=1}^{l}\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\varphi_{\mu}\lambda^{x_{\mu}}u,\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}\chi_{\mu}\iota_{E}(v)\rangle|.

Since
\{(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}\chi_{\mu})\iota_{E}(v)\,|\,\chi\in B\}
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\check{L}}(M;E^{\#\,\vee})
(cf. Remark
4.5
) and
(\varphi_{\mu}\lambda^{x_{\mu}})u\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{L^{c}}(M;E^{\#})
, we infer that the right hand side of (
6.16
) is finite. This shows that
\mathcal{P}(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
and hence
\mathcal{P}
is well-defined. To show that
\mathcal{P}
is hypocontinuous with respect to the first variable, fix a bounded subset
B_{2}
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)
. Let
B
be a bounded subset of
\Gamma_{c}(DM)
. We want to estimate
\sup_{v\in B_{2}}\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi u,\iota_{E}(v)\rangle|
by a continuous seminorm of
u
in
H^{-r}_{\widetilde{L};\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{*})
. With the above notation, (
6.16
) is valid for all
u\in H^{-r}_{\widetilde{L};\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{*})
and
v\in B_{2}
. Since
\{(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}\chi_{\mu})\iota_{E}(v)\,|\,\chi\in B,\,v\in B_{2}\}
is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\check{L}}(M;E^{\#\,\vee})
(cf. Remark
4.5
),
\sup_{v\in B_{2}}\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\varphi_{\mu}\lambda^{x_{\mu}}u,\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}\chi_{\mu}\iota_{E}(v)\rangle|
is a continuous seminorm of
\varphi_{\mu}\lambda^{x_{\mu}}u
in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{L^{c}}(M;E^{\#})
and (
6.14
) implies that it is bounded by a continuous seminorm of
u
in
H^{-r}_{\widetilde{L};\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{*})
. To show the hypocontinuity of
\mathcal{P}
with respect to the second variable, fix a bounded subset
B_{1}
of
H^{-r}_{\widetilde{L};\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{*})
. For a bounded subset
B
of
\Gamma_{c}(DM)
, we again write (
6.16
). We apply (
6.14
) and Lemma
4.20
to deduce that
\sup_{u\in B_{1}}\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\varphi_{\mu}\lambda^{x_{\mu}}u,\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}\chi_{\mu}\iota_{E}(v)\rangle|\leq C_{\mu}\sup_{\chi\in B}\mathfrak{p}_{\mu}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}\chi_{\mu}\iota_{E}(v))
with
\mathfrak{p}_{\mu}
a continuous seminorm on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{\check{L}}(M;E^{\#\,\vee})
. Now, Remark
4.5
yields the desired hypocontinuity. By direct inspection, one verifies that
\mathcal{P}
restricts to (
6.15
) and the proof is complete.
∎

Remark 6.10
.

If
Q\in\Psi^{0}(M;E,E^{*})
is properly supported and of order
-\infty
in the closed conic subset
L
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
, Proposition
5.1
and Corollary
5.4
imply that
Q:\mathcal{D}^{\prime-r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow H^{-r}_{\widetilde{L};\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{*})
is well-defined and continuous for some closed conic subset
\widetilde{L}
of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
satisfying
\widetilde{L}\subseteq L^{c}
. Hence, Proposition
6.9
shows that

\mathcal{D}^{\prime-r}_{L}(M;E)\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M),\quad(u,v)\mapsto\mathcal{P}(Qu,v),

is well-defined hypocontinuous sesquilinear map that restricts to
\Gamma(E)\times\Gamma(E)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
,
(\varphi,\psi)\mapsto(p\mapsto(Q\varphi)_{p}(\psi_{p}))
.

Remark 6.11
.

Proposition
6.9
can be viewed as an extension of the multiplication to distributions. To be precise, the proposition gives a sesquilinear product. The bilinear version is the following: for any
r\in\mathbb{R}
and
L
and
\widetilde{L}
two closed conic subsets of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
which satisfy
\widetilde{L}\subseteq\check{L}^{c}
, the bilinear mapping

\mathcal{P}_{0}:H^{-r}_{\widetilde{L};\operatorname{loc}}(M;E^{\prime})\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M),\quad\langle\mathcal{P}_{0}(u,v),\chi\rangle:=\langle\chi u,v\rangle,\,\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM),

where the last duality is
\langle\mathcal{E}^{\prime-r}_{\check{L}^{c}}(M;E^{\vee}),\mathcal{D}^{\prime r}_{L}(M;E)\rangle
, is well-defined and hypocontinuous and it restricts to
\Gamma(E^{\prime})\times\Gamma(E)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
,
(\varphi,\psi)\mapsto(p\mapsto\varphi_{p}(\psi_{p}))
. This can be shown in an analogous way as in the proof of Proposition
6.9
. Notice that this result is neither weaker nor stronger than the Hörmander theorem for product of distributions
[
28
, Theorem 8.2.10, p. 267]
,
[
5
, Theorem 6.1]
, since one of the spaces is larger and the other smaller when compared with the Hörmander theorem. It is passible that a similar result can be obtained from our theorem on the pull-back (Theorem
4.23
) by applying similar technique as in
[
28
, Theorem 8.2.10, p. 267]
and with that to obtain information on the Sobolev wave front set of the product (cf.
[
30
, Theorem 8.3.3, p. 190]
). We leave this to the reader, as we are not going to need such result.

We are ready to show our generalisation of the compensated compactness theorem. Our result on the microlocal defect measures together with the theory we developed before, allows us to adjust the main idea from the proof of
[
19
, Theorem 2]
to our needs.

Theorem 6.12
.

Let
E
and
F
be two vector bundles over
M
of rank
k
and
k^{\prime}
,
L
a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
satisfying
L\neq T^{*}M\backslash 0
and
a\in\Gamma_{\hom,r}(\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}L(E,F)_{L^{c}})
for some
r\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
\{u_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)
such that
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
converges in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;E)
to some
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;E)
. Assume that for every
(p,\xi)\in L^{c}
there is an open conic set
W\subseteq L^{c}
containing it and a properly supported
A\in\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
such that
\{Au_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime-r}_{L}(M;F)
and
\widetilde{\sigma}^{r}(A)_{|W}=a_{|W}
. Let
b\in\Gamma(\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}L(E,E^{*}))
satisfies
\operatorname{supp}b\subseteq[L^{c}]
and let
B\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,E^{*})
be properly supported, of order
-\infty
in
L
and
\sigma^{0}(B)=b
.

(i)

Assume that
b=b^{*}
and the following implication holds true:

\forall(p,\xi)\in L^{c},\,\forall e\in E_{p},\quad a(p,\xi)(e)=0\,\,\Longrightarrow\,\,b(p,[\xi])(e)(e)\geq 0.

Then, for
\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
which is nonnegative at every point, it holds that

\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Re}\langle\mathcal{P}(Bu_{n},u_{n}),\chi\rangle\geq\operatorname{Re}\langle\mathcal{P}(Bu,u),\chi\rangle,\,\,\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Im}\langle\mathcal{P}(Bu_{n},u_{n}),\chi\rangle=\operatorname{Im}\langle\mathcal{P}(Bu,u),\chi\rangle.

(ii)

Assume the following implication holds true:

\forall(p,\xi)\in L^{c},\,\forall e\in E_{p},\quad a(p,\xi)(e)=0\,\,\Longrightarrow\,\,b(p,[\xi])(e)(e)=0.

Then
\mathcal{P}(Bu_{n},u_{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(Bu,u)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
.

Proof.

We start by pointing out that for all properly supported
Q\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,E^{*})
which are of order
-\infty
in
L
and all
\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
, we have
\chi Q\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
.

We claim that
(ii)
follows from
(i)
. To see this, set
b_{1}:=(b+b^{*})/2
and
b_{2}:=(b-b^{*})/(2i)
and pick properly supported
B_{1},B_{2}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,E^{*})
which are of order
-\infty
in
L
and
\sigma^{0}(B_{j})=b_{j}
,
j=1,2
. In view of the assumption in
(ii)
, we can apply
(i)
with
\pm b_{1}
and
\pm b_{2}
. Applying it with
b_{1}
and
-b_{1}
we infer that for every
\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
which is nonnegative at every point it holds that

(6.17)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\langle\mathcal{P}(B_{1}u_{n},u_{n}),\chi\rangle=\langle\mathcal{P}(B_{1}u,u),\chi\rangle.

When
\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
is real-valued, pick
\chi^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
which is nonnegative at every point and such that
\chi^{\prime}-\chi
is nonnegative at every point and apply (
6.17
) with
\chi^{\prime}
and
\chi^{\prime}-\chi
to deduce that (
6.17
) is valid also for
\chi
. Employing this to the real and imaginary part of general
\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
, we deduce
\mathcal{P}(B_{1}u_{n},u_{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(B_{1}u,u)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
since every weakly convergent sequence is strongly convergent in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
. Arguing analogously for
b_{2}
, we deduce the claim in
(ii)
for
B_{1}+iB_{2}
. Since for each
\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
,
\chi(B_{1}+iB_{2}-B)\in\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
, we can invoke Corollary
5.5
to deduce the claim for
B
.

We now show
(i)
. Since

\operatorname{Im}\langle\mathcal{P}(Bu_{n},u_{n}),\chi\rangle=\operatorname{Im}(\iota_{0,E}(\chi Bu_{n}),u_{n})=(2i)^{-1}\left(\iota_{0,E}((\chi B-(\chi B)^{*})u_{n}),u_{n}\right)

and
\chi B-(\chi B)^{*}\in\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
, Corollary
5.5
verifies the second identity in
(i)
. To show the first inequality, pick a subsequence
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
such that

\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Re}\langle\mathcal{P}(Bu_{n},u_{n}),\chi\rangle=\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Re}\langle\mathcal{P}(Bu_{n_{j}},u_{n_{j}}),\chi\rangle.

We apply Theorem
6.3
to
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
to find
\vartheta\in(\Gamma^{0}_{c}(\pi_{S^{*}M}^{*}L(E,E^{\#})_{[L^{c}]}))^{\prime}
and Lemma
6.8
to
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and
\chi b
to extract a subsequence of it, still denoted by
(u_{n_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
, which satisfies all of the properties stated in the lemma
14
14

14

We point out that
\vartheta
depends on the subsequence chosen for the
\liminf
and hence it depends on
B
and
\chi
, however, this will not matter since we will only work with the last extracted subsequence.
. Since
\sigma^{0}((\chi B)^{*})=(\chi b)^{\#}=\chi b=\sigma^{0}(\chi B)
and (
6.4
) is satisfied with
\chi B
, we infer

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta,\chi b\rangle=\operatorname{Re}\langle\vartheta,\chi b\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(\chi Bu_{n_{j}}),u_{n_{j}})-\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(\chi Bu),u_{n_{j}})

\displaystyle{}\quad-\operatorname{Re}\overline{\left(\iota_{0,E}((\chi B)^{*}u),u_{n_{j}}\right)}+\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(\chi Bu),u)\Big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(\chi Bu_{n_{j}}),u_{n_{j}})-\operatorname{Re}(\iota_{0,E}(\chi Bu),u).

Thus, it suffices to show that
\langle\vartheta,\chi b\rangle\geq 0
; we show that this holds for all
\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
which are nonnegative at every point. By employing a partition of unity, we see that it is enough to prove this only for such
\chi
which also satisfy
\operatorname{supp}\chi\subseteq O
where
(O,x)
is a relatively compact chart over which
E
and
F
locally trivialise via
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
. Let
L_{O}
be as in Remark
6.4
. If
(L_{O})^{c}=\emptyset
, then the claim is trivial since
\chi b=0
. Assume that
(L_{O})^{c}\neq\emptyset
. Write
\chi:=\widetilde{\chi}\circ x\,\lambda^{x}
,
a_{|L^{c}\cap T^{*}O}=\kappa^{*}(\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j})\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}(e^{\prime j}\otimes s_{l})
and
b_{|\pi^{-1}_{S^{*}M}(O)}=\hat{\kappa}^{*}(\widetilde{b}_{l,j})\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(e^{\prime j}\otimes e^{*\,l})
with
\widetilde{\chi}\in\mathcal{D}(x(O))
,
\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}((L_{O})^{c})
and
\widetilde{b}_{l,j}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(x(O)\times\mathbb{S}^{m-1})
and
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{k})
and
(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k^{\prime}})
the local frames for
E
and
F
over
O
induced by
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
and
(e^{\prime 1},\ldots,e^{\prime k})
and
(e^{*\,1},\ldots,e^{*\,k})
the corresponding dual and anti-dual frames for
E^{\prime}
and
E^{*}
over
O
; of course,
\kappa
and
\hat{\kappa}
are defined as in (
2.7
) and (
6.6
). Set
\widetilde{a}:=(\widetilde{a}^{l}_{j})_{l,j}
and
\widetilde{b}:=(\widetilde{b}_{l,j})_{l,j}
and let
\widetilde{\vartheta}:=(\widetilde{\vartheta}^{l,j})_{l,j}
be as in Remark
6.4
. Pick nonnegative
\widetilde{\psi}\in\mathcal{D}([(L_{O})^{c}])
such that
\widetilde{\psi}=1
on a neighbourhood of
(\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{\chi}\times\mathbb{S}^{m-1})\cap\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{b}
and set
\psi:=\hat{\kappa}^{*}(\widetilde{\psi})\in\mathcal{D}([L^{c}]\cap\pi^{-1}_{S^{*}M}(O))
. By employing the assumption in
(i)
, in the same way as in the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 2.3]
, one can show that for every
\varepsilon>0
there is
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\widetilde{\varphi}_{\varepsilon}:[(L_{O})^{c}]\rightarrow\mathbf{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}),\quad\widetilde{\varphi}_{\varepsilon}(t,\omega):=\widetilde{b}(t,\omega)+C_{\varepsilon}\widetilde{a}(t,\omega)^{*}\widetilde{a}(t,\omega)+\varepsilon I,

with
I
the identity
k\times k
matrix, is smooth and positive semi-definite at every point of
\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{\psi}
. The properties of
\widetilde{\vartheta}
imply

0\leq\langle\widetilde{\vartheta},\widetilde{\chi}\widetilde{\psi}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\varepsilon}\rangle=\langle\vartheta,\chi b\rangle+C_{\varepsilon}\langle\widetilde{\vartheta},\widetilde{a}^{*}\widetilde{a}\widetilde{\psi}\widetilde{\chi}\rangle+\varepsilon\langle\widetilde{\vartheta},\widetilde{\chi}\widetilde{\psi}I\rangle.

We claim that
\langle\widetilde{\vartheta},\widetilde{a}^{*}\widetilde{a}\widetilde{\psi}\widetilde{\chi}\rangle=0
. Once we show this, the inequality in
(i)
follows by letting
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}
in the above inequality. We employ the assumption in the theorem to find open conic sets
W_{1},\ldots,W_{d}\subseteq L^{c}\cap T^{*}O
satisfying
\operatorname{supp}\psi\subseteq\bigcup_{\mu=1}^{d}[W_{\mu}]
and properly supported
A_{1},\ldots,A_{d}\in\Psi^{r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
such that
a_{\mu}:=\widetilde{\sigma}^{r}(A_{\mu})
coincides with
a
on
W_{\mu}
,
\mu=1,\ldots,d
. Pick nonnegative
\psi_{\mu}\in\mathcal{D}([W_{\mu}])
such that
\sum_{\mu=1}^{d}\psi_{\mu}^{2}=1
on
\operatorname{supp}\psi
. For each
\mu\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
, define
\phi_{\mu}(t,\xi):=|\xi|^{-r}\psi_{\mu}\circ\hat{\kappa}^{-1}(t,\xi/|\xi|)
,
(t,\xi)\in x(O)\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
. Notice that
\sum_{j=1}^{k^{\prime}}\kappa^{*}(\phi_{\mu})\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}\otimes s_{j})\in\Gamma_{\hom,-r}(\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}L(F,F)_{T^{*}M\backslash 0})
and pick properly supported
B_{\mu}\in\Psi^{-r}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;F,F)
such that
\widetilde{\sigma}^{-r}(B_{\mu})
is this section; of course,
(s^{\prime 1},\ldots,s^{\prime k^{\prime}})
is the dual frame for
F^{\prime}
induced by
\Phi^{\prime}
. Finally, choose
B_{0}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;F,F^{\#})
such that
\sigma^{0}(B_{0})=\sum_{j=1}^{k^{\prime}}\psi\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(s^{\prime j}\otimes\sigma^{j})
with
(\sigma^{1},\ldots,\sigma^{k^{\prime}})
the frame for
F^{\#}
induced by
\Phi^{\prime}
. Then
B_{\mu}A_{\mu}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;E,F)
and
(B_{\mu}A_{\mu})^{*}B_{0}(B_{\mu}A_{\mu})\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;E,E^{\#})
. Notice that

\sigma^{0}(B_{\mu}A_{\mu})=\psi_{\mu}a_{\mu,l}^{j}\circ\kappa^{-1}\circ\hat{\kappa}\,\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(e^{\prime l}\otimes s_{j}),\quad\mbox{where}\quad a_{\mu\,|T^{*}O\backslash 0}=a_{\mu,l}^{j}\pi^{*}_{T^{*}M}(e^{\prime l}\otimes s_{j}).

Hence, denoting by
(\epsilon^{1},\ldots,\epsilon^{k})
the frame for
E^{\#}
induced by
\Phi
, we infer

\sigma^{0}((B_{\mu}A_{\mu})^{*}B_{0}(B_{\mu}A_{\mu}))=\psi\psi_{\mu}^{2}\sum_{h=1}^{k^{\prime}}(\overline{a^{h}_{\mu,j}}a^{h}_{\mu,l})\circ\kappa^{-1}\circ\hat{\kappa}\,\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(e^{\prime l}\otimes\epsilon^{j}).

The assumptions in the theorem together with Corollary
5.5
imply that
\{(B_{\mu}A_{\mu})^{*}B_{0}(B_{\mu}A_{\mu})u_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime 0}_{L^{c}}(M;E^{\#})
and consequently

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\mu=1}^{d}\left(\iota_{0,E}\left((\widetilde{\chi}\circ x)(B_{\mu}A_{\mu})^{*}B_{0}(B_{\mu}A_{\mu})(u_{n_{j}}-u)\right),u_{n_{j}}-u\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu=1}^{d}\sum_{h=1}^{k^{\prime}}\left\langle\vartheta,(\widetilde{\chi}\circ x)\psi\psi_{\mu}^{2}(\overline{\widetilde{a}^{h}_{j}}\widetilde{a}^{h}_{l})\circ\hat{\kappa}\,\pi^{*}_{S^{*}M}(e^{\prime l}\otimes\epsilon^{j})\right\rangle=\sum_{h=1}^{k^{\prime}}\left\langle\widetilde{\vartheta}^{j,l},\widetilde{\chi}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{a}^{h}_{j}}\widetilde{a}^{h}_{l}\right\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\vartheta},\widetilde{\chi}\widetilde{\psi}\widetilde{a}^{*}\widetilde{a}\rangle.

This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

We end the article with a typical application of the compensated compactness theorem to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
-sequential continuity of quadratic forms on the set of solutions of second order PDEs; see
[
18
,
19
,
34
,
54
]
.

Example 6.13
.

As before,
M
is an
m
-dimensional manifold. Let
\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{n}\in\Gamma(TM)
(they are real-valued by definition!). Let
A_{j,l}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M)
,
j,l=1,\ldots,n
, and
A_{1}\in\Psi^{1}(M)
be properly supported. Let
v,v^{(k)},f^{(k)}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, satisfy

\sum_{j,l=1}^{n}\mathcal{V}_{j}A_{j,l}\mathcal{V}_{l}v^{(k)}+A_{1}v^{(k)}=f^{(k)},\,\,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},\quad\mbox{and}\quad v^{(k)}\rightarrow v\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M).

Denote
L_{0}:=\{(p,\xi)\in T^{*}M\backslash 0\,|\,\xi(\mathcal{V}_{j}(p))=0,\,j=1,\ldots,n\}
; clearly
L_{0}
is a closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Let
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
be closed conic subset of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
which satisfy
WF^{-1}_{c}(\{f^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})\subseteq L_{1}
,
v\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 1}_{L_{2}}(M)
and
\{v^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 1}_{L_{2}}(M)
. Setting
L:=L_{0}\cup L_{1}\cup L_{2}
, we claim that for every properly supported
B_{0}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M)
which is of order
-\infty
in
L
, it holds that

(6.18)

\displaystyle\sum_{j,l=1}^{n}\mathcal{P}(A_{j,l}B_{0}(\mathcal{V}_{l}v^{(k)}),\mathcal{V}_{j}v^{(k)})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\sum_{j,l=1}^{n}\mathcal{P}(A_{j,l}B_{0}(\mathcal{V}_{l}v),\mathcal{V}_{j}v),\,\,\mbox{as}\,\,k\rightarrow\infty\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M);

(6.19)

\displaystyle\sum_{j,l=1}^{n}\mathcal{P}(B_{0}A_{j,l}(\mathcal{V}_{l}v^{(k)}),\mathcal{V}_{j}v^{(k)})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\sum_{j,l=1}^{n}\mathcal{P}(B_{0}A_{j,l}(\mathcal{V}_{l}v),\mathcal{V}_{j}v),\,\,\mbox{as}\,\,k\rightarrow\infty\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M).

Before we show the claims, we point out several things. First, if
L=T^{*}M\backslash 0
, the claims are trivial since in this case
B_{0}
is a properly supported
\Psi
DO in
\Psi^{-\infty}(M)
; of course this always holds since we can always take
L_{1}=L_{2}=T^{*}M\backslash 0
, but the result is meaningless! When this is not the case,
L\neq T^{*}M\backslash 0
imposes real restrictions on
\{f_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and
\{v_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and the claims state that the convergence will hold once we employ a
\Psi
DO that removes the bad part of
T^{*}M
: the annihilators of the spaces spanned by the vector fields at every point
15
15

15

If
M
has a (pseudo-)Riemannian metric, these can be identified with the orthogonal complements of the spaces spanned by the vector fields.
(the set
L_{0}
), the place where
\{f_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
are too singular or do not behave as a relatively compact subset of the Sobolev-
-1
distributions (the set
L_{1}
, cf. Corollary
4.7
) and the place where
v
and
\{v_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
are too singular or do not behave like a bounded subset of the Sobolev-
1
distributions (the set
L_{2}
). Finally,
A_{1}v^{(k)}
is irrelevant since it can be absorbed in the right hand side in view of Remark
5.2
.

Notice that (
6.19
) follows from (
6.18
) in view of Corollary
5.5
and the definition of
\mathcal{P}
since
\chi(B_{0}A_{j,l}-A_{j,l}B_{0})\in\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg},c,L^{c}}(M;\mathbb{C}_{M},DM)
, for all
\chi\in\Gamma_{c}(DM)
. To prove (
6.18
), as we pointed out, we may assume
L\neq T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Set
a_{j,l}:=\sigma^{0}(A_{j,l})\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(S^{*}M)
. Let
B_{0}
be as in the claim and denote
b_{0}:=\sigma^{0}(B_{0})\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(S^{*}M)
. Notice that
\operatorname{supp}b_{0}\subseteq[L^{c}]
. Set
u^{(k)}:=(\mathcal{V}_{1}v^{(k)},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{n}v^{(k)})\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;\mathbb{C}_{M}^{n})
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and
u:=(\mathcal{V}_{1}v,\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{n}v)\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 0}_{L}(M;\mathbb{C}_{M}^{n})
. Consider the properly supported
\Psi
DO
\widetilde{A}\in\Psi^{1}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;\mathbb{C}_{M}^{n},\mathbb{C}_{M}^{(1+n(n-1)/2)})
defined as follows: the terms in the last row in the
(1+n(n-1)/2)\times n
matrix of
\widetilde{A}
are
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathcal{V}_{j}A_{j,1},\ldots,\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathcal{V}_{j}A_{j,n}
and all of the previous rows are
(0,\ldots,0,\mathcal{V}_{j},0,\ldots,0,-\mathcal{V}_{l},0,\ldots,0)
where
\mathcal{V}_{j}
is on
l
-th place and
-\mathcal{V}_{l}
is on
j
-th place,
1\leq l<j\leq n
. We are going to apply Theorem
6.12
with this
\widetilde{A}
,
\widetilde{a}:=\widetilde{\sigma}^{1}(\widetilde{A})
and
b:=(b_{0}a_{j,l})_{j,l}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(S^{*}M;\mathbf{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C}))
; clearly
\operatorname{supp}b\subseteq[L^{c}]
. We check that the conditions are satisfied. Notice that
\widetilde{A}u^{(k)}
is given as follows: the last entry is
f^{(k)}-A_{1}v^{(k)}
, while the previous are
[\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{l}]v^{(k)}
,
1\leq l<j\leq n
. The assumptions together with Corollary
4.7
and Remark
5.2
imply that
\{f^{(k)}-A_{1}v^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime-1}_{L}(M)
. Since
[\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{l}]\in\Psi^{1}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M)
, Remark
5.2
also implies that
\{[\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{l}]v^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime-1}_{L}(M)
. Consequently,
\{\widetilde{A}u^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime-1}_{L}(M;\mathbb{C}_{M}^{1+n(n-1)/2})
. Let
(p,\xi)\in L^{c}
and
z=(z^{1},\ldots,z^{n})\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
be such that
\widetilde{a}(p,\xi)z=0
. Pick a chart
(O,x)
about
p
and write
\mathcal{V}_{j}=\mathcal{V}^{l}_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{l}}
and
\xi=\xi_{l}dx^{l}|_{p}
. Set
c_{j}(p,\xi):=\mathcal{V}^{l}_{j}(p)\xi_{l}
and notice that
\widetilde{a}(p,\xi)z=0
implies that
c_{j}(p,\xi)z^{l}=c_{l}(p,\xi)z^{j}
,
j\neq l
, and
\sum_{j,l=1}^{n}a_{j,l}(p,[\xi])c_{j}(p,\xi)z^{l}=0
. At least one
c_{j}(p,\xi)\neq 0
since
(p,\xi)\not\in L_{0}
. Consequently, the first equations imply
z^{j}=\zeta c_{j}(p,\xi)
,
j=1,\ldots,n
, for some
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}
. If
\zeta\neq 0
, plugging this in the last equation, we infer
\sum_{j,l=1}^{n}a_{j,l}(p,[\xi])z^{l}\overline{z^{j}}=0
(recall,
c_{j}(p,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}
,
j=1,\ldots,n
), i.e.
b(p,[\xi])z\cdot\overline{z}=0
. When
\zeta=0
, we have
z=0
and hence
b(p,[\xi])z\cdot\overline{z}=0
. Thus, we can apply Theorem
6.12
to deduce (
6.18
).

In a similar way, one can apply Theorem
6.12
to generalise other results where the standard compensated compactness theorem plays a key role. We leave such investigations for future research and conclude the article with the following consequence of Example
6.13
.

Example 6.14
.

Let
(M,g)
be a pseudo-Riemannian manifold. Let
A\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;TM\otimes\mathbb{C},TM\otimes\mathbb{C})
and
A_{1}\in\Psi^{1}(M)
be properly supported and let
v,v^{(k)},f^{(k)}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, satisfy

(6.20)

\operatorname{div}_{g}(A(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}))+A_{1}v^{(k)}=f^{(k)},\,\,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},\quad\mbox{and}\quad v^{(k)}\rightarrow v\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M).

Let
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
be closed conic subsets of
T^{*}M\backslash 0
which satisfy
WF^{-1}_{c}(\{f^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}})\subseteq L_{1}
,
v\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime 1}_{L_{2}}(M)
and
\{v^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a bounded subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime 1}_{L_{2}}(M)
. Setting
L:=L_{1}\cup L_{2}
, we claim that for every properly supported
B\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M;TM\otimes\mathbb{C},TM\otimes\mathbb{C})
which is of order
-\infty
in
L
and such that
\sigma^{0}(B)=b\operatorname{I}_{TM\otimes\mathbb{C}}
with
b\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(S^{*}M)
, it holds that

(6.21)

\mathcal{P}(BA(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}),dv^{(k)})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(BA(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v),dv),\quad\mbox{as}\,\,k\rightarrow\infty\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M).

Notice that all terms are well-defined elements of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M)
in view of Proposition
6.9
(cf. Remark
6.10
) since the anti-dual bundle of
T^{*}M\otimes\mathbb{C}
is canonically identified with
TM\otimes\mathbb{C}
. As before, we may assume that
L\neq T^{*}M\backslash 0
since the claim is trivial if
L=T^{*}M\backslash 0
. Let
B
and
b
be as in the claim; clearly
\operatorname{supp}b\subseteq[L^{c}]
. Pick properly supported
B^{\prime}\in\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg}}(M)
such that
\sigma^{0}(B^{\prime})=b
and is of order
-\infty
in
L
. Let
\{(O_{\mu},x_{\mu})\}_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be a locally finite cover of
M
of relatively compact charts. Let
(\varphi_{\mu})_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be a partition of unity subordinated to this cover and choose
\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu},\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
such that
\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\mu}
and
\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}=1
on a neighbourhood of
\operatorname{supp}\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}
. Notice that the operator
B^{\prime}_{\mu}:\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
,
B^{\prime}_{\mu}\psi:=\varphi_{\mu}B^{\prime}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}\psi)
, belongs to
\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c}(O_{\mu})
and is of order
-\infty
in
T^{*}O_{\mu}\cap L
. Furthermore, the operator
A^{j}_{\mu,l}:\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(O_{\mu})
,
A^{j}_{\mu,l}(\psi):=dx^{j}_{\mu}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A(\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}\psi\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{l}_{\mu}}))
, belongs to
\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c}(O_{\mu})
. Write
g_{|O_{\mu}}=g_{\mu,j,l}dx_{\mu}^{j}\otimes dx_{\mu}^{l}
,
|g_{\mu}|:=|\det(g_{\mu,j,l})_{j,l}|
and denote by
(g^{j,l}_{\mu})_{j,l}
the inverse of
(g_{\mu,j,l})_{j,l}
. Notice that

\operatorname{div}_{g}\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A(\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)})\right)=\operatorname{div}_{g}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}))-\operatorname{div}_{g}\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A((1-\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu})\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)})\right).

The set
\{\operatorname{div}_{g}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A((1-\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu})\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}))\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime-1}_{L}(M)
since
\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A(1-\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu})
has smooth compactly supported kernel. For
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M;TM\otimes\mathbb{C})
, we employ the notation
u^{j}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O_{\mu})
,
\langle u^{j}_{\mu},\varphi\rangle:=\langle u,\varphi dx^{j}_{\mu}\rangle
,
\varphi\in\Gamma_{c}(DO_{\mu})
. Notice that

\operatorname{div}_{g}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}))=\frac{\partial\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}}{\partial x_{\mu}^{j}}\left(A(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)})\right)^{j}_{\mu}+\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}\operatorname{div}_{g}(A(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)})),\,\,\mbox{on}\,\,O_{\mu}.

Hence, in view of the assumptions and Remark
5.2
, we conclude that
\{\operatorname{div}_{g}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A(\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}))\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime-1}_{T^{*}O_{\mu}\cap L}(O_{\mu})
. Since

\operatorname{div}_{g}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A(\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}))=|g_{\mu}|^{-1/2}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{j}}\left(|g_{\mu}|^{1/2}A^{j}_{\mu,l}\left(g_{\mu}^{l,h}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{h}}v^{(k)}\right)\right),\quad\mbox{on}\quad O_{\mu},

we infer that
\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{j}}\left(A^{j}_{\mu,l}(g_{\mu}^{l,h}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{h}}v^{(k)})\right)
is a relatively compact subset of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime-1}_{T^{*}O_{\mu}\cap L}(O_{\mu})
. Hence, we can apply the claim (
6.19
) from Example
6.13
with
B^{\prime}_{\mu}
to deduce

\mathcal{P}\left(B^{\prime}_{\mu}A^{j}_{\mu,l}\left(g^{l,h}_{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{h}}v^{(k)}\right),\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{j}}v^{(k)}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\left(B^{\prime}_{\mu}A^{j}_{\mu,l}\left(g^{l,h}_{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{h}}v\right),\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{j}}v\right),\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O_{\mu}).

Consider the
\Psi
DO
\widetilde{B}_{\mu}:\Gamma_{c}(TO_{\mu}\otimes\mathbb{C})\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(TO_{\mu}\otimes\mathbb{C})
,
\widetilde{B}_{\mu}(\chi^{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{j}})=B^{\prime}_{\mu}(\chi^{j})\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{\mu}^{j}}
. Of course, it is in
\Psi^{0}_{\operatorname{phg},c}(O_{\mu};TO_{\mu}\otimes\mathbb{C},TO_{\mu}\otimes\mathbb{C})
and is of order
-\infty
in
T^{*}O_{\mu}\cap L
. In view of the definition of
\mathcal{P}
, we infer

(6.22)

\mathcal{P}\left(\widetilde{B}_{\mu}\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A(\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)})\right),\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}dv^{(k)}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\left(\widetilde{B}_{\mu}\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}A(\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}\operatorname{grad}_{g}v)\right),\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}dv\right),\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O_{\mu}).

Since
\sigma^{0}(\varphi_{\mu}B)=\sigma^{0}(\widetilde{B}_{\mu})
in
T^{*}O_{\mu}
, we have
\varphi_{\mu}B-\widetilde{B}_{\mu}\in\Psi^{-1}_{\operatorname{phg},c}(O_{\mu};TO_{\mu}\otimes\mathbb{C},TO_{\mu}\otimes\mathbb{C})
and is of order
-\infty
in
T^{*}O_{\mu}\cap L
. Hence, Corollary
5.5
implies that (
6.22
) holds true but with
\varphi_{\mu}B
in place of
\widetilde{B}_{\mu}
. The operator
\varphi_{\mu}B(1-\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu})
has smooth compactly supported kernel, whence

\mathcal{P}(\varphi_{\mu}BA(\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}),\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}dv^{(k)})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(\varphi_{\mu}BA(\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}\operatorname{grad}_{g}v),\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}dv),\quad\mbox{in}\quad\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M).

Similarly, as
\varphi_{\mu}BA(1-\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\mu})
has a smooth compactly supported kernel, we infer

\mathcal{P}(\varphi_{\mu}BA(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}),\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}dv^{(k)})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(\varphi_{\mu}BA(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v),\varphi^{\prime}_{\mu}dv),\quad\mbox{in}\quad\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M).

In view of the definition of
\mathcal{P}
, this immediately implies (
6.21
).

In the special case when
A=\operatorname{Id}
, (
6.20
) becomes

(6.23)

\Delta_{g}v^{(k)}+A_{1}v^{(k)}=f^{(k)},\,\,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},\quad\mbox{and}\quad v^{(k)}\rightarrow v\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M),

where
\Delta_{g}=\operatorname{div}_{g}\operatorname{grad}_{g}
is the geometric Laplacian. In this case the claim is

(6.24)

\mathcal{P}(B(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v^{(k)}),dv^{(k)})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(B(\operatorname{grad}_{g}v),dv),\quad\mbox{as}\,\,k\rightarrow\infty\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(M).

For real valued
\psi
, notice that
\mathcal{P}(\operatorname{grad}_{g}\psi,d\psi)=g(\operatorname{grad}_{g}\psi,\operatorname{grad}_{g}\psi)
. Hence, in the special case of a
1+3
Lorentzian manifold, the quadratic form in (
6.24
) is a pseudo-differential modification of the Lagrangian of
v
without an external potential.

Appendix A
The optimality of the conditions in Theorem
3.21

Proposition A.1
.

Let
n,m,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
be such that
k\leq m
and
k<n
and define the following linear map of rank
k
:

f:\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k},0,\ldots,0).

(i)

The map
f^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
does not extend to a continuous mapping
f^{*}:H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
for any
r_{2}<(n-k)/2
.

(ii)

The map
f^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
does not extend to a continuous mapping
f^{*}:H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
if
r_{2}-r_{1}<(n-k)/2
for any closed conic subset
\widetilde{L}
of
\mathbb{R}^{m}\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\})
which satisfies

(A.1)

\left(\{0_{m}\}\times((\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\{0_{m-k}\})\backslash\{0_{m}\})\right)\backslash\widetilde{L}\neq\emptyset.

In particular,
f^{*}
does not extend to a continuous mapping
f^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{f^{*}L}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
if
r_{2}-r_{1}<(n-k)/2
for any closed conic subset
L
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\})
which satisfies
L\cap\mathcal{N}_{f}=\emptyset
and
\left(\{0_{n}\}\times(\{\eta^{\prime}_{0}\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k})\right)\cap L=\emptyset
for some
\eta^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\backslash\{0\}
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, for
q\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we denote by
\delta_{q}
the
\delta
-distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{q}
. We first make the following preliminary observation. For
s>0
, we claim that the tempered distribution
\langle D\rangle^{-s}\delta_{q}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{-s})\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{q})
is given by

(A.2)

\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{-s})(y)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(s/2)2^{q}\pi^{q/2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{(s-q-2)/2}e^{-t}e^{-|y|^{2}/(4t)}dt,\quad y\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\backslash\{0\}.

Denote by
u^{(q)}_{s}(y)
the right-hand side of (
A.2
). We first show that
u^{(q)}_{s}
is smooth outside of the origin. For
|y|\geq\varepsilon>0
, we have

(A.3)

\frac{|y|^{2}}{4t}+t\geq\frac{|y|^{2}}{8t}+\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|y|^{2}}{8t}+\frac{t}{2}\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8t}+\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|y|}{2}.

Since the derivatives with respect to
y
of the integrand in (
A.2
) are finite sums of terms of the form
Cy^{l}t^{\lambda}e^{-t}e^{-|y|^{2}/(4t)}
for some
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
C,\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, (
A.3
) implies that
u^{(q)}_{s}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{q}\backslash\{0\})
; (
A.3
) also yields that
0<u^{(q)}_{s}(y)\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{-|y|/2}
,
|y|\geq\varepsilon
. Notice that
(t,y)\mapsto t^{(s-q-2)/2}e^{-t}e^{-|y|^{2}/(4t)}
belongs to
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{q})
(integrate first with respect to
y
). This yields
u^{(q)}_{s}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{q})
and, in view of the fact
\mathcal{F}(e^{-|\cdot|^{2}})=\pi^{q/2}e^{-|\cdot|^{2}/4}
, we infer

\mathcal{F}(u^{(q)}_{s})(\xi)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(s/2)}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{s/2-1}e^{-(1+|\xi|^{2})t}dt=\langle\xi\rangle^{-s},\quad\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{q},

which shows (
A.2
). Throughout the proof we continue to employ the notation
u^{(q)}_{s}
for
\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{-s})
. We claim that

(A.4)

\varphi u_{s}^{(q)}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{q})\,\,\mbox{for any}\,\,\varphi\in\mathcal{D}_{L^{\infty}}(\mathbb{R}^{q})\,\,\mbox{satisfying}\,\,\varphi=0\,\,\mbox{on a neighbourhood of}\,\,0.

To verify it, notice that
\partial^{\alpha}(\varphi u_{s}^{(q)})
is a finite sum of terms of the form

Cy^{l}\partial^{\beta}\varphi(y)\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\lambda}e^{-t}e^{-|y|^{2}/(4t)}dt,\quad\mbox{for some}\,\,\beta\leq\alpha,\,l\in\mathbb{N},\,C,\lambda\in\mathbb{R}.

Hence, (
A.3
) implies
\varphi u^{(q)}_{s}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{q})
. Finally, we point out that
u^{(q)}_{s}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{R}^{q})
when
s>q
.

Throughout the rest of the proof, for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, we denote
x=(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})
, with
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{m-k}
. Similarly, for
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we denote
y=(y^{\prime},y^{\prime\prime\prime})
, with
y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
y^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-k}
. We first address
(i)
. Pick any
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{k})\backslash\{0\}
. By construction,
\varphi\otimes u^{(n-k)}_{n-k}\in H^{r_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
since
r_{2}<(n-k)/2
. Choose nonnegative
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n-k})
such that
\psi(0)>0
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}\psi(y^{\prime\prime\prime})dy^{\prime\prime\prime}=1
and define
\psi_{j}(y^{\prime\prime\prime})=j^{n-k}\psi(jy^{\prime\prime\prime})
,
y^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-k}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Clearly
\varphi\otimes(u^{(n-k)}_{n-k}*\psi_{j})\rightarrow\varphi\otimes u^{(n-k)}_{n-k}
as
j\rightarrow\infty
in
H^{r_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and hence in
H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
as well. Notice that
f^{*}(\varphi\otimes(u^{(n-k)}_{n-k}*\psi_{j}))=(u^{(n-k)}_{n-k}*\psi_{j})(0)\varphi\otimes\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}
. Since

u^{(n-k)}_{n-k}*\psi_{j}(0)=\frac{1}{\Gamma((n-k)/2)2^{n-k}\pi^{(n-k)/2}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}}t^{-1}e^{-t}e^{-|y^{\prime\prime\prime}|^{2}/(4tj^{2})}\psi(y^{\prime\prime\prime})dtdy^{\prime\prime\prime}

and the sequence of functions in the integral is pointwise increasing with respect to
j
, we can apply monotone convergence to deduce
u^{(n-k)}_{n-k}*\psi_{j}(0)\rightarrow\infty
as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Hence
f^{*}(\varphi\otimes(u^{(n-k)}_{n-k}*\psi_{j}))
does not converge in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
and the proof of
(i)
is complete.
We turn our attention to
(ii)
. Assume that
f^{*}
extends to a continuous mapping
f^{*}:H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
for some
r_{1}
,
r_{2}
and
\widetilde{L}
as in
(ii)
. Then
(i)
implies
r_{2}\geq(n-k)/2
, which, in view of
r_{2}-r_{1}<(n-k)/2
, implies
r_{1}>0
. Pick
r^{\prime}_{2}>r_{2}
such that
r^{\prime}_{2}-r_{1}<(n-k)/2
. Clearly,
u^{(n)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+n/2}\in H^{r_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Let
(\psi_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be a
\delta
-sequence as before but defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
instead. Of course,
\psi_{j}*u^{(n)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+n/2}\rightarrow u^{(n)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+n/2}
in
H^{r_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. We claim that
f^{*}(\psi_{j}*u^{(n)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+n/2})\rightarrow c_{0}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
with

c_{0}:=\Gamma(r^{\prime}_{2}/2+k/2-n/4)\Gamma(r^{\prime}_{2}/2+n/4)^{-1}2^{k-n}\pi^{(k-n)/2}>0

and, by assumption, in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
as well. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
be arbitrary and notice that

\langle f^{*}(\psi_{j}*u^{(n)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+n/2}),\varphi\rangle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(r^{\prime}_{2}/2+n/4)2^{n}\pi^{n/2}}\\
\cdot\iiint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}}t^{(r^{\prime}_{2}-\frac{n}{2}-2)/2}e^{-t}e^{-|x^{\prime}|^{2}/(4t)}e^{-|y^{\prime\prime\prime}|^{2}/(4tj^{2})}\psi(y)\varphi(x^{\prime}+y^{\prime}/j,x^{\prime\prime})dtdxdy.

It is straightforward to check that the integrand is dominated pointwise by a function in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m+n})
for all
j
and hence we can apply dominated convergence to deduce

\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\langle f^{*}(\psi_{j}*u^{(n)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+n/2}),\varphi\rangle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(r^{\prime}_{2}/2+n/4)2^{n}\pi^{n/2}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}}t^{(r^{\prime}_{2}-\frac{n}{2}-2)/2}e^{-t}e^{-|x^{\prime}|^{2}/(4t)}\varphi(x)dtdx.

This shows that
f^{*}(\psi_{j}*u^{(n)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+n/2})\rightarrow c_{0}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}
weakly in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
and hence also in the strong topology (since
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
is Montel)
16
16

16

When
r^{\prime}_{2}>n/2
,
u^{(n)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+n/2}
is continuous and the fact
f^{*}u^{(n)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+n/2}=c_{0}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}
immediately follows from Lemma
4.26
.
. We deduce
u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{1}}_{\widetilde{L}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. We show that this leads to a contradiction. We only consider the case
k<m
, since the case
k=m
can be treated analogously. There is
\xi^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{k-1}
such that
(0_{m},\xi^{\prime}_{0},0_{m-k})\not\in\widetilde{L}
. Choose an open neighbourhood
O
of the origin in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and an open cone
V=\mathbb{R}_{+}(B(\xi^{\prime}_{0},\varepsilon)\times B(0_{m-k},\varepsilon))
with
0<\varepsilon<1/2
such that
(O\times\overline{V})\cap\widetilde{L}=\emptyset
. We can choose
O=O_{1}\times O_{2}
with
O_{1}
and
O_{2}
open balls both with radii
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and centres at the origins in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
\mathbb{R}^{m-k}
. Take
\phi_{0}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R})
such that
0\leq\phi_{0}\leq 1
,
\phi_{0}(\lambda)=\phi_{0}(-\lambda)
,
\phi_{0}=1
on
[-\varepsilon_{0}/2,\varepsilon_{0}/2]
and
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{0}\subseteq(-\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0})
. Set
\varphi_{1}(x^{\prime}):=\phi_{0}(|x^{\prime}|)
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
; clearly
\varphi_{1}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{k})
with
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{1}\subseteq O_{1}
. Pick
\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m-k})\backslash\{0\}
with
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{2}\subseteq O_{2}
. Let
V_{1}
be the open cone
\mathbb{R}_{+}B(\xi^{\prime}_{0},\varepsilon/2)
in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
and notice that
\{\xi^{\prime}\in V_{1}\,|\,|\xi^{\prime}|\geq 1\}\times B(0_{m-k},\varepsilon/2)\subseteq V
. Denoting
\varphi:=\varphi_{1}\otimes\varphi_{2}
, we infer (cf. Corollary
3.5
)

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle>\int_{V}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi(u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{m-k}}))(\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2r_{1}}d\xi

\displaystyle\geq\int_{V_{1},\,|\xi^{\prime}|\geq 1}\langle\xi^{\prime}\rangle^{2r_{1}}|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{1}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2})(\xi^{\prime})|^{2}d\xi^{\prime}\int_{B(0,\varepsilon/2)}|\mathcal{F}\varphi_{2}(\xi^{\prime\prime})|^{2}d\xi^{\prime\prime}.

Since
\varphi_{2}
has compact support,
\mathcal{F}\varphi_{2}
is entire and hence the very last integral is strictly positive. As
\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{1}}\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{1}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2})\in L^{2}(B(0_{k},1))
, we deduce
\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{1}}\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{1}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2})\in L^{2}(V_{1})
. Notice that
\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{1}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2})(\Phi\xi^{\prime})=\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{1}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2})(\xi^{\prime})
,
\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\Phi\in\operatorname{O}(k)
. Hence, the above together with the compactness of the unit sphere yields that
\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{1}}\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{1}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{k})
. In view of (
A.4
), the latter implies that
\langle\cdot\rangle^{r_{1}}\mathcal{F}u^{(k)}_{r^{\prime}_{2}+k-n/2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{k})
which is straightforward to check that it is not true.

Since
H^{r_{2}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime r_{2}}_{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
continuously, the second part of
(ii)
follows from applying the first part with
\widetilde{L}:=f^{*}L
; the conditions on
L
imply that
f^{*}L
satisfies (
A.1
) in view of (
3.53
). This completes the proof.
∎
